# TSURU — Master Ecosystem Roadmap & Tracking

**Status:** LIVING DOCUMENT — this is the single tracking doc for the whole Tsuru ecosystem.
**Created:** 2026-06-11 (by the roadmap workflow, same day as the four approved workstreams).
**Owner:** tsuru.
**Upstream sources:** the full audit corpus in `docs/audit/tsuru/` (especially `tsuru_capability_inventory.md`, `tsuru_gap_analysis.md`, `tsuru_reconciliation_report.md`, `tsuru_product_strategy.md`, `tsuru_executive_vision.md`), the workstream specs in `docs/roadmap/` (`tsuru_rebrand_plan.md`, `tsuru_fairs_spec.md`, `tsuru_fairs_architecture.md`, `rbac_express_contract.md`), and `docs/RBAC_ORG_MODULES_PORT.md`.

---

## 1. How to read and update this document

1. **Every tracked item has a stable ID `TSR-NNN`.** IDs are never reused or renumbered, even when an item is closed or split. When splitting, keep the original ID on the parent and add new IDs for children (note the lineage in the row).
2. **Status values (exactly five):**
   - **Done** — shipped/verified (for code: implemented + type-checked/tested; deployment-and-rollout steps are tracked as their own items). For specs: the document exists and is approved.
   - **In progress** — actively being worked, or partially landed.
   - **Planned** — approved direction with a defined home (a workstream, phase, or spec section), not yet started.
   - **Not started** — known from the audit, no workstream covers it yet. These are the silent-drop risks — review §6 every update.
   - **Won't do** — explicit decision not to build (or to reword/retract a marketing claim instead). Always cite the decision source.
3. **To update:** change the Status + Evidence/next-step cells in §2; if a Not-started item gets a workstream, move its reference into §5 phases; if a promise disposition changes, update §3; append a dated line to the §8 changelog. Do not delete rows — strike through or mark Won't do.
4. **Source column codes:** `C#/S#/T#/O#` = gap-analysis IDs (`tsuru_gap_analysis.md`); `Top-10 #n` = its prioritized list; `P#n` = promise rows in `tsuru_reconciliation_report.md` §1; `Strat §x` = `tsuru_product_strategy.md`; `Vision` = `tsuru_executive_vision.md` §5; `Decision 2026-06-11` = the owner decisions that created the four workstreams (rebrand, fairs, RBAC-in-Express, trueque MVP).
5. **Authoritative decisions baked into this doc (2026-06-11):**
   - **Rebrand**: JMarkets → Tsuru on all user-facing surfaces; FREE e-invoicing becomes the headline pillar; NO domain/infra renames yet. Spec only so far (`tsuru_rebrand_plan.md`).
   - **Fairs**: real public fairs app (stands, joinable by orgs), own FastAPI backend (`fairs-be`) on the shared Postgres, stand editor as an independent POS section, managed from the Tsuru admin (landing admin) connected to the fairs BE — hybrid model, public landing stays 100% static (`tsuru_fairs_spec.md`, `tsuru_fairs_architecture.md`).
   - **RBAC**: implemented in the Express server (`E:/dev/BeautyMarket/server`) — overriding the older cross-app-be recommendation in `docs/RBAC_ORG_MODULES_PORT.md` §5. **Implemented as code in this session** (see §2.B), per `docs/roadmap/rbac_express_contract.md`.
   - **Trueque**: MVP integrated into the fairs spec (offers/proposals/WhatsApp handoff, no ledger/credits in v1; `settlementType` forward-compat column).

| TSR-001 | Hacienda v4.4 e-invoicing pipeline (clave/consecutive → XML → XAdES sign → ATV submit → validation poll → PDF → email/webhook) | Done | Inventory §3 (Live & mature) | `biller-apps/auth/app/sales-api/src/services/sales_pipeline.py`; SQS FIFO mesh `cloudformation/hacienda-messaging.yml`. Maintain; debt tracked in TSR-002/003/004/009. |
| TSR-002 | Fiscal credential hardening: encrypt P12/PIN/ATV password (KMS), stop returning them in GET | **Done (code + key deployed; backfill is a manual step — §7)** (2026-09-18) | C4, O6, Top-10 #2 (Critical, legal) | **Both halves closed.** *Stop returning them:* the tenant GET returned the password, the PKCS12 and its PIN decrypted on every settings-screen load; it now returns `has_password` and the certificate's name/expiry, nothing else. Nothing consumed the PIN or the bytes — only the password was prefilled, so an omitted password on save now means KEEP rather than overwrite. *Encrypt:* `encode_string` was never encryption — it stored `base64(value + suffix)` with ONE suffix shared by every row, so reading the table read the secrets, and its decode was a substring `.replace()` that silently corrupted any secret containing the suffix. Replaced with KMS: direct for the small secrets, envelope (AES-GCM under a single-use data key) for the P12, which exceeds the 4 KB limit. Encryption context binds each ciphertext to its organization — verified that one moved between organizations is refused and a modified certificate fails its auth tag. Both formats are version-marked and every reader accepts either, so the deploy was not a flag day; `scripts/reencrypt_fiscal_credentials.py` converts existing rows, dry-run by default, verifying each one by re-opening the certificate with its PIN before it commits. Key: `cloudformation/fiscal-credentials-key.yml` + `deploys/deploy-fiscal-credentials-key.sh`, rotation on, **Retain on delete and replace** (losing it makes every credential permanently unreadable), encrypt granted only to organization-configurations and decrypt-only to the signing pipeline. Pairs with TSR-003. |

| TSR-124 | Hacienda v4.4 `Exoneracion` capture — no UI anywhere | **Done — FE + store-be, 15 tests** (2026-09-12) | QA deep-dive 2026-07-09; POS `CALCULATION_AUDIT.md` §2 | sales-be needed nothing: `ExemptionDTO` was already validated, persisted (`billing_sale_line_tax_exemptions`) and mapped to the `Exoneracion` XML element. The gap was entirely client-side, plus the order hop. Built as a per-TAX block rather than per-line, because that is how the document models it — the XML hangs `Exoneracion` inside each `Impuesto`, and `MontoExonerado = MontoImpuesto × TarifaExonerada / 100` needs a tax to compute against. **Not new OrderLine columns**: the exoneration rides inside the existing `order_line.taxes` JSONB, which already mirrors the document tax shape field for field, so there is no migration and a line can carry an exonerated IVA beside a fully-payable excise. `TaxCalculationService` now subtracts the exonerated portion from `net_tax` and reports `exonerated_total`, leaving `BaseImponible` at its full amount (Hacienda **-454** computes the base from Subtotal plus the base-building excises, and the exoneration applies to the tax, not the base). It deliberately does **not** exonerate an IVA the ISSUER is already absorbing — with a royalty the customer never paid it, so forgiving it too would double-count. `MontoExonerado` is displayed and never editable: it is an output, and sales-be rejects an exonerated amount exceeding its tax. Two surfaces: `line-detail/ExemptionSection` for a per-sale authorization, and `products/sections/ExemptionSection` for an article that is ALWAYS exonerated — the product's three flat columns, which had round-tripped through the types since migration `u1c2d3e4f5a6` but were unreachable from any form. `_imported_line_taxes` reshapes the product's columns onto the line's IVA row, never onto an excise (Nota 10.1 forgives VAT, not the specific consumption taxes) and never copying `exemption_amount`, which was computed against one unit. **Had to ship with TSR-126**: the doc makes a reference mandatory on an "FE con exoneraciones locales", so enabling exoneration capture alone would have generated rejected documents. |
| TSR-125 | Hacienda v4.4 `OtherCharges` document block (codes 01–10/99, ≤15/doc; code 04 requires third-party id) — absent in POS FE and sales BE; plus `Σ MedioPago == TotalComprobante` reconciliation not enforced in BE | Not started | QA deep-dive 2026-07-09; POS `CALCULATION_AUDIT.md` §1.5 + §4 | `fe/pos-system`: `useAllOtherCharges` unused; no checkout section. BE side needs model + DTO + service + routes (sales service). |
| TSR-126 | References: the reference-code enum was wrong on 8 of 14 codes, and the POS gated references to NC/ND | **Done — sales-be + FE, 16 tests + 51 probes** (2026-09-12) | QA deep-dive 2026-07-09; POS `CALCULATION_AUDIT.md` §2; owner request 2026-09-12 | Scope widened from "audit the NC/ND section" to the whole reference contract, because reading the analysis doc's `Informacion_Referencia` block showed both sides were wrong. **The enum**: `ReferenceCode` disagreed with the data-api catalog AND the doc on eight values — 06 is *Devolución de mercancía*, not a contingency substitution; 07 is *Sustituye comprobante electrónico*; 08 *Factura Endosada*; 09/10 the financial note codes; 11 *Proveedor No Domiciliado*; 12 *Crédito por exoneración posterior* — it invented 03 and 13, which no catalog row defines, and **omitted 17** (*Pago a comprobante*), making a REP impossible to file. Second instance of the CLAUDE.md §1 pattern after TSR-221: the doc and the catalog agree and the enum is wrong. A `ReferenceDocType` enum was added (the field was never validated at all) and a catalog-fidelity probe now walks both catalogs and fails on drift — verified by mutating a description and watching it fail. **Availability**: the POS gated references to NC/ND and, at `:260`, actively DISCARDED them for every other type. The doc makes them mandatory in five situations, not two, and five reference codes describe a Factura REPLACING an earlier comprobante (05 provisional-contingency, 07 electronic doc, 08 endorsed) — so the ATV-contingency path was unreachable. Now available on every fiscal document and required per the spec, including the conditional **FE-with-local-exoneration** case, which is derived from the lines and handed to the validator. **Restrictions** (the TSR-219 class of rule, unenforced): code 17 is REP-only, code 06 and type 09 NC/ND-only, type 16 FEC-only, and a REP may only reference `01|04|07|08|10|20`. The FE dropdowns filter on the same table so an illegal pair cannot be picked. **Timing**: `type`, `number` and `date` were validated only in `build_xml` — pipeline step 8, *after* the consecutive is allocated at step 4 — so a reference missing its document type burned a consecutive to discover it; `other_type` was never validated at all, and `Razon` (a mandatory String(180)) was silently replaced with a single space. All moved to step 1, the 10-reference ceiling enforced, and the FE captures `other_type`/`other_code` and requires the reason. ⚠️ One discrepancy left unresolved rather than guessed: on reference TYPES the doc and catalog disagree (catalog has 01-18+99 and no 20; the doc's Note 10 table omits 15/18 and its REP block requires 20). The union is accepted and both extras are named in code; settling it needs `ANEXOS_Y_ESTRUCTURAS_V4.4.pdf`. Blocks REP support only. |
| TSR-127 | POS sale-receipt printing is unreachable — `PRINT_RECEIPT.md` documents a print button in `src/pages/pos/POSPage.tsx`, a file that no longer exists; the only `window.print` lives in `ReportePage`; checkout `Receipt.tsx` has no print action | **Done (code)** (2026-09-07) — **DB applied to dev** | QA shell sweep 2026-07-09 | Closed by building the ticket **server-side**, not in the browser. A `@media print` approach was written and then rejected: it would have meant two maquetadores for the same comprobante — the server's (which already renders the order PDF and the cross-docking report) and a new CSS one — so a reprint would stop matching the original, and the slip could never be emailed or reopened later. Now `app/templates/ticket.html` → wkhtmltopdf at 72mm (no page-height: the roll is continuous, and fixing one would pad short tickets with blank paper) → S3 → `attachments.ticket_url`, via `POST /orders/{doc}/ticket`. Deliberately **regenerates** each call so a ticket printed after invoicing picks up the consecutive, key and QR it did not have before; generated **on demand** because most orders are never printed. The heading names what the slip is — `PROFORMA` carries "No es un comprobante fiscal" under it, since a slip that looks like a receipt invites being treated as one. Migration `ac9d0e1f2a3b` applied to dev; 9 tests; `docs/PRINT_RECEIPT.md` rewritten against reality. Comandas per kitchen station remain (models exist). |

The in-memory context survives alongside it for ephemeral, i18n-keyed app toasts (including the silent catalog-invalidation events), which is the one thing a server feed cannot do: those must re-render in the new language when the user toggles it. |
| TSR-129 | Reconcile the two storefront-deploy pipelines the POS knows about: `useDeployments` surfaces the markets-api pre-deployments→publish→history pipeline (never exercised, `TODO(verify-endpoint)` on every route) while the REAL org-site deploy is the sales-be provisioner (TSR-118 W2, live) with **no POS UI** (no trigger, no status visibility) | Not started | QA sales/deploy sweep 2026-07-09 | Decide which pipeline the POS DeploymentsPage should surface; verify or retire the markets-api one; consider surfacing provisioner status (`organization_settings.infrastructure_status`) in the POS. |
| TSR-130 | **POS offline-sale replay is broken (confirmed bug):** `public/sw.js` `syncPendingSales()` sends `Authorization: Bearer ${sale.token}` but `useCartFlow.handleConfirmPayment` never persists a `token` field into `db.sales` (schema has it optional) → replay POSTs `Bearer undefined` and fails forever; sale stays `synced:false`, inventory already decremented. Even with the token stored, Cognito ID tokens expire (~1h) before a typical reconnection | **Done** (2026-08-06) | QA sales-subflow sweep 2026-07-09 | Replay moved out of the service worker into an authenticated foreground sync bridge that obtains a fresh Cognito token through `salesApi`. IndexedDB v2 tracks pending/syncing/synced/failed states, retry metadata, user ownership, payload and response. Immediate submit and replay share an `Idempotency-Key`; sales-be adds an org-scoped unique key plus an advisory transaction lock so retries return the original sale. Retriable failures show a queued-success receipt; permanent failures keep the cart and preserve local inventory. |

Also in the same pass: the gender "other → back to select" control now fires on `onMouseDown` with `preventDefault` (the focused input's blur used to re-render the button out from under the pointer, so the click needed several presses) and sits at `z-10`; `select.input`/`select.pp-input` gained explicit `color-scheme` + `option` colors so the OS-drawn option list stops falling back to the system palette (reported on macOS Edge — awaiting a screenshot to confirm the closed control renders correctly). |
| TSR-149 | **POS sent branch UUIDs where the branch APIs take the integer `code`** — `POST /branches/{branch_code}/terminals` from the POS session-setup screen (and the branch update/status mutations on the stations page) put `branch_id` in the path, so store-be answered 422 `int_parsing` on the UUID | **Done (code)** (2026-08-31) — **not deployed** | Owner report 2026-08-31 | `fe/pos-system` (`chepelcr/tsuru-pos-system`): `SessionSetupScreen.tsx` and `PuestosPage.tsx` now address branches by `branch.code`; the branch status toggle moved off the general PATCH (which has no `status` field) onto the dedicated `/branches/{code}/status` route. Root cause was a naming trap — `CreateTerminalRequest.branch_id` was a `number` holding a branch *code* while `Branch.branch_id` is a UUID string; the field was inert on the wire (the branch is in the path) and has been removed along with the now-unused `branchId` form props. `tsc --noEmit` clean. |
| TSR-150 | **Business type per organization** — every org saw the same POS regardless of what it sells; there was no way to offer a restaurant its mesas or a minisuper its price checker without showing them to everyone | **Done (code)** (2026-09-07) — **DB applied to dev** | Owner request 2026-09-07 | `organizations.business_type` (9 values) + two *independent* flags `is_retail_supplier` and `is_pyme` — deliberately NOT type values, because a minisuper can be a registered PYME *and* supply a chain, and a single-value column cannot express that. `syncBusinessTypeModules` in `management-be` resolves the **union** of type + flags and writes `organization_modules`, enabling granted modules and *disabling* (never deleting) revoked ones. `rbac-seed.ts` gained 9 vertical modules + 24 submodules; `quotes` joined `DEFAULT_ORG_MODULE_NAMES`. POS: `useBusinessType` reads the module list directly and **fails closed** — `usePermissions().hasModule()` was unsuitable because it fails *open* while loading and auto-grants to owners (right for a permission, wrong for a vertical: a minisuper owner must not see Mesas). Shared `BusinessIdentityFields` in org settings **and** wizard step 1, which now reveals in beats. Migration `0019` + `db:reseed-rbac` applied to `tsuru/dev` 2026-09-07: 18 modules, `quotes` reached all 8 orgs, **0 orgs gained a vertical module** (nothing switches on until a type is chosen). Next: deploy `management-be`.  **Amended 2026-09-10 (TSR-240): the gating half of this item was reversed.** Every org now holds every vertical module; the business type keeps only the emphasis job (which surfaces the app leads with), and hiding a surface became a per-org preference rather than something we withheld. `useBusinessType` no longer fails closed because there is nothing left to close. |
| TSR-151 | **Pedido manual capture was thin** — free-text delivery point, an unused campaign field, a Payment section that does not belong on an unpaid order, and no way to write the order number or attach a B2B department | **Done (code)** (2026-09-07) | Owner request 2026-09-07 | A `PM` checkout is now **two cards**: Pedido and Receptor. The Documento card is not collapsed for a `PM`, it is **absent** — sale condition, activity code and currency+rate were extracted to `checkout/fields/` and moved onto the Pedido card, and its *Notas* was deleted rather than moved because `useCartFlow` already merged it into *Comentario*. Payment is gone entirely. The card leads with a **proforma toggle**, then a writable order number. Delivery point became a three-mode switch — registered point / receiver's address / structured CR cascade via the shared `LocationSelect` — with **no free-text-only path left**; validation requires a `store_id` or a real address. "Evento o campaña" removed. Currency and rate stayed **one** component: the auto-rate lock, pinned CRC/USD/EUR order and rate-unavailable guard are one coupled behaviour. |
| TSR-152 | **The manual-orders endpoint did not exist** — every pedido manual failed or sat in the offline outbox forever (`docs/MANUAL_ORDERS.md` §10); `OrderDetailTotals` could not even express a discount or tax total | **Done (code)** (2026-09-07) — **DB applied to dev** | `MANUAL_ORDERS.md` §10 | `POST /orders` discriminates on `source`, **by hand rather than a Pydantic Union**, so the storefront body — which predates the field and never sends it — keeps validating untouched (verified). A new path was rejected: the offline outbox persists an absolute `syncUrl`, so changing it would orphan queued pedidos. Totals recomputed server-side from the lines (a client sending `line_total: 999999` gets the real figure); user-writable `document_number` with 409 on collision; `Idempotency-Key` dedup via a partial unique index. **Missing structures added**: `OrderDetailTotals` now exposes `discounts`/`taxes` (the model always had them — the DTO simply never surfaced them, so a caller could not reconcile a total), and lines gained `product_id`, `cabys`, `net_price` and JSON tax/discount breakdowns. Also built `POST /orders/{doc}/invoice`, without which a delivered pedido could be billed twice. Migration `aa7b8c9d0e1f` applied to dev. |
| TSR-153 | **Two POS UI defects** — the payment "Más" menu revealed nothing, and every select showed the bare OS chevron flush against its border | **Done (code)** (2026-09-07) | Owner report 2026-09-07 | The "Más" bug was a **CSS-layer bug, not overflow logic**: `.dropdown-menu` was declared *outside* `@layer`, so at equal specificity it beat every utility on the element — the portaled menu got `position:absolute`, `z-index:30` and `display:flex` instead of `fixed z-modal grid`, painting it behind the drawer (`z-drawer` 200). Moved into `@layer components` (verified in the built CSS: `.dropdown-menu` at byte 7764, utilities after it at 14089) and re-pointed at `z-drawer-modal`; the offline fallback now lists all 8 Hacienda payment codes instead of leaving one item behind "Más". Selects: a native `<option>` honours almost nothing (the popup is OS-drawn), so built `SelectField` — a portaled, keyboard-navigable, ARIA combobox listbox — and re-pointed the shared `Select` at it with an `<option>`-children compat layer, converting **32 files without rewriting a single handler**. Chevron moved to 12px/40px for anything still native. Added the `prefers-reduced-motion` guard the codebase lacked. |
| TSR-154 | Restaurant vertical — mesas, combos, modificadores, cuenta dividida, servicio 10%, comandas | **Done (code)** (2026-09-07) — admin CRUD screens pending | Owner request 2026-09-07 | Mesas full-stack (model/repo/service/controller/DTOs + `useTables` + `TablesPanel`), addressed by **integer `branch_code` per TSR-149** because the POS session only holds the code. `tables.is_dynamic` doubles as the bar's open tab — a tab *is* a table, so the bar vertical needed no second mechanism. **Combos explode at add-to-cart**: a combo mixing a 13% item with an exempt one cannot be one flat line on a fiscal document, so the discount distributes proportionally (the only split preserving each component's taxable ratio) and is clamped at zero so an over-priced combo cannot raise a component's base — 11 tests. Servicio 10% is an **itemized line, never a markup**, and excludes itself from its own base (it would otherwise compound on every recompute). Split-bill shares must reconcile to the original bill; unassigned lines go to share 0 rather than being dropped. |
| TSR-155 | Product code lookup + minisuper vertical | **Done (code)** (2026-09-07) | Owner request 2026-09-07 | **Code lookup ships ungated to every organization** — scanning is how a POS is used, so it rides `commercial/read/products` rather than a vertical module; `find_by_company_and_any_code` matches the number across every code type via JSONB containment (verified against live dev data: `ART001` resolved correctly). Offline mirror gained the same rule so a till behaves identically without signal. Scale barcodes (weight/price embedded in EAN-13) parse through a parameterised pure helper — the layout is per-shop, not standardised — with 8 tests. |
| TSR-156 | Proformas / cotizaciones for every organization | **Done (code)** (2026-09-07) | Owner request 2026-09-07 | **Not a new document type**: a proforma is an order in `quote` status, so approving one is a status change and "Facturar pedido" needs no special case. Ungated — a `quotes` default module, not a vertical or a PYME privilege. Transition guard on both sides blocks **`quote → delivered`**: the one that matters, since it would let an unapproved cotización be invoiced as though the customer had agreed. Entry point is a toggle at the top of the Pedido card, not a second footer button. |
| TSR-157 | **Cross-docking sale points could only be created by uploading a spreadsheet** — a supplier with the distribution figures but no Excel file could not record them | **Done (code)** (2026-09-07) | Owner request 2026-09-07 | CRUD beside the parser (`/orders/{doc}/crossdocking/sale-points`), writing the same rows, so a partial upload can be finished by hand and an Excel-imported point can be renamed to fix a typo. **Totals are derived** from the items and the product's `units_per_box`, never sent, so a hand-captured order reconciles against an imported one and the existing report/PDF work unchanged. Over-allocating a product beyond what the order line ordered returns 422 **naming the line** — the check the spreadsheet path gets free from its template. |
| TSR-158 | Bar vertical — cuentas abiertas, precios por horario | **Done (code)** (2026-09-07) — schedule CRUD UI pending | Owner request 2026-09-07 | Tabs reuse `tables.is_dynamic`. Happy-hour resolution handles **windows that wrap midnight** (22:00–02:00 is the normal shape; a naive `start <= now <= end` never matches one) and **never raises a price above catalog** — a misconfigured "discount" is a misconfiguration, not a surcharge. The server re-resolves on submit: price is authority-critical and a stale or tampered client clock must not set it on a fiscal document. 10 tests. |
| TSR-159 | Farmacia vertical — lote/vencimiento, controlados, recetas | **Done (code)** (2026-09-07) — controlled-register UI pending | Owner request 2026-09-07 | FEFO (**First Expiry, First Out**, not FIFO — for anything with a shelf life the date that matters is when it goes bad, not when it arrived). Undated stock sorts **last** so it cannot jump ahead of something about to expire; `0` days means "expires today" — a warning, not its absence. `controlled_sales` is append-only: a register that can be edited is not a register. |
| TSR-160 | Servicios vertical — servicios por hora, facturación recurrente | **Done (code)** (2026-09-07) — EventBridge scheduler + UI pending | Owner request 2026-09-07 | Monthly cadence **clamps instead of overflowing**: the 31st + 1 month is the 28th, so a subscription cannot skip February. Overdue schedules are included, not skipped — if the scheduler missed a day the customer still expects the invoice. A run creates a **draft**; emitting a fiscal document is a decision a person makes, not a cron job. **This is the only piece needing new deployment infrastructure** (store-be is Lambda with no scheduler today). |
| TSR-161 | Ferretería vertical — unidades con conversión, pedidos especiales | **Done (code)** (2026-09-07) — unit CRUD UI pending | Owner request 2026-09-07 | Stock in rollos, sell by the metro. Reaches the tax engine only through `quantity` and `unit_price`, so the Hacienda cascade is untouched — which is why the vertical is cheap. A zero/negative conversion factor is rejected rather than silently zeroing inventory movement; an explicit `price_override` wins, because deriving a cut metre's price from the roll loses money on every cut. |
| TSR-162 | Agenda vertical — salón **and** taller | **Done (code)** (2026-09-07) — calendar UI pending | Owner request 2026-09-07 | One module with two consumers, not two calendars: a salón books a person, a taller books a person **and** a vehicle (nullable `asset_id`). Commission precedence resolved in code rather than a SQL scoring expression, so the rule people argue about when a payout looks wrong is readable in one place. |
| TSR-163 | Taller vertical — órdenes de trabajo as an order type | **Done (code)** (2026-09-07) — OT screen pending | Owner request 2026-09-07 | **A work order is not a new entity** — it is a `PM` order with `order_type='work_order'`. Parts and labour are both just lines; the status chain already matches a taller with only render-time labels differing (Recibido / En reparación / Listo / Entregado); the cotización step *is* TSR-156's `quote`; and "Facturar pedido" closes it unchanged. The one genuinely new thing is `client_assets`, scoped to the client like departments and stores — a table rather than a JSON blob because per-asset history ("what did we do to this car last time") only exists if the asset outlives the visit. |
| TSR-164 | **Feria del agricultor is a multi-org grouping feature, not a business type** | Not started | Design decision 2026-09-07 | A feria is a *collective of independent vendors*, so the real feature is grouping and settlement across many organizations (shared event, per-vendor sessions, consolidated reporting). It does not fit `business_type` at all, and modelling it as one would paint us into the wrong shape. Deliberately **absent from the picker** rather than present-and-inert. |
| TSR-165 | POS↔sales-api serialization contract: sales-api serializes camelCase, the POS types are snake_case | **Done** (2026-09-08) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | Every documents-list field read back `undefined` — cards rendered `?` doc type, "Invalid Date", ₡0, and React warned on duplicate keys because `sale_id` was undefined. sales-api dumps Pydantic with `by_alias=True` (`jbiller_common/utils/response_utils.py`), so the whole gateway is camelCase. Fixed at the one client that talks to it: `createClient(SALES_API_BASE, { snakeCaseResponses: true })` in `fe/pos-system/src/lib/api.ts`. Requests unchanged — the BE models set `populate_by_name=True`. |
| TSR-166 | Hacienda pipeline stalls after ATV validation in `tsuru/dev`: PDF + notification stages have no deployed consumer | **Done — deployed** (2026-09-08) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | Resolved by TSR-176 (`document-pdf-generator`) and TSR-177 (`document-notification`): both Lambdas are deployed with event-source mappings on their FIFO queues, so the topics that had zero consumers now have one each. Confirmed on every live run since — the runner verifies the PDF in S3 by HeadObject after each emission. `document-pdf-generator` has **no Lambda and no log group** in PACIFIC-PROD (947999370977); `document-notification` has a log group but the function is gone. Their SNS→SQS wiring is intact (`hacienda-pdf-topic.fifo`→`hacienda-pdf-queue.fifo`, `hacienda-notification-*`) but neither queue has an event-source mapping, so anything the validator publishes is never consumed. Live proof: all 5 documents for org `d09b299b` show `notified=false`, `send_attempts=0`, `notification_send_date=null`. Deploy both Lambdas and their event-source mappings. |
| TSR-167 | `tsuru-dev-historical-documents.fifo` has no subscription — the history queue is orphaned | **Done — deployed** (2026-09-12) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | Closed by TSR-253/255. The topic moved into `cloudformation/hacienda-messaging.yml` beside a new `historical-documents-queue.fifo`, and `hacienda-history` now carries a **second** event-source mapping on it. Verified live: `list-subscriptions-by-topic` returns 1 (was 0), and the function reports two ENABLED mappings (`document-history.fifo`, `historical-documents-queue.fifo`). The second mapping needed a manual `sam deploy` — CI runs build → `update-function-code` → API Gateway and **never sam-deploys the per-service stacks**, so an event-source mapping added to a `lambda.yml` does not ship on push. |
| TSR-168 | No application logging reaches CloudWatch from any sales/Hacienda Lambda; 7-day retention on the pipeline groups | **Done — deployed** (2026-09-11); retention still open | QA audit 2026-09-08 | `sales-api`, `hacienda-taxes` and the document lambdas emit only `START`/`END`/`REPORT` — no `logger` output at all, despite `sales_pipeline.py` and `event_publisher.py` logging at INFO on every stage. `document-validator` and `document-notification` groups are set to `retentionInDays: 7` with `storedBytes: 0`. Net effect: the e-invoicing pipeline is unauditable in the one environment where it runs. **Root cause found 2026-09-10:** nothing ever called `logging.basicConfig`, so the root logger kept its default WARNING level and every `logger.info`/`debug` in the shared libs was dropped; separately `ResponseUtils.handle_exception` mapped exceptions to HTTP codes and re-raised them **without logging**, so a 500 left no traceback anywhere. **Fixed for data-be's 34 Hacienda Lambdas** — new `jmarkets_common/configuration/logging_config.py` (`configure_logging()`, `LOG_LEVEL` env override, reuses the Lambda runtime's root handler instead of stacking a second one, pins botocore/boto3/urllib3/sqlalchemy to WARNING), called from `FastApiConfig.__init__` so all 34 services inherit it; `handle_exception` now logs domain errors at WARNING and unhandled ones at ERROR with `exc_info=True`. **Live 2026-09-11**: the `consumer-cabys` group, previously 0 bytes, now carries `Hacienda CABYS v1.0.0 initialized`, the AppConfig SSM path, and — the useful one — `HTTP Request: GET https://api.hacienda.go.cr/fe/cabys?q=almohada&top=20 "HTTP/1.1 200 OK"`, so the upstream Hacienda call is now visible. **sales-be closed 2026-09-11** (`233fa0c`): the same two fixes applied to `jbiller_common` — 87 modules there use plain `logging.getLogger` and were all being discarded. Wiring differs: `FastApiConfig.__init__` covers six services, while `document-validator`, `document-pdf-generator` and `hacienda-history` build their apps directly and call `configure_logging()` explicitly; `lambda-authorizer` already called `basicConfig` and was left alone. Powertools is untouched — its `Logger` keeps its own handler and does not propagate, so the 14 modules using `PowertoolsConfig.get_logger` cannot double-log. Verified live: an identical warm ping that previously produced **nothing** now yields `SaleController routes registered` and `Keep-warm ping received` in `tsuru-dev-sales-api-lambda`, and application INFO now appears in `hacienda-history` and `document-validator` too. **Still open:** `retentionInDays: 7` on the pipeline log groups. |
| TSR-243 | Idle container-image Lambdas return a hard 500 on the first request after ~14 days | **Done — deployed** (2026-09-11) | CABYS 500 report 2026-09-10 | `GET /countries/188/cabys?search=almohada&size=20&type=4` returned 500 twice. The Lambda never ran: API Gateway execution logs show the Lambda service answering **409 `CodeArtifactUserPendingException`** ("Lambda is initializing your function"), which API Gateway converts to a 500 without retrying. All 34 data-be Hacienda functions are `PackageType: Image`; `consumer-cabys` had been idle since its 2026-08-07 deploy (log group 0 bytes, no streams), so AWS had reclaimed its optimized image cache. Confirmed by direct invoke: 200 with 4 correct CABYS rows, `Init Duration: 8607.87 ms` — longer than the 7 s between the two failed attempts. **Fix:** a `KeepWarmRule` EventBridge rule (`rate(5 days)`, constant `Input: {"warmup": true}`) + `KeepWarmLambdaPermission` added to 33 `cloudformation/lambda.yml` templates (`consumer-exchange-rate` skipped — its daily business cron already keeps it warm), and `is_warmup_event`/`warmup_response` in `jmarkets_common/utils/lambda_utils.py` wired into all 34 `lambda_handler`s so a ping costs one init and no application work. The constant `Input` replaces the whole event, so a warm ping can never be mistaken for the `source: aws.events` business cron or an SQS batch. 34/34 templates parse; helper + logging behaviour unit-verified. **Deployed 2026-09-11** in two steps, because the CI flow only covers one of them: pushing `tsuru-data-be` (run `34559744590`, green) rebuilt all 34 images and ran `aws lambda update-function-code`, but **no CI stage sam-deploys the per-service stacks**, so the rules needed `bash deploys/deploy-sam-stacks.sh dev PACIFIC-PROD` — 34/34 OK, **33 `*-keepwarm` rules now ENABLED on `rate(5 days)`**. That order matters: the image must understand warm pings before any rule sends them. Verified live — warm ping returns `{"status": "warm"}` in 2.26 ms with no application work, the real CABYS query returns 200, and CloudFormation re-resolving `:latest` did **not** roll back the image CI had shipped (spot-checked `taxes`/`locations`/`payments`/`consumer-exemptions`). `deploy-sam-stacks.sh` needed two migration leftovers fixed first (commit `8bccf6a`): it looked up `jcampos-*` stack names renamed in `6eec1ad`, which made its stuck-stack recovery dead code, and defaulted to the `J-CAMPOS` profile whose credentials died with `90dce5b`. **Extended to `sales-be` and `store-be` 2026-09-11**: 9 image functions in sales-be and `cd-backend` in store-be now carry the rule (`rate(5 days)`, constant `Input`), and all ten answer the ping. **`management-be` is deliberately excluded** — `tsuru-dev-api-handler` is `PackageType: Zip`, which cannot produce this 409; same for `sales-be/lambda-authorizer`. Applying it there surfaced TSR-247/248. |
| TSR-169 | **Every Hacienda artifact URL points at a hostname that does not resolve** | **Partially fixed** (2026-09-08) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | `/tsuru/dev/hacienda/s3/cloudfront_domain` was `https://tsuru-uploads.jcampos.dev` — **NXDOMAIN**. The live CDN is `https://uploads.tsuru.jcampos.dev`, which serves the identical key `200 application/pdf, 16 813 bytes`. Read by `sales_pipeline.py:687` and `validator_pipeline.py:426` when the URL is **built at emission time**. SSM updated to the live host (v2) **and** the hardcoded default in `be/sales-be/cloudformation/hacienda-params.yml` corrected, so a redeploy no longer reverts it. TSR-106/134 moved the domain and updated cd-backend's SSM but missed this namespace. |
| TSR-169b | The dead host is **persisted** on existing rows — a config fix does not heal them | **Not started** | QA audit 2026-09-08 | `SaleService.get_xml_files` returns the stored `sale.pdf_url` / `xml_url` / `json_url` columns verbatim (`jbiller_common/services/sale_service.py:352`) — it does **not** synthesize. So all 5 existing documents keep `tsuru-uploads.jcampos.dev` in `sales.pdf_url`, `sales.xml_url` and every `attachments.*_file_url`, and their PDFs stay unreachable no matter what SSM says. Needs a one-off backfill (`REPLACE(url,'tsuru-uploads.jcampos.dev','uploads.tsuru.jcampos.dev')`) or a `regenerate_xml` pass. Separately, documents #001–#004 are stored as `signed.xml` while the URL names `Factura Electronica {consecutivo}.xml`, so those keys 404 regardless of host. |
| TSR-170 | A rejected document exposes no rejection reason | **Done** (2026-09-08) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | The parser existed and was never called. `parse_signed_response` decodes the envelope and `parse_errors` splits `DetalleMensaje` into a structured list — the validator persisted the status and the response file but never the reasons, so `billing_sale_hacienda_errors` was empty for every rejected document. Wired into `_update_sale_with_status`, best-effort so an unparseable response still records the STATUS. Two parser bugs fixed on the way: the sandbox says "recibido" not "aceptado" on a rejection, so that boilerplate line survived and pushed the header past the positional `rows[1:]` skip — the header then surfaced as an error with no code. The header is now matched by content. **Backfilled all 17 stored responses (33 error rows)**, so the existing corpus is explicable too. |
| TSR-171 | Billing an order never links the order back to the invoice — a delivered order can be billed twice | **Not started** | QA audit 2026-09-08 | `isOrderInvoiced()` (`fe/pos-system/src/lib/orderToInvoice.ts`) gates the "Facturar pedido" action on `order.invoice.sale_id`/`consecutive_number`, but **nothing writes that field**: the flow builds a document-editor tab client-side and the checkout POSTs `/sales` with the order number only as free text in `notes` (`Pedido #{n}`). The "Facturado" badge can never appear and the guard never fires. |
| TSR-174 | **`cd-backend` is down in `tsuru/dev` — every orders/POS request 502s** | **Fix written, deploy pending** (2026-09-08) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | `app/enums/order_status.py:59` annotates `current: str | None`. PEP 604 unions are only valid as *runtime* expressions on Python 3.10+, and this service deploys on the **python3.9** Lambda runtime, so the annotation is evaluated at def time and raises `TypeError: unsupported operand type(s) for \|: 'type' and 'NoneType'` **at import**. Every invocation of `cd-backend-dev-lambda` dies in `INIT` — orders, products, branches, terminals, sessions, assignments. Introduced by `cded617` (TSR-150…164, 2026-09-07). Fixed with `from __future__ import annotations`; a repo-wide AST scan found this to be the only module affected. **Needs a push to `develop`** so GitHub Actions rebuilds the image. |
| TSR-175 | The POS cannot create any document while TSR-174 stands | **Blocked** | QA audit 2026-09-08 | The new-document editor opens on the shift-setup screen and needs a puesto + terminal. `GET /branches` 502s, so the dropdown is empty, no shift can start, and the cashier never reaches the cart. This is why the end-to-end emission test could not run — the blocker is upstream of the Hacienda pipeline entirely. |
| TSR-176 | Deploy `document-pdf-generator` | **Done — deployed** (2026-09-08) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | PDF generation is **not** internal to sales-api: a separate container Lambda renders HTML→PDF with wkhtmltopdf and then publishes `SEND_NOTIFICATION`, which is why the notification Lambda alone could never close the loop. It had been commented out of the build matrix since Wave 4 as "wkhtmltopdf install fails". Three faults, none of them the 404 the note blamed: AL2023 has no `wkhtmltopdf` package; the fallback called `wget` before installing it (the reported 127, which also swallowed the real error); and this image's dnf5 refuses a local RPM by path *or* URL. Installs via `rpm -Uvh --nodeps` now, gated on `wkhtmltopdf --version`, with DejaVu/Liberation fonts. `tsuru-dev-document-pdf-generator-lambda` is live and consuming `hacienda-pdf-queue.fifo`. |
| TSR-177 | Deploy `document-notification` | **Done — deployed** (2026-09-08) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | First attempt failed early validation (`AWS::EarlyValidation::ResourceExistenceCheck`): the stack declares its own log group, and `/aws/lambda/tsuru-dev-document-notification-lambda` was still there orphaned from a previous deployment whose stack had been deleted. The group held **0 bytes across 0 streams**, so it was removed and the deploy re-run. All 7 SAM stacks green; the Lambda is live and consuming `hacienda-notification-queue.fifo`. **The full chain now has a consumer at every stage for the first time.** |
| TSR-190 | Account actions moved from the sidebar to a navbar menu | **Done** (2026-09-08) | Owner request 2026-09-08 | *Salir* and *Cambiar organización* sat at the bottom of the sidebar, out of reach whenever it was collapsed. They belong to the person rather than the navigation, so they are an avatar dropdown in the navbar now, together with *Perfil*. The sidebar keeps the identity row — still a shortcut to the profile, and it marks active like any other nav item — showing `username` instead of the full four-part name, which only ever rendered ellipsed beside a 28px avatar. |
| TSR-191 | The profile page showed the platform role, not the org role | **Done** (2026-09-08) | Owner report 2026-09-08 | Profile read *Cliente* while the sidebar read *Administrador* for the same person. `ProfilePage` used `user.role` — the PLATFORM-level Cognito role, which is `customer` for everyone after the first sync — instead of the org-scoped role from `my-permissions`. Both surfaces now read the same source, falling back to the platform role only while permissions load. |
| TSR-192 | Profile layout: paired fields, equal cards | **Done** (2026-09-08) | Owner request 2026-09-08 | Personal info is two tracks (name/surname, email/username) with role on its own row; the security card is two side-by-side actions with the icon beside the title and justified copy. Both cards share an even split. |

| TSR-194 | "Exacto" was a no-op once any cash amount existed | **Done** (2026-09-08) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | `exact()` bailed out whenever a `01` cash payment was already present, so after the first entry the button silently did nothing: change the cart and it kept offering the previous sale's figure (₡9 261.4013 on a ₡4 749 cart) with no way to correct it but retyping. It now recomputes cash as `cartTotal` minus any non-cash payments. |
| TSR-195 | Confirmar disabled because the displayed total is not the real total | **Root cause found; display fix pending** (2026-09-08) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | The gate `paidTotal >= cartTotal` was correct — the **display** was wrong. The product's `sale_price` is `4749.4013`, shown everywhere as `₡4 749`. A cashier collecting exactly what the screen says is 40 céntimos short, so Confirmar disables itself with no message. My first float-precision theory was **disproved** (the button stayed disabled with céntimo comparison; that change was kept on its own merits). Unblocked in practice by TSR-194, since "Exacto" now fills the true total. **Still open:** money should be rounded to céntimos at the source so the figure on screen is the figure charged. A disabled primary action that shows no reason is its own defect. |
| TSR-197 | **FE and BE totals disagree by the whole tax — the POS sent no line taxes** | **Fixed (FE)** (2026-09-08) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | Line `taxes` were only populated by the line-detail drawer, which a scan-and-charge never opens, so documents went out with `taxes: []`. sales-api answered *"Payment total 4749.40130 does not cover document total 4333.00000"* — its `TotalComprobante` was the **net**, with no IVA at all. Had a payment happened to match, the invoice would have been filed **under-declaring IVA entirely**. Lines now fall back to the product's configured taxes, deriving the Hacienda `rate_code` from the percentage (13% → `08`); 0% is deliberately not guessed, since it splits across exento / no-sujeto / crédito-pleno. |
| TSR-198 | ~~Catalog prices are internally inconsistent~~ — **retracted**; the POS was not sending the product's discounts | **Fixed (FE)** (2026-09-08) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | **This row's original claim was wrong.** The catalog is perfectly consistent, as the owner pointed out: the product carries a **3% discount** (`discount_type_id` 17, `amount` 129.99) and every figure ties out — `4333 − 129.99 = 4203.01`, `× 13% = 546.3913` (matches `taxes[0].amount`), `4203.01 + 546.3913 = 4749.4013` (matches `sale_price`). The real defect was the same one as TSR-197: line `discounts` were only populated by the line-detail drawer, so a scan-and-charge sent none and the backend computed the total without the discount. Lines now fall back to the product's configured discounts, resolving the stored **catalog row id** through the discount-types catalog (unlike taxes, `discount_type_id` is not a Hacienda code). A discount whose code will not resolve is dropped rather than guessed — natures 01/03 divert IVA to `ImpuestoAsumidoEmisorFabrica`, so an invented code changes the line's tax treatment. |
| TSR-213 | **Note 20: the discount nature drives the tax base and who pays it** | **Done — BE deployed + FE mapped** (2026-09-08) | `0fed71c` sales-be; `useCartFlow` + `services/note20.test.ts` in pos-system | The rule now lives in ONE place — `discount_service.line_discount_natures()` with `NATURES_ISSUER_ASSUMES={01,03}` and `NATURES_BASE_NOT_ERODED={01,02,03}` — and both `sales_pipeline._compute_lines` and `summary_service` read it, so the line and the summary can no longer disagree. **01/03**: base stays gross, the VAT leaves `impuesto_neto` and lands in `ImpuestoAsumidoEmisorFabrica` (previously hardcoded 0, so issuer-assumed IVA went undeclared). **02**: same gross base, but the tax stays on the customer. **04-09**: base erodes, unchanged. FE mapped in the same session: the POS cart totals used to be `Σ price×qty` with tax backed out as the difference — the stored gross price, no tax engine involved — which agreed with the BE only because the catalog uses nature 07. `useCartFlow` now runs each line through `DiscountCalculationService` + `TaxCalculationService`, the same engines the document is built from. `note20.test.ts` pins all four natures to the BE's longhand numbers (gross 1000, 10% discount, IVA 13% → 01/03: net 0, assumed 130, total 900; 02: net 130, total 1030; 07: net 117, total 1017). That test caught a real bug on the way in: the cart was passing gross as `base_amount`, which is the IVACE manual-base override, so every ordinary-discount line was taxed on its pre-discount amount. |
| TSR-214 | Payments must EQUAL the total — the POS was sending cash tendered | **Done** (2026-09-08) | Matrix run 2026-09-08 | The spec restriction is `TotalComprobante == Sumatoria(MontoTotalMedioPago)`, so overpayment is as invalid as underpayment. The POS sent the tendered amount, so the ₡5 000/₡10 000 quick-amount buttons produced documents sales-api rejects — only *Exacto* ever worked. A payment now carries `amount` (fiscal), `tendered_amount` and `change_amount`, with migration `j0k1l2m3n4o5`, so the drawer reconciles without putting the tendered figure on a fiscal document. The error message also said "10000 does not cover 4749"; it now names the direction. |

It is advisory: "favor proceder actualizar sus datos". It has never blocked an acceptance. The claim that every document sits at PROCESSING was an artifact of reading the runner's *first poll*, which reports the status at that instant, not the terminal one. Actual distribution across the 33 documents: **11 accepted, 11 rejected, 11 never validated** (status 0). Real rejection causes are per-document and are tracked in TSR-224. |

Two spellings of one fact, so they could disagree, and did: every code-99 line came back "discount.other_discount_type is required when discount_type=99". **Decision (owner, 2026-09-08): the FE sends `reason`; `other_discount_type` is an XML-only field derived backend-side.** `DiscountDTO` now mirrors the two in a model validator — reason fills the XML field on the way in, and the XML field fills reason on the way back, so documents read from Hacienda are not left with a blank reason. Both backend checks (`DiscountValidator`, `DiscountService._validate_codes`) accept reason-only and still reject a genuinely empty one; verified that `build_xml_descuentos` emits `CodigoDescuentoOtros='Ajuste comercial'` from a reason-only line. `DiscountsTab` writes only `reason` and shows the field on nature 99 alone. |
| TSR-218 | IVA rate matrix + enum/catalog fidelity | **Done** (2026-09-08) | `services/ivaRates.test.ts`; `be/data-be/scripts/catalogs_seed_data.json` | All 11 `TarifaIVA` codes tested for amount, for the rate code filed with Hacienda, and for Note 20 at every rate. | TSR-219 | Transitional IVA rates were accepted on invoices | **Done — BE** (2026-09-08) | `hacienda/validators/tax_validator.py`; `document_validator.py`; analysis doc Nota 8.1 | The v4.4 rate table marks rate codes **05** and **06** "Exclusive use NC/ND" and **07** "Disabled / NC/ND only" — they are legal only on an electronic debit note (02) or credit note (03). Nothing enforced it: `TaxValidator` checked only that the code was in the enum, and `TaxService._rate_for` prices any code it is handed without asking what document carries it, so an invoice could be filed at transitional 8% and would be rejected by Hacienda **after** burning a consecutive. `TaxValidator.validate` now takes `document_type` (keyword-optional, so existing callers are unaffected) and rejects the three transitional codes on anything but 02/03, with `HACIENDA_TAX_RATE_CODE_NC_ND_ONLY`. `DocumentValidator` passes the type through. Verified as a matrix across FE/TE/ND/NC/FEE: the three transitional codes reject everywhere except the two note types; the other eight accept everywhere. **Consequence for the FE**: on the documents the POS emits, the transitional codes do not exist — so 8% has no valid code at all and 4% has exactly one (`04`), which is why `ivaRateCodeFor` maps neither ambiguously. |

Verified against the **signed XML** rather than the API response: `CodigoTarifaIVA` matches the requested code and `TotalComprobante` matches the expected total on all eight — 4333.00000 / 4376.33000 / 4419.66000 / 4506.32000 / 4896.29000 / 4354.66500 / 4333.00000 / 4333.00000 for codes 01/02/03/04/08/09/10/11. These are the same per-rate figures the FE `ivaRates.test.ts` asserts, scaled to net 4333, so FE and BE agree at every rate. Transitional codes 05/06/07 excluded by design (TSR-219). Status is PROCESSING, not ACCEPTED, for the usual TSR-216 reason. |
| TSR-221 | Tax type codes 07/08/12 were shifted by one in sales-be | **Done — BE** (2026-09-08) | `hacienda/enums/tax_type.py`; data-be catalog; analysis doc tax table | The BE enum named 07 `OTROS`, gave 08 the "IVA (cálculo especial)" description that belongs to 07, and named 12 for the used-goods regime that is actually 08. The data-api catalog (country 188) and the v4.4 analysis doc agree with each other and against the enum: **07** IVA cálculo especial (editable base), **08** IVA Régimen de Bienes Usados (factor), **12** Impuesto Específico al Cemento. Consequence: 07 — the one code whose entire purpose is an editable base — was not in `_IVA_CODES`, so it fell through to the generic branch and was rejected with "requires either amount or rate". Corrected, and both `_IVA_CODES` sets (service and validator) now cover 01/07/08. Only `tax_validator` imported the renamed members, so the rename was contained. |
| TSR-222 | Editable taxable base + used-goods factor now honoured | **Done — BE, verified live 2/2** (2026-09-08) | `tax_service.editable_base_for`; `tax_validator.validate_line`; migration `k1l2m3n4o5p6`; run `hacienda-live-20260909T015216Z` | `base_amount` was already accepted and persisted by the API but **never read** — `TaxService` priced every line off the subtotal, so a POS line with an edited base silently disagreed with the FE. Per the analysis doc the base may be edited in exactly two situations, neither of them a rate: **tax code 07** ("the taxable base is edited directly and the tax is calculated on it") and **IVACobradoFabrica "01"** ("the taxable base is editable in these cases"). `editable_base_for` owns that rule; a base supplied anywhere else is rejected up front as `HACIENDA_BASE_AMOUNT_NOT_EDITABLE` rather than quietly applied. Separately **code 08** computed `subtotal x factor x tarifa`, over-taxing by the rate — the doc says the tax "results from multiplying the subtotal by the margin factor", so the factor IS the calculation; the factor value comes from the data-api catalog but only the value travels, it is not a Hacienda code. Added `iva_collected_factory` (DTO, ORM, mapper, `_LineAdapter`) and populated `IVACobradoFabrica`, which the mapper had hardcoded to None. Verified live on signed XML: code 07 → BaseImponible 5000.00000 (not the 4333 subtotal), ImpuestoNeto 650.00000, total 4983.00000; IVACobradoFabrica 01 → same figures with the flag present in the XML. |
| TSR-223 | Repo-root `settings.cfg` pointed sales-be tooling at another service | **Done** (2026-09-08) | `be/sales-be/settings.cfg` | The root file was a verbatim copy of `app/organization-configurations/settings.cfg`, so every tool run from the repo root resolved config under `/tsuru/{stage}/organization-configurations`. AppConfig searches `settings.cfg` before `src/settings.cfg`, so the copy won: `alembic upgrade` — which migrates the **sales** schema — looked for its database secret in a different service's namespace and failed with "Database credentials not found ... SSM keys 'database.secret' or 'aws.database' are missing", wording that reads as absent credentials rather than as looking in the wrong place. Repointed at the shared `commondata` namespace, which is both where the database secret lives and the value `_resolve_base_path()` already falls back to. Per-app files under `app/<name>/` are untouched. Verified: `alembic current` now resolves with no `SSM_BASE_PATH` override, and the Hacienda live runner — which also reads the root file — still passes 1/1. Noted in the file that only `/tsuru/dev` is provisioned; stag and prod hold zero parameters as of 2026-09-08. |
| TSR-224 | Hacienda's rejection reasons name real defects in the fiscal work | **Done — 36/36 ACEPTADO live** (2026-09-08) | 17 stored Respuesta Hacienda XMLs, backfilled into `billing_sale_hacienda_errors` | All seven fixed and verified by real accepted documents. **Rate buckets**: a zero-rated line's bucket comes from its RATE CODE, not from the amount — rate 01 and 11 are NO SUJETO, 10 is EXENTO (-481/-485/-107/-111). **-488**: the IVA family was the literal `{"01","08"}` in three more places, so code 07 was treated as a local tax and its CodigoTarifaIVA suppressed; centralized as `_IVA_FAMILY`. **-451**: the summary knew only about discount natures, so a factory-collected line was assumed on the line and payable in the resumen — the rule is now one function, `line_assumes_tax()`, read by both. **-454**: `BaseImponible` is Subtotal + ISC/ISEBA/ISEBEC, not the base the tax was computed on; the two were conflated. **-518**: natures 01/03 must carry a 100% discount, enforced locally. **-45**: nature 02 cannot tax the un-eroded base — -45 ties ImpuestoNeto to BaseImponible x tarifa and -454 ties BaseImponible to Subtotal, so 02 was dropped from `NATURES_BASE_NOT_ERODED` (01/03 escape only because they assume the tax). **-1**: the XSD element is `CodigoDescuentoOTRO`, in caps — the misspelling failed XSD validation on every code-99 document. Live: discount matrix 12/12, rate matrix 8/8, regression 7/7, final 9/9. |
| TSR-225 | Test harness reported the first poll as the outcome | **Done** (2026-09-08) | `tests/local/hacienda_live_runner.py` `_poll_to_terminal` | GENERATE_PDF stopped being proof of a terminal verdict when PDF generation moved to emission (TSR-207) — the validator publishes it while Hacienda still answers "procesando". The poll loop broke on that event and reported PROCESSING, so documents Hacienda went on to ACCEPT were recorded as never resolving. This is what produced the false "everything is stuck at PROCESSING" reading behind TSR-216. The loop now keeps polling while the status is PROCESSING and a terminal verdict is expected. |
| TSR-226 | Specific excises wired end to end; 72/72 accepted live | **Done — BE + FE, 8 suites 72/72 ACEPTADO** (2026-09-08) | `hacienda/services/tax_service.py`; `discount_service.FACTORY_ASSUMED_EXCISES`; `fe/pos-system/src/lib/specialTaxes.ts`; suites `mixed_taxes` / `factory_matrix` / `alcohol_matrix` / `isebec` | Every remaining tax type (ISC, IUC, ISEBA, ISEBEC, IPT, ISEC) filed as real documents, mixed with special discounts, IVACE, IVARBU and the factory-assumed codes. Four rules came out of the rejections rather than the prose. **Assumption is per TAX ROW, not per line** — 03/04/05/12 are absorbed by the issuer always (-476/-488), while the IVA on the same line may still be charged. **`BaseImponible` = Subtotal + ISC(02) + ISEBA(04) + ISEBEC(05) + ISEC(12)**, and IVA is computed on top of it (-454 + -45 together). **The millilitre trap**: `CantidadUnidadMedida` and `VolumenUnidadConsumo` allow 2 fraction digits, not 5, so a 355 ml can is rejected on the SCHEMA before any arithmetic — the volume rounds to the centilitre and `Proporcion` is derived from the ROUNDED volume, or the declared quantity and amount disagree. **ISEBEC is two formulas**: beverages divide by the consumption volume, jabón de tocador multiplies by it (the field carries grams), decided by CABYS prefix `35321010101` — the old "2202"/"3401" prefixes are Harmonized System headings that match no CABYS, so both branches were dead. ISC and every other rate-driven code now DERIVE the amount from the rate rather than trusting the caller (-45). Not implemented and documented in-code: the "Detalle de productos del surtido, paquetes o combos" variant of ISC and ISEBEC — `DetalleSurtido` is not modelled anywhere in the stack. Final run: discount_matrix 12/12, rate_matrix 8/8, regression 7/7, final 9/9, mixed_taxes 12/12, factory_matrix 12/12, alcohol_matrix 9/9, isebec 3/3. |
| TSR-227 | Retail-chain data on documents, keyed on the client | **Done — FE + BE, verified live 1/1** (2026-09-08) | `fe/pos-system/src/lib/chainClients.ts`; `checkout/sections/ChainClientSection.tsx`; `document_xml_mapper._map_otros` | A chain like Walmart will not process an invoice without its own purchase-order number and delivery point. That is a property of the **customer**, not of our organization, so it keys on the client's cédula jurídica through a registry (`ChainClientId.WALMART = 3102007223`) rather than on our `isRetailSupplier` flag — which said only that we sell to chains at all, true of every customer including the corner shop. Matching on the NAME was rejected as a shortcut: "Walmart", "WAL MART CR" and a typo are one taxpayer, and a renamed catalog entry would silently stop producing the required fields. Adding a chain is one entry plus a card. The fields were duplicated on the Pedido card (manual orders only) while the chain needs them on the **electronic invoice** too; consolidated into one card that appears for any document type. They reach the XML as coded `OtroTexto` entries — `ordenCompra`, `departamento`, `puntoEntrega`, `gln` — because the chain reconciles against the comprobante, so a purchase-order number living only in our own order record is invisible to them. `_map_otros` replaced a hardcoded `Otros=None`. Verified on a real accepted document carrying all four nodes. |
| TSR-228 | Every crossdocking order 500'd on a stray identifier | **Done — store-be** (2026-09-09) | `app/mappers/orders_mapper.py` `build_crossdocking_data` | `GET /orders?search=…` raised `NameError: name 'ln' is not defined` for any organization with a crossdocking order. The line was pasted from `order_to_response`, which iterates `ln` over ORDER LINES and falls back to the line's own description because a manual line need not be a catalog product. Neither applies to a crossdocking item: the model has no description column, so `it.description` would only have traded the NameError for an AttributeError. Takes the product's description. |
| TSR-229 | store-be's engine had drifted from the biller; imported lines now costed | **Done — store-be, 52 unit tests** (2026-09-10) | `services/tax_calculation_service.py`; `services/order_service.py`; `tests/unit/services/test_imported_order_lines.py`; `scripts/backfill_order_line_calculations.py` | The orders service carries its own copy of the discount/tax engine and it had diverged from the one that files documents — every divergence a place where a pedido and the invoice it becomes disagree. Brought in line with TSR-224/TSR-226: nature 02 cascades ordinarily, excises 03/04/05/12 are issuer-assumed per tax row, `IVACobradoFabrica` 01 is honoured (-451), ISEBEC branches on the real CABYS prefix with the two correct formulas, volumes quantize to 2 fraction digits before the amount is derived from them, and code 99 prices off the subtotal. Separately `_hydrate_tax_jsonb` zipped input DTOs against results returned in Hacienda's **evaluation** order, so a product listing its IVA first had the ISC amount written onto the IVA row. **Imported lines now carry fiscal detail**: the Excel has no tax structure and — in practice — no line discounts either, so the product supplies taxes/codes/CABYS/net price and the order's discount is recorded as 07 Descuento Comercial; when every line reads zero while the header totals a discount, it is allocated across the lines in proportion to gross with the rounding remainder on the largest line. Lines are recomputed over the ORDER quantity and the order totals re-added from the lines. **Reprocess** now recomputes whether or not the spreadsheet is still on file, which makes the button a repair tool; `backfill_order_line_calculations.py` does the same across existing rows, dry-run by default. The two storage spellings of `order_line.taxes` (manual wrote the request shape, imports the canonical one) are unified, with legacy rows normalized on read. |
| TSR-230 | Facturar pedido opens the checkout drawer, nothing else | **Done — FE, 223 tests** (2026-09-10) | `components/pos/checkout/OrderCheckoutDrawer.tsx`; `hooks/useInvoiceOrder.ts`; `lib/orderToInvoice.ts` | Billing a pedido opened a document TAB and navigated into the POS workspace: the user got a full point-of-sale screen they had not asked for with the drawer on top of it, and the order's lines had to survive a round-trip through the cart store — which required every product to be in the offline catalog cache, so a missing one was dropped and the checkout opened showing **a total of zero**. `useCartFlow` now takes an optional set of lines instead of always reading the cart store, and `CheckoutDrawer` takes its document type and form state from the caller; `OrderCheckoutDrawer` composes them. Same receiver, payments, references, copies and chain card; different lines; no tab, because billing an existing pedido is not the authoring of a new document. The lines come from the ORDER, which already carries CABYS, net price, codes, taxes and discounts — rebuilding from the catalog would re-price a delivered sale at today's terms. **The Walmart card never appeared for a client picked in the POS**: the checkout read `receiver?.identification?.number ?? selectedClient?…` and an untouched receiver carries `number: ''`, which is not nullish, so the fallback never ran; it uses `resolveReceiverId` now, and orders carry the client id + identification from the BE. Two fiscal fixes found on the way: an ad-hoc line discount was filed as nature **01 Regalía** (which moves the line's whole IVA into `ImpuestoAsumidoEmisorFabrica`, declaring that the issuer absorbed tax the customer paid) and is now 07 Comercial; and a manual order sent estimated line money and no structured taxes at all, so a POS-captured excise could not be recovered when the pedido was billed. |
| TSR-231 | Order money is two decimals, and the lines add up to the total | **Done — store-be + FE, 3 000-order property check** (2026-09-10) | `app/utils/money.py`; `fe/pos-system/src/lib/money.ts`; `tests/unit/services/test_imported_order_lines.py::TestOrderMoneyPrecision` | `_round_money` quantized at 5 dp while its own docstring said 2, so an order imported from a spreadsheet of whole colones stored figures like `4903.63104`. Worse, order totals were summed from RAW line values and rounded at the end — which is a different number from the sum of the rounded lines. Over 3 000 generated multi-line orders with a header discount the two diverged on **47%**, which on screen is an order whose lines visibly do not add up to its total. The rule is now owned in one place per side — round each part, then add the ROUNDED parts — and `allocate_money` splits a header discount so the shares add back exactly, remainder to the largest line. The same 3 000 orders now reconcile on all three checks: no sub-céntimo figures, no line whose total differs from its own subtotal plus tax, no order whose total differs from the sum of its lines. The FE mirror shifts through the DECIMAL STRING rather than multiplying, because `Math.round(1.005 * 100)` rounds down where Python's `ROUND_HALF_UP` rounds up; verified identical across the midpoint cases. Hacienda's five decimals are untouched — a comprobante is computed by sales-api at its own precision and an order is not a fiscal document. |
| TSR-232 | An order line now mirrors the document line, field for field | **Done — store-be + FE, migration `bd0e1f2a3b4c` applied to dev** (2026-09-10) | `alembic/versions/bd0e1f2a3b4c_order_line_document_fields.py`; `app/dtos/requests/manual_order_dto.py`; `orders_mapper`; `fe/pos-system/src/lib/orderToInvoice.ts` | Anything the POS knew that the order line had no column for was lost at capture and had to be guessed back from the catalog when the pedido was billed — from a catalog that may have moved since the customer agreed to the order. Added `codes`, `unit_measure`, `commercial_unit_measure`, `customs_part`, `base_amount`, `iva_collected_factory`. **`codes` is the line's OWN**: the response derived internal/vendor/buyer codes from the linked PRODUCT's array, which holds whatever the most recent import wrote, so two chains ordering the same product overwrote each other and an order could display a different customer's article code; the spreadsheet gives every line its own. **`unit_measure`** is required by Hacienda on every line — with no column, billing a pedido fell back to "Unid", misdeclaring anything sold by weight or volume. **`base_amount` + `iva_collected_factory`** are the only two situations where the taxable base may legitimately depart from the subtotal, and they now reach the calculator on the imported path, the manual path and the FE cart, so a factory-collected line stops charging the customer IVA the issuer absorbs (-451) — `TaxCalculationService` had never checked that flag at all, so the till showed a total the document contradicts by exactly the tax. Verified by comparing the field sets: every non-computed `CommonDetailDTO`/`DetailDTO` field is now present on the order request DTO, the order response DTO and the product DTO (the three product gaps — `product_id`, `net_tax`, `total_amount_line` — are correct by design: a catalog product has no line identity and no per-line computed totals). Known remaining gap: per-tax `exemption` is on the document's `TaxDTO` but modelled at PRODUCT level (`exemption_authorization_code` / `exempted_rate` / `exemption_amount`) rather than per tax row, so a line-level exoneration does not travel through an order. |
| TSR-233 | Chain card keyed on data, not only on a registry entry | **Done — FE** (2026-09-10) | `lib/chainClients.ts`; `hooks/useChainClient.ts`; `docs/CLIENT_STATUS_GUIDE.md` | The Walmart card still did not appear after TSR-230's `??` fix, and the remaining cause was the DATA, not the lookup. A client created by an order import has **no cédula** — the spreadsheet has no such column, `client_repo.upsert` takes only name and GLN — and is written with **status 0** ("pending, auto-created via order, incomplete data"), which also keeps it out of the POS client picker (`?search=status:1`). So the registry had nothing to match on however the lookup was written. `chainClientFor` now takes every identity the caller holds — the cashier's typed receiver, the catalog client, the order's GLN — because the two routes into the checkout know different things about the same customer. `useChainClient` adds the decisive signal: a client with **departments or delivery points on file** is a chain customer by definition, which needs no registry entry and is true the day the import runs. `ManualOrderSection` already gated its delivery-point picker on exactly that check, so the pedido card and the invoice card now agree about who is a chain instead of deciding separately. The card names the chain when one is identified and stays generic otherwise. `ChainClientGln` exists but is deliberately EMPTY — a guessed GLN would silently attach one chain's purchase-order fields to another company's documents. |
| TSR-234 | Receiver/customer panel discarded the selected client | **Done — FE, 10 tests** (2026-09-10) | `components/clients/ClientDrawerForm.tsx`; `sections/IdentitySection.tsx`; `clientDrawerForm.test.ts` | Four bugs, all producing a drawer with fields the client plainly has. **(1)** `isReceiverTouched` decides whether the selected client fills the form, and it counted `identification: { code: "01", number: "" }` as user input — the exact shape the id-type select writes the moment it renders — because the object held one non-empty value. The client was therefore discarded and the identification, address and GLN all came back empty. It now asks whether each field identifies somebody: an identification needs a NUMBER, a residence a location, a phone a number. **(2)** Inside the mapper, `r?.identification?.number ?? src?…` hit the same empty-string-is-not-nullish trap as the checkout (TSR-230): the form writes `""`, so `??` returned it and the client's cédula never reached the field. **(3)** The id TYPE was wrong for a different reason — `IdentitySection` resets the code, clearing the number and razón social with it, whenever the current code is absent from the list filtered by customer type, and that effect fired when the identifications catalog finished loading, comparing against a customer type not yet resolved. Every client without `customer_type` on record — which is every client auto-created from an order import — opened with a wrong id type and an empty id. The reset now fires only when nationality or customer type actually changes, `buildForm` infers the customer type from the id code, and a saved code outside the filtered set stays selectable (a `<select>` with no matching `<option>` silently shows the first one). **(4)** The trade name was seeded from the receiver's `name` (the legal name) and `formToReceiver` echoed back `carryOver.trade_name`, so the razón social appeared in both boxes and the input was read-only in practice; it maps to `trade_name` both ways now. |
| TSR-235 | Product form: one engine, a working unit picker, the rate code | **Done — FE, 8 tests** (2026-09-10) | `hooks/useProductLineAmounts.ts`; `products/sections/GeneralInfoSection.tsx`; `products/sections/OtherTaxSection.tsx` | The product form computed the same line **two different ways on one screen**: `ProductDrawerForm` passed the tax service `subtotal: price` — the PRE-discount amount — plus a raw `discounts` array the service documents as ignored for routing, while `CommercialValueSection` below it ran the cascade properly. A discounted product previewed its IVA on the wrong base, and a royalty or bonus nature never routed into `ImpuestoAsumidoEmisorFabrica` at all. `useProductLineAmounts` is the single path now — discount cascade first (which also validates nature 99's required reason), then the tax service with the routing flags coming from the cascade, the same order `LineDetailDrawer` and `useCartFlow` use, at quantity 1 because a product is the template for a line rather than a line. Per-discount amounts shown are the cascaded ones; they were `price × rate` each, so the rows did not add up to the total beside them. **The unit-of-measure control was rendered but never connected** — the `<select>` had no `value` and its `onChange` only flipped local state, the free-text input had neither — so no product could carry a unit and every invoice built from one fell back to "Unid". It stores the Hacienda code now, mirrors the commercial unit and defaults to `Unid`. `unit_measure`, `commercial_unit_measure`, `customs_part` and `base_amount` now reach the backend at all, and the tax rate CODE travels with the percentage, taken from the CABYS row which names it — the percentage is not a key, since exento (10), no sujeto (11) and crédito pleno (01) are all 0%. Also brought over the line detail's two special-tax auto-selections (the ISEBA alcohol bracket from the degree, the ISEBEC amount from the CABYS) and collapsed the two copies of the empty form, which had already drifted. |
| TSR-236 | Products and orders backfilled; order 2900679405 corrected | **Done — applied to dev** (2026-09-10) | `scripts/backfill_product_fiscal_defaults.py`; `scripts/set_order_status.py`; `scripts/backfill_order_line_calculations.py` | A product created by an order import has no unit and no taxes — the spreadsheet has no fiscal columns — so every line built from one fell back to "Unid" and carried **no IVA**, which is why 26 orders reported `taxes = 0`. Backfilled **41 units and 39 IVA rows**, taking the rate from the product's CABYS where it has one and 13% otherwise, and never touching a product that already has a tax (a genuinely exempt article is a rate-10 row, not an absent one, so "no taxes" unambiguously means "never configured"). Then re-costed all **42 orders**; both scripts are idempotent and the lines sum to their totals exactly. `set_order_status.py` corrects a status backwards, which the API cannot do — `ORDER_STATUS_TRANSITIONS` is forward-only, right for the workflow but leaving a mis-click unfixable; ran it for **2900679405**, which was `delivered` (not `shipped`) and is now `processing`. One self-inflicted bug caught in the same session: the order backfill briefly filled `OrderLine.base_amount` from `Product.base_amount`, but the product column is a computed OUTPUT (the IVA base at quantity 1) while the line column is the editable-base OVERRIDE the calculator prices off — that pinned a 23-unit line's tax to one unit's base and dropped the order total by ₡12 000. Fixed in the import path and the backfill, with a one-time repair for the values written. |
| TSR-206 | Two PDF surfaces — the action modal had its own tab | **Done** (2026-09-08) | Owner report 2026-09-08 | Opening *Validación* and then clicking the modal's PDF tab produced a second, different viewer for the same document. `DocumentPdfDialog` is the PDF surface; the tab is gone from `DocumentActionModal`. |
| TSR-207 | PDF is generated at emission instead of behind validation | **Done — deployed** (2026-09-08) | Owner request 2026-09-08 | GENERATE_PDF was only published by the validator after a terminal ATV status, so the printable copy queued behind a polling loop that retries `hacienda.validator.max_attempts` times and can sit in PROCESSING indefinitely — which is why 006-008 had signed XML and no PDF. sales-api now publishes GENERATE_PDF alongside SAVE_DOCUMENT (own FIFO dedup id), and the validator still republishes on a terminal status to refresh the artifact. |
| TSR-208 | Notification waits for the PDF | **Done — deployed** (2026-09-08) | Owner request 2026-09-08 | The customer's copy is the PDF, so `notification_pipeline` returns early while `pdf_url` is empty. With the PDF and the validation now able to finish in either order, whichever lands last re-triggers the send; the pre-existing `_already_sent` guard keeps that from emailing twice. |
| TSR-209 | `deploy-api` failed: the deploy role could not add Lambda permissions | **Done — green** (2026-09-08) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | The API-Gateway stack creates an `AWS::Lambda::Permission` per backing function; the GitHub Actions role had `apigateway:*` but no `lambda:AddPermission`, so the deployment failed on the first one and could not roll it back either (DELETE_FAILED). It only started failing once the document-* lambdas existed. Granted in `cloudformation/deploy-role.yml`; the workflow is green. |
| TSR-210 | ATV status 0 rendered a literal "0" and no badge | **Done** (2026-09-08) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | A just-submitted document sits at `validation_status = 0`, and `{status && …}` both hid the badge and printed "0" into the action row. 0 is now a first-class state (*En proceso*), and the Validación action shows for it. |
| TSR-211 | Discount-nature matrix fixtures | **Done (fixtures); run pending** | Owner request 2026-09-08 | `tests/local/suites/discount_matrix.json` plus 11 cases under `app/sales-api/tests/local/cases/discount_*.json` — one document per nature 01-09/99, identical otherwise, so any summary difference is attributable to the nature alone; plus a 100% case on nature 07 to isolate the zero-total edge the owner suspects exists in the catalog. `payments[0].amount` is null by design: the computed total is what is being measured. |
| TSR-212 | On-demand revalidation / scheduled sweep | **Not started** | Owner request 2026-09-08 | Clicking *Validación* on an unvalidated document should poll the ATV, or an EventBridge rule should sweep pending documents every 30 minutes. Neither exists: sales-api has no revalidate endpoint (only `POST …/invoice-validation`, which is the receiver accept/reject action) and the validator has no scheduled entry point. Needs either a new endpoint plus FE wiring, or a scheduled rule on the validator. |
| TSR-201 | sales-api had no S3 **and** no SNS permission | **Done — deployed** (2026-09-08) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | `tsuru-dev-sales-api-role` carried neither. Without S3 the pipeline died on `s3:PutObject` at step 14, *after* submitting to Hacienda. With S3 granted the document persisted — and the pipeline still never started, because step 16's `sns:Publish` was also missing and `sales_pipeline` deliberately swallows a publish failure so it cannot roll back a submitted document.
