Both grants added to `app/sales-api/cloudformation/lambda.yml` and deployed. **Documents 006–008 now emit, persist, upload their signed XML and reach the validator.** |
| TSR-204 | `document-validator` image had never worked — `src.`-prefixed imports | **Done — deployed** (2026-09-08) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | The image copies the *contents* of `src/` into `LAMBDA_TASK_ROOT`, so the runtime packages are `controllers`/`handlers`/`services` — there is no `src`. Six modules still imported `from src.…`, so every invocation died with `Runtime.ImportModuleError: No module named 'src'` before reaching any code. It only became visible once sales-api was finally allowed to publish. `document-pdf-generator` and `document-notification` already use bare imports, which is why they package correctly. Fixed and deployed; the validator now runs clean and polls the ATV on its retry cadence. |
| TSR-205 | `deploy-api` job fails in the sales-be workflow | **Not started** | QA audit 2026-09-08 | The API-Gateway regeneration step fails with a CloudFormation `HandlerErrorCode: General…` on every run. It does not block the SQS-driven pipeline (`update-functions` succeeds and the images ship), but it means new or changed HTTP endpoints are not reaching the gateway. |
| TSR-202 | **A document is filed with Hacienda before it is persisted, inside a transaction that rolls back** | **Not started — design flaw** | QA audit 2026-09-08 | `sales_pipeline` submits to the ATV at step 13 and only *then* uploads the XML (14) and writes the log row (15), all inside the SaleService transaction. When the S3 upload failed with AccessDenied the transaction rolled back — so consecutive `00100001010000000006` was allocated, a clave was built and the document was handed to Hacienda, while the sale list still shows **5 documents and no record of it**. A legal filing must not be able to outlive the local record; submission has to move after persistence, or become a separate committed step with its own retry. |
| TSR-203 | S3 key prefix uses the raw `STAGE` instead of the stage token | **Not started** | QA audit 2026-09-08 | `_upload_signed_xml` and `_resolve_s3_bucket` read `os.getenv("STAGE")` directly (`DEVELOPMENT`) rather than `_stage_token()` (`dev`). The new object key was `hacienda/DEVELOPMENT/...` while every existing artifact — and the whole SSM namespace — lives under `dev`. Only the bucket name escapes it, because the SSM `hacienda.s3.bucket` override masks the same bug there. |
| TSR-199 | Branch/terminal UUIDs were never sent, so every sale failed validation | **Done** (2026-09-08) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | The payload carried only the Hacienda `branch_number`/`terminal_number` codes, but `_resolve_branch_and_terminal` validates the **identifiers** — the Sale row then took a generated default and sales-api rejected it with *"Branch <uuid> not found for organization"*, a different uuid each attempt. The setup screen already knows both, so `sessionContext` now records `branch_id`/`terminal_id` and the checkout sends them. |
| TSR-200 | Assignment cache masked a valid "no active assignment" answer | **Done** (2026-09-08) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | `useAssignment` threw its own "no assignment" error **inside** the try, so a successful empty result fell through to the offline cache and resurrected an assignment naming a branch the organization no longer has. The cache is now used only on a transport failure, and a confirmed-empty answer clears the stale row. |
| TSR-196 | Line `unit_measure` is only set via the line-detail drawer | **Done** (2026-09-08) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | Hacienda requires `UnidadMedida` on every line, but the POS only populated it when the cashier opened the line-detail drawer — which a plain scan-and-charge never does — so the most ordinary sale came back *"unit_measure is required"*. Defaults to `Unid` when absent. |
| TSR-183 | The selected organization lived in `sessionStorage` — two tabs, two tenants | **Done** (2026-09-08) | Owner request 2026-09-08 | `selectedOrgId` was written to `sessionStorage`, which is scoped to a single browser tab, so any second tab or restored window silently fell back to `orgs[0]`. The same user genuinely had two tabs open on two different organizations. Moved to `localStorage` behind one accessor (`lib/selectedOrg.ts`) with try/catch for private-mode. Closes the mechanism behind TSR-182. |
| TSR-184 | Document drafts were shared across organizations | **Done** (2026-09-08) | Owner request 2026-09-08 | `pos-document-store` persisted one flat `open_documents` list with no organization on it, so switching tenants carried the open tabs — each with a cart, a selected client and a receiver — straight over. The store now keys parked drafts by organization (`documents_by_org`) and `OrgProvider` swaps them in a `useLayoutEffect`, so no tab paints for another tenant even for a frame. Unattributed drafts from before the upgrade are dropped once rather than guessed at. |
| TSR-185 | Switch organization without logging out | **Done** (2026-09-08) | Owner request 2026-09-08 | Changing tenants required a full logout/login because the selection was only ever written at login. The sidebar now has a *Cambiar organización* entry that routes to the existing picker; combined with TSR-183/184 the switch persists and carries no drafts across. |
| TSR-186 | Branch list loads on mount instead of on focus | **Done** (2026-09-08) | Owner request 2026-09-08 | `SessionSetupScreen` fetched its puestos in the field's `onFocus`. Now loaded on mount like every other select in the app — a shift cannot start without a puesto, so that list is never the place to save a request. |
| TSR-187 | Receiver identification is sent with its display dashes | **Done** (2026-09-08) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | With the receiver finally populated (TSR-180), sales-api answered *"Identification.number must be all digits"*: clients are stored and displayed as `1-1664-0506` and that literal string went onto the document. `buildReceiver` now strips non-digits for both the form receiver and the selected-client fallback, leaving the display format untouched everywhere else. |
| TSR-188 | Most products carry no CABYS, and the POS only finds out at submit | **Not started** | QA audit 2026-09-08 | With the identification fixed, the next wall was *"cabys must be exactly 13 digits"*. `GET /products` shows `cabys: null` on 9 of the first 10 rows for org `d09b299b` — CABYS is legally required on an electronic invoice, so those products cannot be billed at all. Two separate items: **(a)** the catalog data needs CABYS codes (not something to invent — it sets the tax rate); **(b)** the POS should refuse or warn when a product with no CABYS is added to an *electronic* document, instead of failing the whole sale at submit with a raw English backend string. |
| TSR-189 | Exact-cash amount carries a float artifact into the payment | **Not started** | QA audit 2026-09-08 | The *Exacto* button filled the cash field with `9261.401300000001` on a ₡9 261 total. The document total is rounded for display but the payment amount is not, so `payments_total` can disagree with `voucher_total` in the hundredth-of-a-colón — exactly the kind of drift `validate_payments_cover_total` exists to catch. Round the payment to the document's currency precision. |
| TSR-178 | `Select` silently drops `onFocus` — the POS station dropdown was permanently empty | **Done** (2026-09-08) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | `SelectProps` extends the native select attributes, so `onFocus` on a call site type-checks, but `components/ui/Input.tsx` only ever read `aria-*` out of `...rest` and never forwarded it to `SelectField`. `SessionSetupScreen` loads its branches in `onFocus`, so the handler never ran, the puesto list stayed empty, no shift could start and no document could be created — the same class of trap as the `Idempotency-Key` bug documented in `lib/api.ts`. `onFocus`/`onBlur` now forward through `Select` → `SelectField` → the trigger button. |
| TSR-179 | `product_id` typed `int` while product ids are UUIDs — every POS sale 422'd | **Done** (2026-09-08, deployed) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | `POST /sales` answered `422 int_parsing` on `details[].product_id` for every document the POS sent. `CommonDetailDTO.product_id` was `Optional[int]` while `SaleLine.product_id` is `String(255)` — the DTO disagreed with its own model, and the write path was papering over it with `str(...)`. The read path had the mirror bug (`int(...) if str(...).isdigit()`), so a UUID read back as `None`. Both fixed in `jbiller_common` and deployed. |
| TSR-180 | An untouched checkout sends `receiver: {}`, which beats the selected client | **Done (code)** (2026-09-08) | QA audit 2026-09-08 | After TSR-179, `POST /sales` answered "Receiver is required for document type '01'" **while the UI showed a selected receptor**. `buildReceiver` in `useCartFlow` treats `inbound` as authoritative when truthy, and an untouched checkout carries `{}` — truthy — so the `selectedClient` fallback was never reached and the payload went out with an empty receiver. Now the form's receiver only wins once it identifies somebody (name / email / identification number / foreign id). **This is very likely why documents #001–#004 were rejected by Hacienda.** |
| TSR-181 | Documents grid capped at two cards per row on desktop | **Done** (2026-09-08) | Owner request 2026-09-08 | `auto-fill` packed three cards into a 1280px viewport, which is too narrow for a 20-digit consecutivo plus a five-button action row. New `.grid-docs` keeps the auto-fill behaviour below 768px and pins two equal tracks above it. |
| TSR-182 | `useDefaultOrganization` resolves to a different org across reloads | **Not started** | QA audit 2026-09-08 | The same session loaded `/dashboard/documents` as *Corella Artavia Vilma* and, after a plain reload, as *Pacific Code Labs* — with the open document tabs (and their carts) carried over from the first org. A cashier's org must not change under them, and a cart built for one organization must not survive into another. |
| TSR-172 | Document detail page (`/dashboard/documents/:saleId`) with a Hacienda pipeline timeline | **Done** (2026-09-08) | Owner request 2026-09-08 | `fe/pos-system/src/pages/dashboard/DocumentDetailPage.tsx`, modeled on `OrderDetailPage`. Lines + totals, receiver, payments, references, receiver validation, notes, and a 5-stage timeline (signed → submitted → validated → PDF → notified) derived from the fields the backend writes per stage, so a stalled document shows exactly where it stopped. `DocumentCard` header and a new "Ver" action navigate to it. |
| TSR-237 | Branch + terminal moved onto the DOCUMENT; the shift-setup gate is gone | **Done** (2026-09-10) | Owner request 2026-09-10 | The POS hid its whole workspace — product grid included — behind a full-screen "elige puesto y terminal" form whenever the persisted store was empty. For the single-branch, single-terminal org (most of them) that is a question with exactly one possible answer, asked before anything can be done. `SessionSetupScreen` is **deleted**; `useSessionSelection` now resolves it automatically (assignment → still-valid current selection → first branch that actually has a terminal) and a new **Sucursal y terminal** card in the checkout drawer shows and overrides it *on the document it applies to*. The assignment is applied **once per assignment** (ref-guarded), so an assigned cashier covering another till for one document can say so instead of being snapped back. A branch with no terminal is a legitimate selection: the card offers "create a terminal here" rather than silently choosing elsewhere. `fe/pos-system`: `hooks/useBranches.ts`, `hooks/useSessionSelection.ts`, `components/pos/checkout/sections/BranchTerminalSection.tsx`. |
| TSR-238 | In-app notification centre — persisted, per-user, real-time (**closes TSR-128**) | **Done (code)** (2026-09-10) — **DB + AppSync + validator LIVE on dev** (2026-09-11); Lambda + gateway pending the CI image | Owner request 2026-09-10; supersedes TSR-128 | Two tables, not one: `app_notifications` holds the EVENT once, `user_notifications` holds one row per recipient with only their read state — a document rejection concerns the cashier who issued it *and* whoever has to fix it, and the cashier reading it must not clear the other's badge. (Named `app_*` because `notifications` was already taken by the outbound email/webhook dispatch audit.) New **`user-notifications` Lambda** in sales-be (feed + badge + mark-read/read-all, `x-user-id` pinned to the path user). Delivery is **AppSync Events**, zero polling: `UserNotificationService.create` persists *and* SigV4-publishes to `/notifications/{cognitoSub}` in one call, so every producer gets real-time for free; a Lambda subscribe-authorizer compares the channel's last segment to the caller's `sub`, so nobody can listen to anyone else. FE hydrates **once** and lives on pushes (`staleTime: Infinity`, reconnect → one re-read, dedupe by id). Publishing is best-effort by design — a push failure never rolls back the row. `cloudformation/appsync-events.yml`, `alembic/versions/l2m3n4o5p6q7_*`, `shared/jbiller_common/notifications/`, `fe/pos-system/src/hooks/use{UserNotifications,RealtimeNotifications}.ts`. **Next:** apply the migration, deploy `appsync-events.yml` + the Lambda, set `VITE_APPSYNC_EVENTS_URL`.  **Deployed 2026-09-11 (dev):** migration `l2m3n4o5p6q7` applied (both tables + all indexes, incl. the unique `(app_notification_id, user_id)` recipient constraint); stack `tsuru-dev-appsync-events` CREATE_COMPLETE — API `eabriwxvynhmhebiw5x752sldq`, Cognito subscribe / IAM publish, `onSubscribe` DIRECT → `SubscribeAuthorizer`, endpoints written to `/tsuru/dev/notifications/appsync/events/*`; `document-validator` redeployed and its role verified carrying `appsync:EventPublish` on `apis/eabriwxvynhmhebiw5x752sldq/*`. The authorizer was smoke-tested by direct invoke: own channel → `null` (allow), another user's channel → `Unauthorized: channel does not belong to caller`. **Still pending:** the `user-notifications` Lambda (waiting on the CI-built ECR image — push to build it) and the gateway regen that exposes its routes. |
| TSR-239 | Hacienda verdict reaches the person who issued the document | **Done (code)** (2026-09-10) | Owner request 2026-09-10 | A rejection nobody notices is a document that never gets corrected, and the only way to find out was to go and look at the documents list. `document-validator` now announces ACCEPTED / PARTIAL / REJECTED through TSR-238 the moment the verdict lands on the Sale — **not** in the notification lambda downstream, which waits for the PDF and skips documents it has already emailed (neither condition has anything to do with whether the issuer should be told). The body carries Hacienda's own rejection reasons, and clicking the notification opens `/dashboard/documents/{saleId}`, where they are already rendered. Deduped on (sale, event type) so the at-least-once queue and a later revalidation do not ring twice; the notification row shares the sale's transaction, so it cannot survive a rolled-back verdict. `shared/jbiller_common/notifications/document_status_notifier.py`. |
| TSR-240 | Business type stops gating features — every org gets the whole product | **Done (code)** (2026-09-10) — **supersedes the gating half of TSR-150** | Owner request 2026-09-10 | TSR-150 made `business_type` the *writer* of `organization_modules`, which drew the line in the wrong place: a bakery that starts taking table orders had to be reclassified before the tab appeared, and every vertical feature gained a second, invisible way to be broken — a correct implementation the org simply never saw. Now `ALL_ORG_MODULE_NAMES` (base + all nine verticals) is granted to every org, `syncBusinessTypeModules` only ever **adds and re-enables**, and `BUSINESS_TYPE_MODULES` is renamed `BUSINESS_TYPE_EMPHASIS` — it decides what the app *promotes*, never what it permits. Because the availability matrix that feeds the roles UI is filtered by enabled org modules, **roles are no longer limited by business type either**. The legitimate half of the old gate survives as a *preference*: `FeatureVisibilityFields` in org settings lets an org hide surfaces it does not use, per-org, reversible, and enforced nowhere but the UI. `management-be/src/seeds/rbac-seed.ts`, `services/OrganizationService.ts`, `seeds/org-modules-backfill.ts` (re-enables rows the old sync disabled); `fe/pos-system/src/hooks/useBusinessType.ts`, `store/orgFeatureVisibility.ts`. **Next:** re-run `db:seed:org-modules` per environment. |
| TSR-241 | Billing a pedido left the chain card's departamento and punto de entrega empty | **Done** (2026-09-10) | Owner report 2026-09-10 | Both selects are keyed by UUID, but an order carries CODES — the Excel import writes the chain's `department_code` and the delivery point's `gln`, never our row ids. So the prefill set `department_code`/`gln` and the selects, finding no id to match, rendered as if nothing had been chosen; only the purchase-order number (a plain text input) showed up. `ChainClientSection` now resolves the ids from the loaded lists once they arrive — department by `department_code` **or** `supplier_code` (the chain's paperwork calls it the provider code) and the store by `gln` then `store_code` — and writes the resolved id back into the form, because the document payload needs it too. |
| TSR-242 | Typing in an open select did nothing unless the list was long enough to show a search box | **Done** (2026-09-10) | Owner report 2026-09-10 | `SelectField` only rendered its filter box at ≥8 options; below that, typing ran a type-ahead that merely moved the highlight, so nothing on screen changed and the only way to search was to open a longer list and click into its box. Typing now filters everywhere and *reveals* the box so you can see what you typed; it stays revealed for the rest of that open (unmounting the input mid-typing drops focus to the body and the next keystroke goes nowhere). Text keys are handled only when they land on the trigger — the filter input owns its own caret. |

| TSR-251 | El desplegable de CABYS no aparecía en el formulario de producto — **renumbered from TSR-243 on 2026-09-11**: that ID had already been assigned on 2026-09-10 to the cold-image Lambda item and was referenced in 11 commits across data-be/sales-be/store-be, so first assignment kept it. Commits in `tsuru-pos-system` still carry `[TSR-243]` and refer to **this** row. | **Done** (2026-09-10) | Owner report 2026-09-10 | The search ran and the results were rendered — into a menu that painted *behind* the drawer. Both CABYS pickers (`products/sections/FiscalInformationSection.tsx` and `pos/line-detail/FiscalInfoSection.tsx`) portaled to `document.body` at `z-popover` (80) while the form's `Drawer` is `z-drawer` (200); body-level siblings, so the lower one is invisible. Exactly the failure `SelectField`'s doc comment already describes for the payment overflow menu. Both now use `OverlayPortal` at `z-drawer-modal` (210). They also switched from `absolute` + document coordinates to `fixed` + viewport coordinates with a capture-phase scroll listener: the input lives inside a drawer that scrolls independently of the document, so a position measured once at open time drifted away from its input as soon as the drawer body moved. |

| TSR-252 | `management-be/cloudformation/lambda.yml` ships a placeholder `Code` that silently overwrites the live API handler | **Mitigated — documented** (2026-09-11) | Found 2026-09-11 while applying TSR-168 retention | The template's `Code` is an inline `ZipFile` stub returning `"Placeholder - Deploy code with update-lambda.sh"`; the real bundle is shipped separately by `scripts/pipeline-update.sh` (`update-function-code`), so CloudFormation has no idea what is running. A plain `sam deploy`/`cloudformation deploy` against it rolls `tsuru-dev-api-handler` back to a stub that answers **200 with a placeholder body** — no error, no failed deploy, and nothing in the file warned of it. A prominent comment now does (`4318b7f`). The retention change was applied out of band with `put-retention-policy` for exactly this reason (verified: group at 30 days, function still 692 KB of real code). **Proper fix not done:** the template should reference a real S3 artifact, or the stack should be deployed only through a path that re-runs `pipeline-update.sh` immediately after. |
| TSR-250 | `sales-api` cold start exceeds the Lambda init budget — `Status: timeout` on every cold init | **Not started** | Observed 2026-09-11 while verifying TSR-168 | `INIT_REPORT Init Duration: 9999.46 ms / Phase: init / Status: timeout` — the init phase hits its ~10s ceiling, so Lambda abandons it and re-runs initialization inside the invoke (which then succeeds: `Duration: 3721.27 ms`). Not user-visible today because the retry works, but it makes every cold start pay initialization twice and leaves no headroom. **Not CPU-bound — tested and disproven 2026-09-11**: raising the function to 1769 MB (a full vCPU) left init at `9999.13 ms / timeout`, so more CPU does not help and the memory was reverted to 1024 MB. The surviving explanation is I/O, not compute: container image layers are fetched lazily on first access, so the first init pays a cold pull while the re-init inside the invoke reads what is already local — which is exactly the 10s → 3.7s gap observed. `consumer-cabys` (212 MB) sits at 8.6s on the same base image, i.e. the whole fleet runs close to the 10s ceiling and sales-api (223 MB) is simply the first over it. Real mitigations are image-size reduction or accepting the retry; keep-warm (TSR-243) already prevents most cold starts. Do not re-try the memory bump. |
| TSR-247 | Four sales-be Lambdas were dead on arrival — every invoke returned `Runtime.ImportModuleError` | **Done — deployed** (2026-09-11) | Warm-ping testing, TSR-243 | `document-validator` (`No module named 'pydantic_xml'`), `document-notification` and `document-pdf-generator` (`redis`), `hacienda-history` (`fastapi`) — the handler never ran, which is why three of those log groups had never recorded a line. One cause: their Dockerfiles `COPY --from=shared jbiller_common`, which brings the shared library's **code** but installs none of its **dependencies**; `configuration/__init__.py` eagerly imports `fast_api_config`/`redis_config`, so importing it for any reason drags in fastapi and redis. `shared/pyproject.toml` already declares all 18, so the fix was to pip-install the library as a package — the pattern `ab438bf` had already applied to `user-notifications` — rather than patching package names into four `requirements.txt` files and waiting for the next transitive import (`5fec868`). |
| TSR-248 | `hacienda-history` has never started since the 2026 shared-layer migration | **Done — deployed** (2026-09-11) | Warm-ping testing, TSR-243 | Once TSR-247 cleared the dependency error, three further layers surfaced, all leftovers of `ebfe30a` ("Hacienda History Integration into shared layer"), which moved the service under `src/` and into `jbiller_common` but left references to what it moved: `exceptions/taxpayer_dao_exception.py` and `token_dao_exception.py` were dropped while `document_history_controller.py` kept importing both (restored **verbatim** from `ebfe30a~1` so the 503 codes and `IvoisException` base match the `except` clause); `models/sqs_message_error_logs.py` imported `models.base`, which never existed at that path; and four call sites still imported the old local `configuration` package (`6e231f6`, `86cf536`). It now answers the warm ping. **Consequence beyond keep-warm:** this is the consumer for `document-history.fifo`, so the history sweep could not have been running — relevant to TSR-167. |
| TSR-249 | Deploy scripts called `py -3`, the Windows Python launcher, and failed silently on macOS/Linux | **Done** (2026-09-11) | TSR-243 rollout | Ten occurrences across six scripts in data-be, sales-be and store-be. The venv bootstrap ran unconditionally, printed "Installing aws-sam-cli...", installed nothing, and the deploy died on the next line with no error explaining why — which is what broke `store-be/deploys/deploy-lambda.sh` mid-rollout. Scripts now prefer a `sam` already on PATH (installed globally via `brew install aws-sam-cli`, 1.166.1), fall back to a venv only if absent, and fail loudly with the install command otherwise (`be3f7f4`, `a387397`, `c06fdac`). |
| TSR-244 | `gen_api_template.py` drops a service's routes instead of failing when its spec is missing | **Done** (2026-09-11) | Found while running the TSR-238 deploy locally | **Not a risk on the normal path** — the gateway is regenerated by CI on push, where `deploy.yml` installs every `app/*/requirements.txt` and assumes an OIDC role, so all specs build and nothing is lost. The hole is what happens when one spec *doesn't* build: the template is the WHOLE gateway (every route redeclared each deploy), and a service missing from `all_paths` had its routes silently removed, with exit code 0. Three ways in: a failed import printed `[FAIL]` and was then skipped by an `os.path.exists` test; `--skip-refresh` with no spec on disk did the same; and `deployed_lambdas()` cannot tell "not deployed yet" from "credentials expired". A local run without `pydantic_xml` emitted a 1-path template, which is how this was noticed. The case that still matters in CI is narrow but real: the requirements loop ends in `\|\| true`, so a failed install is swallowed, and a commit that breaks one service's import would drop that service's routes rather than fail the build. Each path now aborts naming its cause, plus a final guard that refuses to write a zero-path template; `--allow-partial` is the deliberate escape hatch. |

| TSR-245 | Events API gets a stable hostname, and the POS build reads it from SSM | **Done (code)** (2026-09-11) | Owner request 2026-09-11 | The generated Events endpoint is a 26-character hash, and it is compiled INTO the POS bundle — so recreating the API would strand every client until a rebuild. `cloudformation/appsync-events.yml` now also owns an ACM certificate (DNS-validated through the hosted zone, so the stack is self-contained), an `AWS::AppSync::DomainName` + `DomainNameApiAssociation`, and a Route53 A-alias, giving `https://events.tsuru.jcampos.dev/event` and `wss://events.tsuru.jcampos.dev/event/realtime` on one host — the whole thing is conditional on `CustomDomainName`/`HostedZoneId`, so the stack still deploys without it. Verified that Amplify handles it: `getRealtimeEndpointUrl` treats a non-generated host as a custom domain and appends `/realtime` rather than doing the `appsync-api`→`appsync-realtime-api` substitution, so the frontend needs the HTTP URL and nothing else. The **backend publisher stays on the generated endpoint** — SigV4 straight at AppSync with no CloudFront hop — so the two roles get two parameters: `…/events/endpoint` (publish) and the new `…/events/public-endpoint` (browsers). For the POS build the value lands in the frontend config namespace as `/tsuru/{env}/platform/appsync/events-url` (`cloudformation/params.yml`), and the POS workflow now READS it over OIDC instead of carrying a repo variable — soft-failing to a build without live notifications rather than blocking a release. Note `cloudformation/params-pos-system.yml` is dead: it writes a pre-rebrand `pollos-sales` namespace that exists in no environment.  **Deploy note (2026-09-11):** the first attempt rolled back with a bare *"Certificate validation failed with status: FAILED"* after two minutes — too fast for a DNS timeout. Cause: `tsuru.jcampos.dev` is a CNAME to `chepelcr.github.io`, and RFC 8659 has the CAA lookup walk UP the tree *following CNAMEs*, so the authority consulted for a new `*.tsuru.jcampos.dev` name is GitHub's — sectigo/digicert/letsencrypt, no Amazon. A CAA set that names any issuer excludes every issuer it omits, so ACM read it as "Amazon may not issue here". Every AWS-certified name already in this zone carries its own explicit CAA record for exactly this reason; the template now creates one (`EventsCaaRecord`, `DependsOn` from the certificate so the record exists before ACM checks it).  **LIVE on dev (2026-09-11):** certificate ISSUED, `events.tsuru.jcampos.dev` resolves to CloudFront, TLS verifies, and an unauthenticated publish returns 401 — the endpoint is up and enforcing auth. `/tsuru/dev/platform/appsync/events-url` = `https://events.tsuru.jcampos.dev/event` (published via the corrected `deploy-params.sh` stack name; the other 13 platform parameters verified unchanged). |
| TSR-246 | `deploy-params.sh` pointed at a stack name that does not exist | **Done** (2026-09-11) | Found while wiring TSR-245 | It hardcoded `tsuru-{env}-jmarkets-ssm-params` while the live stack is `tsuru-{env}-platform-ssm-params`. Running it would not have updated anything — it would have tried to CREATE a second stack over parameter names the first already owns, failed on the conflict and rolled back. Same shape as TSR-244: a script that is confidently wrong about the thing it manages. Name corrected and `--events-api-url` added. |


| TSR-253 | Rewrite hacienda-history onto the shared stack with a single first-run/on-demand sweep owner | **Done — deployed** (2026-09-12) | Owner execution brief 2026-09-11 | The ivois-era internals are gone (own DAO, ad-hoc token object, marshmallow schema, `IvoisException` tree). Identity now comes from `OrganizationAggregateRepository`, the token from a local `OrganizationTokenResolver`, the fetch from `HaciendaAtvClient`. The clave parser and the branch→terminal→max-consecutive grouping were kept. **Hacienda's `emisor` is type + number padded to twelve** (`01` + `000102440077`); the bare number returns 403, so the sweep could never have worked even after the `"PLACEHOLDER"` was replaced. Three things only a live run found: (1) `deploys/deploy-params.sh` still named the stack `jcampos-{env}-hacienda-history-ssm-params`, a leftover of the `/jcampos`→`/tsuru` migration, so the stack was never created and `/tsuru/dev/hacienda-history` was **empty** — the sweep could not resolve database credentials at all; (2) Hacienda answers **HTTP 500, not an empty list**, for an offset past the end of a history, and with 100 documents at page size 50 the short-page stop never fires — a sweep that had already persisted 100 rows lost the whole record to the DLQ (this hits any taxpayer whose count is an exact multiple of the page size, not just the sandbox); (3) the consumer built its `BatchProcessor` **without a logger**, so the failure produced START/END and a DLQ message with no traceback — indistinguishable from never running. All three fixed and pinned by tests. |
| TSR-254 | Idempotent Hacienda branch, terminal and consecutive sync into store-be | **Done — deployed** (2026-09-12) | Owner execution brief 2026-09-11 | New SQS consumer on `organization-branches-queue.fifo`; `app/main.py` dispatches `Records` ahead of Mangum. Branches and terminals use `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` on `(organization_id, code)` so operator edits survive every later sweep; only the counter moves, via `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE … GREATEST`, which takes the same row write lock as the sales allocator's `SELECT FOR UPDATE` and is evaluated after waiting for it — proven by 13 tests against real PostgreSQL, both interleavings. `consecutives.current_number` → **BIGINT**: the numeral ends in a ten-digit serial, so it must reach 9 999 999 999 and int4 stops at 2 147 483 647. `branches.type` stopped being `Literal['stand','restaurant']` — TSR-139 replaced those with a per-org catalog and dropped the CHECK, leaving a discovered branch un-saveable through the same API it came from; now validated against the org's `branch_types`. **Caused a dev outage:** `Optional[str | int]` in the new DTO is unevaluable on the `python:3.9` Lambda image, and because `app/main.py` imports the handler at module scope the failed import returned `Runtime.Unknown` for **every** invocation, the HTTP API included. Fixed with `typing.Union` plus an AST test that fails on a PEP 604 union in any runtime-evaluated annotation and self-deletes when the image moves to 3.10+. **Terminal codes are unique per BRANCH, not per organization (found 2026-09-12 by the live run, fixed same day).** `uq_terminals_org_code` rejected branch 14/terminal 1 for an org that already had branch 1/terminal 1 — both legal, since Hacienda's consecutive is branch(3)+terminal(5) and the pair identifies the point of sale. The sync was right to refuse reassigning a terminal and roll back; the constraint was wrong, and it had been hiding **all three** of this taxpayer's real branches. store-be's own routes already assumed the looser rule (`/branches/{branch_code}/terminals/{terminal_code}`). Migration `c2d3e4f5a6b7` swaps it for `(organization_id, branch_id, code)` — tolerating either the `UniqueConstraint` or the differently-named unique `Index` the model had diverged into — and the downgrade **refuses** when two branches already share a code, because re-numbering or deleting a terminal would break the fiscal consecutive it owns. `insert_from_history` conflicts on the three-column target and drops its cross-branch guard; create/update uniqueness checks are branch-scoped; `find_by_code_and_organization` returns the first match rather than raising MultipleResultsFound on exactly the data this permits. sales-be's read-only mirror updated to match. **Verified live:** branches 12/14/115 discovered alongside the operator's 1 and 2 (whose names, types and phones were untouched), terminals 12/2, 14/1 and 115/47 coexisting, six per-branch-per-type counters, and branch 1's existing 237 unmoved. |
| TSR-255 | Persist historical documents and expose an organization-scoped read/sync API | **Done — deployed** (2026-09-12) | Owner execution brief 2026-09-11 | `hacienda_historical_documents`, unique on `(organization_id, clave)`, written only through `upsert_by_clave`: nulls are stripped before the statement is built, every retained key is `coalesce`d, a signed verdict is never overwritten by a later skeleton, and `source=POS` is sticky. The verdict mapping already existed and was never read — `HaciendaValidationService.fetch` populates `parsed_message` with totals, issuer, receiver and parsed errors, and `ValidatorPipeline` was using only the error list. `validate_historical` deliberately skips `_dispatch_next` (a historical document has no Sale and no XML, so GENERATE_PDF would fail). The POS-side publish was moved **outside** `_persistence_scope` and made best-effort: `DatabaseConnection.__exit__` rolls back on any exception, so publishing inside it let an SNS outage discard the Sale verdict, the audit row, the attachment and the bell, and skip GENERATE_PDF. The test that claimed to cover that stubbed the scope with `nullcontext`, so it passed while the code had the opposite property. **Live proof on `d09b299b` (VILMA CORELLA ARTAVIA):** 100 documents swept from 2024, 97 type 01 + 3 type 04, every row carrying an ATV verdict and a non-null total with real issuer/receiver names, and `consecutives.current_number` still 237 — never rewound. Routes answer 401 through API Gateway while an unknown path answers 403, i.e. wired and Cognito-protected. **The browser still saw nothing (found 2026-09-12, fixed same day).** The card stayed empty and DevTools showed a red 200: `hacienda-history` never added `CORSMiddleware`, and API Gateway's `aws_proxy` integration adds CORS headers to its **own** OPTIONS answer but not to a real response, so the preflight passed, the GET returned 200 with real rows, and the browser discarded the body — a failure that is invisible to curl, to the Lambda logs, and to the status code. The per-app `FastApiConfig._configure_cors` was one hardcoded origin list copied per service and already drifting, so the policy moved to `jbiller_common/configuration/cors_config.py` (one allowlist plus a `*.jcampos.dev` regex) and both `hacienda-history` and `document-validator` now call `configure_cors(app)` — the same config the rest of the stack uses, in one place, rather than a fifth copy. |
| TSR-256 | Remove legacy third-party branding, including the PDF footer's third-party fiscal identification | **Done** (2026-09-12) | Owner execution brief 2026-09-11 | The footer carried `Comprobante generado por IVOIS (3-101-512496)`, `www.ivois.io` and their logo on **every emitted fiscal document** — another company's cédula jurídica. Now `Comprobante generado por Tsuru (1-1664-0506)` with `tsuru.jcampos.dev` and the Tsuru wordmark; the DGT-R-033-2019 notice and the QR are untouched. Also removed: the `IvoisException` tree, `IVOIS-*` app names in `settings.cfg`, two dead `build-lambda.sh` scripts pointing at `IVOIS-*` profiles and `ivois-*` stacks, and two stale code comments. `command grep -ril ivois` is now clean outside `docs/audit/` (a historical record, deliberately untouched), gitignored `tests/local/out/`, and the tracking docs. |
| TSR-257 | POS historical-documents module and reports/historical RBAC grants | **Done — deployed** (2026-09-12) | Owner execution brief 2026-09-11 | A sibling of Documentos, not a tab inside it — historical documents have no lines, no PDF, and are not necessarily ours. Three gating layers with their three different failure modes preserved (nav fail-open, route fail-closed, in-page fail-open). The empty state distinguishes "no documents" from "never synced" off `historical_requested`, which matters because **every existing org is in the second state** — the flag is already true, so only a forced re-run reaches them. Sync is deliberately not disabled when the list query fails: a cold Lambda or expired credentials is exactly when a sweep is needed. Search debounced at 500 ms (`filters` is part of the query key, so every keystroke was a request). RBAC: `documents/historical` with read/export/update, and **no `role_permissions` backfill** — `owner` short-circuits on `isOwner`, `admin`/`manager` hold module-wide `documents` grants that `resolveEffectivePermissions` re-expands over every available submodule, and `organization_submodules` is override-only so a new submodule is available to every org without a row. **Relocated to `reports/historical` on 2026-09-12**, before anyone depended on the old pair: the ledger reads as a report — Hacienda's read-only record of this taxpayer, with no lines, no PDF and nothing to compose — so it became an item of the Reportes section rather than a sibling of Documentos, and `ReportePage` gained a summary card (total, the four ATV verdicts, distribution by type). The move is not a rename, and it broke RBAC in **both** directions: `staff` held a module-wide `reports: ['read']` row, and module-wide grants re-expand across every available submodule on each resolve, so the instant `historical` appeared under `reports` every cashier could read the whole taxpayer history; meanwhile `admin` silently **lost** `update` — the sweep trigger — because module-wide `reports` is read/export only where the old module-wide `documents` grant happened to include update. Staff is now two explicit rows (`reports/general`, `reports/iva`) and admin holds `reports/historical: ['update']` per-submodule. That is the general hazard of this catalog shape and is now written down in both CLAUDE.mds: adding a submodule grants it to every role holding its module wide, and moving one revokes whatever the old module carried. Also added `reports/iva`, which had been in the sidebar for a while but never in the catalog. The card's counts are exact whole-history totals (one `size=1` request per bucket, read off `total_elements`) rather than an aggregate of whichever page the list is showing, which would have described 20 rows as the ledger; the four verdicts are labelled icon tiles because the design system's status ramp does not separate warning from success under protanopia (dE 3.9 measured), so colour cannot be what identifies a verdict. Verified live after the move: platform_admin/owner/admin resolve read+export, admin also update, manager read, staff none. A raw-SQL backfill that shipped with it was **removed** — it was redundant for owner/admin and its one real effect was granting `manager` an `export` it holds nowhere else under `documents`, recorded only inside a SQL string; its 9 rows were deleted from dev so the database matches `rolePermissionMatrix`. The assumption is now pinned by tests rather than a comment. |
| TSR-258 | **The rate code was being stripped on save, which made every order imported afterwards unbillable** | **Done — FE + store-be, 49 new tests** (2026-09-12) | `fe/pos-system/src/lib/productFormMapping.ts`; `src/services/storedTaxToLineTax.ts`; `src/services/ivaRateCode.ts`; `be/store-be/app/dtos/requests/{product,manual_order}_request_dto.py` | Reported as `tax.rate_code is required when tax.code='01'` on billing a pedido. The cause was not in the order path at all: **`ProductDetailPage` and `ProductsPage` each carried their own copy of the product-save mapping**, and the detail page's dropped `tax_rate.code`, emitted no `tax_rate` whatsoever unless the catalog `id` happened to be set (a CABYS-derived entry has a code and often no id), and also dropped `unit_measure`, `commercial_unit_measure`, `customs_part`, `base_amount` and `special_fields.proportion`. Editing any product from its detail page was a data-loss operation, and `_imported_line_taxes` copies the product's taxes verbatim, so the damage surfaced much later on an unrelated screen. For the IVA family the rate code is not decoration — sales-api derives the percentage from the code alone and ignores `rate` — so a line with a rate and no code cannot be priced at all. Both directions of the mapping are now one function (`productFormFromProduct` / `productSavePayload`); the loader had the mirror defect, dropping `proportion` on read. Separately the order→invoice and scan-and-charge paths were two more copies of the stored-tax→document-tax translation, and they had drifted: the order path had **no `rate_code` fallback**, neither carried the code-08 `factor` (so IVARBU priced at zero and was rejected), and both mishandled `special_fields` — the stored side nests the per-unit amount as `tax_amount: {id, amount}` while the document wants flat `tax_amount_id`/`tax_unit_amount`, and an `as never` cast hid it, so **every specific excise reached the engine with no unit amount**. One `lineTaxesFromStored` now owns it. The order path also silently defaulted a missing tax type to `01` (turning an excise line into an IVA line) and a missing discount nature to `07` (un-assuming the issuer's IVA); both now DROP the row, which surfaces as a totals mismatch instead of a misdeclaration. Guardrail per owner decision: `ProductTaxDTO` and `ManualOrderTaxDTO` now **reject** an IVA-family tax with no rate code, reject an invalid `rate_code`, require the 08 factor, and apply the per-code `special_fields` matrix — failing where the data is entered rather than where it is filed. The dead `ManualOrderTaxDTO.base`/`.factory_assumed` (accepted and never read) were removed. |
| TSR-259 | The fiscal document carried LESS than the non-fiscal pedido | **Done — FE** (2026-09-12) | `fe/pos-system/src/hooks/useCartFlow.ts` | The electronic-document `details[]` was an 8-field subset of `LineDetail`, while the manual-order payload — which is not a fiscal document — sent the full set and sales-be (`CommonDetailDTO`/`DetailDTO`) accepted it all along. Dropped on **every** document: `base_amount` (the IVACE manual base the cashier set, so the till and the comprobante differed by exactly the tax), `iva_collected_factory` (Hacienda answers **-451** when a line declares it and the issuer does not then absorb the IVA — and it is also what makes `base_amount` legal), `codes` (the line's Nota-6 codes, which is what a retail chain reconciles against), `commercial_unit_measure`, `customs_part`, `product_type`, `factory_tax`. Both were already proven live in TSR-222, 2/2 on signed XML — the POS simply never sent them. `unit_measure` also stopped skipping the product's own unit before falling back to `"Unid"`, which had been filing anything sold by weight as a discrete unit. |
| TSR-260 | Reprocess could not repair the orders that most needed it | **Done — store-be + FE, 13 tests** (2026-09-12) | `be/store-be/app/services/order_service.py`; `app/repositories/product_repository.py`; `app/utils/product_fiscal_defaults.py`; `fe/pos-system/src/components/orders/ReprocessDialog.tsx` | Phase 2 of `reprocess_order` re-derived **money only**, and recomputing money over a line with no tax structure is a no-op — so the fiscal repair only happened on the phase-1 path, which needs the original spreadsheet still in S3. A storefront order, a manual one, or any order whose file was deleted could never be fixed. `refill_line_fiscal_fields` now fills each line's MISSING fields from its product (cabys, taxes, unit, codes, net price, IVACobradoFabrica — never overwriting what the line has) and returns what it changed, so a repair says what it did instead of reporting a silent success. The logic was extracted from `backfill_order_line_calculations.py`, where it had drifted: that copy quantized the header-discount allocation at **5 decimals** where the service rounds at 2, so a backfilled order's line discounts did not add back to its header at the precision order money is stored in; both now call `allocate_order_header_discount`. The recompute phase's bare warning-only `except` became an ERROR that re-raises — it had made the most consequential phase silently optional, returning 200 on an order that had not been repaired. Two upstream causes fixed: **storefront orders were created with no fiscal data at all** (and because `_recompute_imported_line` returns early on a line with no structured detail, they were also permanently unrepairable), and `upsert_by_internal_code` created import-products with no unit and no taxes, which is how 26 orders came to report `taxes = 0`; both now seed from `app/utils/product_fiscal_defaults`, shared with the backfill script. On the POS the Reprocess dialog offered only a colour picker, so the action read as "recolour the report"; the fiscal repair is now stated. `OrderDetailPage` also showed no CABYS, unit or tax column, so a line missing fiscal data looked normal until the document was filed — the three the biller treats as mandatory are now shown, with the gaps named. |
| TSR-261 | `/xml/regenerate` rejected every ordinary invoice | **Done — sales-be** (2026-09-12) | `be/sales-be/shared/jbiller_common/services/sale_calculation_service.py` | `_recompute_line` backfilled `line.base_amount` from the subtotal on **every** line. But that column means "the issuer EDITED the taxable base", legal in exactly two cases (tax code 07, `IVACobradoFabrica` 01) and rejected anywhere else with `HACIENDA_BASE_AMOUNT_NOT_EDITABLE` — so it turned every ordinary line into an apparently-edited one. The first emission passed because validation is pipeline step 1 and this runs at step 2; regenerate reloads the Sale through `sale_to_dto` and validates it again, so no ordinary invoice could be re-emitted. Nothing needed the backfill: `TaxService.editable_base_for` already returns the derived base when `base_amount` is None, which is precisely the not-edited case. Proved with a layer-1 probe reproducing the stored row. Two consistency items in the same flow: `line.total_amount` was the post-discount subtotal here and the gross in `_compute_lines`, while it is the XML's `MontoTotal` (pre-discount, since the element order is MontoTotal → MontoDescuento → SubTotal) — anything reading the stored line was a discount short; and `_BASE_IMPONIBLE_EXCISES` was declared twice with complementary halves of the same explanation. |
| TSR-262 | Data repair for already-poisoned products and orders | **Done — applied to dev** (2026-09-13) | `be/store-be/scripts/backfill_product_fiscal_defaults.py`; `scripts/backfill_order_line_calculations.py` | Ran against dev with `PACIFIC-PROD`. **Products: 5 repaired** — all carried a 13% IVA row with a null `tax_rate.code`, exactly the population TSR-258's broken save path created; all resolved unambiguously to code 08, none needed manual attention. **Orders: 44 repaired** — missing rate codes on the lines' own stored tax rows plus missing CABYS. Every order total was unchanged, which is the point: the money had always been right, what was missing was the fiscal identity that makes the document filable. Both scripts re-run clean (0 changes), so they are idempotent. **The first attempt failed on 16 orders**, and the failure was the new guardrail working: order LINES had copied the null rate code from the products, so `ProductTaxDTO` rejected them during recompute and the repair could not parse the very orders it exists to fix. `refill_line_fiscal_fields` now repairs a line's EXISTING tax rows as well as filling absent ones — the same derivation, refusing to guess a 0% code because exento (10), no sujeto (11) and crédito pleno (01) are all 0%. `repair_tax_rows` in `app/utils/product_fiscal_defaults.py` is the single owner, shared by the product backfill, the order backfill and Reprocess. 14 unit tests. |
| TSR-263 | **A billed order never recorded its invoice, so it stayed billable** | **Done — FE** (2026-09-13) | Browser test of orders 2900679388 / 7026682727 | Found by billing a pedido through the UI rather than through a test harness — the document was issued correctly, and `crossdocking_orders.invoice_sale_id` stayed null afterwards. `POST /orders/{doc}/invoice` has existed since TSR-152 and its own OpenAPI description says why: *"without this link the frontend cannot tell that a pedido is already invoiced, so nothing prevents a second factura for the same order"*. **Nothing on the FE ever called it.** `isOrderInvoiced` therefore kept returning false, *Facturar pedido* stayed enabled, and a second click would have emitted a second real document and burned another consecutive for the same delivery. `OrderCheckoutDrawer` now links after a **confirmed** sale only — a queued one has no consecutive yet and the outbox replay is what produces it, so linking would record an invoice that does not exist. The link failing is deliberately non-fatal: the document is issued and legally exists by then, so failing the checkout would tell the cashier the sale did not happen when it did; the failure is surfaced instead, because its cost is that the order still looks billable. Order 2900679388 — billed before the fix — was linked by hand to the document it had already produced. |
| TSR-264 | **No manual order could ever be created from a browser** | **Done — store-be gateway + FE** (2026-09-13) | `be/store-be/scripts/gen_api_template.py` (`CORS_ALLOW_HEADERS`); `fe/pos-system/src/components/pos/checkout/sections/ManualOrderSection.tsx` | Found the same way TSR-263 was — by driving the real POS instead of a harness. Registering a pedido reported *"Pedido en cola — se enviará cuando vuelva la conexión"* and filed it in the offline outbox, against a backend that was up the whole time: `navigator.onLine` was **true**, the host answered curl in 0.45s, and the order simply never existed server-side. The POST carries `Idempotency-Key` (the outbox id, so an offline pedido replays as one order and not several), which makes it a preflighted request — and this gateway's `Access-Control-Allow-Headers` did not list that header. The preflight failed, `fetch` rejected with a bare `TypeError`, and the POS cannot distinguish a blocked preflight from an unplugged cable, so it classified a permanent failure as a temporary one and retried forever. **Nothing surfaced an error at any layer.** sales-be's gateway has carried the header since it was written, which is why billing worked in the very session manual orders were silently failing — the two gateways are generated by different scripts and had drifted. The allow list is now one `CORS_ALLOW_HEADERS` constant, because it is emitted twice (per-path OPTIONS mock; CORS gateway responses) and a header allowed by one but not the other fails the preflight on exactly the error responses that matter. Second defect on the same screen: *Registrar pedido* answered *"Indicá un punto de entrega o una dirección"* about a delivery card that visibly showed the client's address — the card DISPLAYS `receiverAddress` while what is saved is `delivery_location`, and only `selectMode` copied one into the other. Two paths into receiver mode skip it: the automatic fallback when a client has no registered stores (the common case, wrong on first render), and changing client while already in receiver mode — which left the PREVIOUS client's address in the payload, the worse of the two because validation then passes. Both now build through one `receiverLocation` helper. |
| TSR-265 | **Goods vs services was classified by the unit of measure, not the CABYS** | **Done — sales-be** (2026-09-14) | `be/sales-be/shared/jbiller_common/hacienda/services/summary_service.py`; probe matrix (80 probes) | A Factura carrying a health-services line came back REJECTED with **-110** (*"el resumen carece del monto total de servicios gravados, pero cuenta con servicios gravados"*) and **-111** (*"el monto total de mercancias gravadas no coincide … (18000.00)"*). `_classify_kind` read the unit of measure — `Sp`/`Spe`/`Os` are services, everything else goods — so CABYS `9319999000000` ("Servicios de salud humana") sold by `Unid` was filed as merchandise: its 20 000 inflated `TotalMercanciasGravadas` to 38 000 and `TotalServGravados` was omitted from a document that had services in it. The spec says otherwise and **the function's own docstring cited the section while implementing a different rule** — analysis doc, "Classification between Services and Goods": the issuer must inspect the **first digit of the CodigoCABYS**, services {5,6,7,8,9}, goods {0,1,2,3,4}. Hacienda settled it independently: the only service signal on that line was its CABYS, and Hacienda saw a service. These are the same digit sets data-be uses to type a CABYS row (`CabysProductType.from_code`), so the catalog that types a product and the summary that files its amount now agree. The unit of measure remains a fallback for a line with no CABYS. Proved by re-emitting the identical document: **…0001 Rechazado / …0002 Aceptado**, same ₡47 230 total, `TotalServGravados` 20 000 + `TotalMercanciasGravadas` 18 000 = the same `TotalGravado` 38 000. |
| TSR-266 | **One wire language: snake_case across every service** | **Done — sales-be, store-be, data-be, POS** (2026-09-14) | `*/utils/response_utils.py`; `fe/pos-system/src/types/orgConfigurations.ts`, `lib/media.ts`, `hooks/{useSales,useHistoricalDocuments}.ts` | The API answered in a different language than it was asked in: requests and DTO field names were snake_case, responses came back camelCase, and the POS carried a `snakeCaseResponses` converter whose only job was undoing it. **sales-be**: 330 of our own aliases removed (284 `alias=`, 46 serialization/validation, 1 `alias_generator`), selected from the AST and only where the alias was a pure case-transformation of its own field — which is what separates ours from Hacienda's. **store-be**: `contentType` on the three file DTOs was the only camelCase it actually emitted; 10 dormant aliases on the inbound branch contract went with it, plus `isOffer` and swagger prose advertising filter names the enum no longer accepted. **data-be**: one flag in the same shared helper, 12 real fields, verified by regenerating all 34 specs — **zero camelCase domain properties remain**. **POS**: the media upload was outright broken by the change (`fileName`/`uploadUrl` no longer exist); `dtos/common.ts` was deleted as a `@deprecated`, unreferenced file that declared `createdOn`/`countryCode` for an API that has always answered snake_case. **Kept deliberately everywhere**: Hacienda's own ATV and Spanish API field names, HTTP header names, and `_type` — a discriminator that cannot be a pydantic attribute. Events move with it and their consumers accept both spellings so in-flight messages are not dropped; the SAVE_BRANCHES cross-repo fixture was regenerated on both sides. |
| TSR-267 | **Fiscal test matrix emitted end to end from the POS** | **Done — Pacific Code Labs, 3 documents ACCEPTED** (2026-09-14) | Consecutives …0002 / …0003 / …0004, signed XML | Ten products isolating one fiscal variable each, billed as three documents so a rejection would name its own cause. **Doc A** — five IVA rates in one document (13/4/2/1/0 → codes 08/04/03/02/10) with a service line; proved TSR-265. **Doc B** — the discount cascade: line 2 carries `07` at 2000 **and `04` at 900**, because the second discount applies to the 18 000 the first left behind, not to the original 20 000 — a summed 15% would have produced 3000/17000. Line 3 carries 3.17% at full precision (71.5152), the rate that was being displayed as 3.2%. **Doc C** — the two special routings: the regalía line has `MontoDescuento` 15000, subtotal 0, `ImpuestoNeto` 0 and **`ImpuestoAsumidoEmisorFabrica` 1950**, exactly Note 20; the `IVACobradoFabrica` line prices normally beside it. The run also caught a real guard doing its job **before a consecutive was burned**: a 20% regalía was rejected at validation because Nota 20 makes codes 01/03 free goods — the discount must equal 100% of `MontoTotal`. |
| TSR-268 | **Product saves were not judged by the rules that judge their documents** | **Done — store-be + FE, 29 tests** (2026-09-14) | `be/store-be/app/dtos/requests/product_request_dto.py`; `tests/unit/dtos/test_product_fiscal_validation.py`; `fe/pos-system/src/components/products/sections/IvaTaxSection.tsx` | A product is the template every document line is built from, so a fiscal rule enforced only at emission is enforced far too late: the save succeeds, the catalogue looks right, and the failure lands on the first invoice — against a consecutive, naming a field the cashier never filled in. Found by doing it: a product saved with a **20% regalía** was refused at emission because Nota 20 makes codes 01/03 *free goods* (Hacienda **-518**, the discount must be 100% of MontoTotal). Four sales-be rules had no save-time counterpart — `tax_type_id` unchecked against the enum; code 08 (IVA Bienes Usados) missing its factor, which makes `tax = subtotal x factor` **zero** silently on the document; transitional rates 05/06/07, which sales-be allows only on credit/debit notes and a product is not a document at all; and the two set-level rules — one IVA per line, and free goods at 100% **following the cascade** (50% then 100% of the remainder is valid, 50% then 50% is not). The FE now reports the two IVA rules inline so they read as guidance beside the field rather than as a 422 after Save. **Deliberately NOT required: ISEBA's `proportion`** — the existing tests caught that addition and were right, since `TaxCalculationService` derives it as `quantity x percentage / 100`; the product supplies the inputs and only the line can produce the output. **Every guard is conditional on the fiscal data being present**: a product with no taxes, no discounts and no CABYS still saves, because an org that is not registered with Hacienda still needs a catalogue — pinned by `TestNonFiscalProducts` so nobody tightens one later and breaks the orders-only path. |
| TSR-269 | **Six payloads parsed as raw dicts, one of them into a DB write** | **Done — sales-be, 45 tests** (2026-09-14) | `shared/jbiller_common/hacienda/dtos/responses/hacienda_history_document.py`; `dtos/requests/{document_search_dto,fire_callback_dto}.py`; `hacienda/dtos/events/document_history_event.py`; `app/lambda-authorizer/src/models/auth_context.py` | **F6** — Hacienda's `/comprobantes` response was walked with `.get()` across five Spanish keys, reshaped into an ad-hoc dict and `**`-splatted into `upsert_by_clave`: the dict's keys WERE the repository's kwargs, an unenforced contract between an external upstream and a database write. A key rename surfaced as a `TypeError` mid-transaction in a Lambda whose failure mode is a DLQ'd sweep. Now one malformed row costs that row, not the page; an unparseable date loses the date, not the document; and a metadata object still cannot become an empty page, which is what stops a sweep. **F4/F5** — the `search` blob: the sales list **silently returned an unfiltered page** on malformed JSON, which is worse than a 500 because nothing said the filter had been dropped; the historical list hand-wrote ~20 isinstance checks to do the same job correctly. One DTO, 400 on both. Its **date-vs-datetime distinction is load-bearing** — a bare `date` end-bound means the whole day, so collapsing it would have silently excluded a day of results; the existing test caught that when I first collapsed it. **F2** — the sweep request existed in three incompatible forms plus a fourth parser, and its `offset` continuation cursor was emitted only when truthy. **F1** — the one untyped body in the repo was an SSRF-shaped callback URL handed to the HTTP client; now `HttpUrl` + an http/https scheme check. **F3** — the authorization path's three dicts mixed `[...]` and `.get()`, so a missing key was a `KeyError` → **500 where a deny belonged**. |
| TSR-309 | **Every auth page swallowed every Cognito error — registration failed silently** | **Done (code)** (2026-09-19) | Owner report 2026-09-19 (tester could not register) | A tester tried to create an account three times and saw nothing at all; the responses carried `UsernameExistsException` / "User already exists" (his first attempt had left an UNCONFIRMED user — see TSR-308). Two defects in the same four lines repeated on all five auth pages: (1) the error went to `add()`, i.e. the notification **bell**, which is rendered by `DashboardHeader` — `AuthLayout` has none, so on /login, /register, /verify-email, /forgot-password and /reset-password it went nowhere; (2) `bodyKey` is an i18n **key**, and they passed the raw provider string, so even where a bell exists it would render untranslated English. Fixed in `fe/pos-system`: `lib/authErrors.ts` maps the exception (from `name` or a wrapped `__type`) to a real key plus the next step the user can take, splitting `InvalidParameterException` by message text — which is how Cognito reports both "this email was never verified so it cannot be reset" (the reported forgot-password half) and "already confirmed"; `FormAlert` + `AuthErrorAlert` render it **in the form** with a recovery button. Mail-delivery failures (`UnexpectedLambdaException` et al — the TSR-308 trigger) get their own message instead of reading as a credentials problem. 7 new tests pin the mapping incl. that every key it can return exists in both dictionaries; 449/449 pass, tsc + style policy clean. **Verified live** against the dev pool: registering the reported address now shows "Ya existe una cuenta con este correo…" + "Verificar mi correo". Commit `608b399`. |

### 2.B — Security, RBAC & multi-tenancy (Strategy pillar P3: precondition for everything multi-tenant)

**This is the pillar the 2026-06-11 sessions changed.** The RBAC-in-Express contract (`docs/roadmap/rbac_express_contract.md`) was fully implemented and verified across three parallel workstreams (server, POS UI, landing admin); all type checks/builds pass; 51/51 RBAC unit tests green. The deployment session then **applied the migration + seeds to the live DB, committed/pushed everything (monorepo + standalone repos), and realigned the catalog 1:1 with the POS sidebar** (TSR-037). The catalog reseed was executed and verified on 2026-06-11 — **the live DB now serves the new sidebar-mirroring catalog**. Rollout step 1 is complete; next gates are TSR-028/029 and the enforce flip (TSR-027).

Tiers **Semilla** (₡0 forever) / **Cosecha** / **Cooperativa** / **Feria** (custom). The "gratis" promise is protected by an explicit promise block: e-invoicing free on every tier, no sales commission, free data export, no expiry. **Amounts are preliminary** — `config.draftPricing: true` renders an "under review" notice; flip it off in the admin once final. **CTAs are inert** (`config.ctaComingSoon: true` → "Próximamente") until TSR-145 ships. **Two compliance corrections (2026-08-24):** all receipt types (FE/TE/NC/ND/FC/FEX) **and** ATV contingency mode moved into the free Semilla tier — both are legally required capability, not packaging, so they can never be gated (see the non-negotiables table in `tsuru_plans_implementation.md` §1). Stripe remains schema-only (`organizations.stripeCustomerId`, `plan`, no SDK). |

Wired into navbar (32px) + footer (28px). Going live needs no code change: upload via admin Media, set the URLs on Site identity. |
| TSR-144 | Business-card QR for `tsuru.jcampos.dev` | **Done** | This session (2026-08-23) | Generated with the POS's own `qrcode@1.5.4` (the engine behind `fe/pos-system/.../QrShareModal.tsx`) so the card QR and the merchant-facing QR come from one tool. Assets + specs + regenerator in `docs/brand/qr/` (brand / brand-on-white / mono, SVG + 2048px + 512px). Version 4, 33×33 modules, EC **H**; **min 21 mm printed, 25 mm recommended**. All raster variants decode-verified with jsQR. |
| TSR-145 | Implement the plans model in the BE + org creation + POS (entitlements, limits, upgrade UX) | **Spec written, not built** | This session (2026-08-24) | Full implementation spec: `docs/roadmap/tsuru_plans_implementation.md`. Key decision: **entitlements are a second axis from RBAC** (RBAC = which *user*; entitlements = what the *org* bought) — deny as **402 + requiredPlan**, never 403, so "hit your limit" never reads as "permission denied". New tables `plans` / `plan_entitlements` / `organization_subscriptions` (`limitValue NULL` = unlimited; per-org `overrides` implement Feria + grandfathering); plan activation *writes* the existing `organization_modules` rather than duplicating it. Rollout mirrors RBAC's `PLAN_ENFORCEMENT=log→enforce`. Org creation gets **no plan step** — every org starts Semilla. Steps 1–7 ship a working tier system with **zero Stripe**. |
| TSR-147 | CR pricing market research — validate the draft tiers against what merchants can actually buy | **Done** | This session (2026-08-24) | `docs/roadmap/tsuru_pricing_market_research.md`. Tsuru's real category is **POS + e-invoicing (US$20–80/mo)**, not pure invoicing ($5–20). At ₡452/USD: Cosecha $44.25/mo ($36.87 annual) sits mid-band and is **defensible** (Alegra POS Pro $50, POSMOVI Básico $45, Wolk $35) — the bundled storefront is what justifies it. **Cooperativa was above the entire market ceiling** (₡36.000/mes — Alegra POS Plus and POSMOVI Premium both land there). **Decided + applied 2026-08-24:** Cooperativa → **₡35.000/mes · ₡315.000/año**, and the annual discount widened 2 → **3 free months (25%, matching Alegra)** → Cosecha **₡180.000/año**. Doc rewritten **colón-first** (competitors converted *into* colones; no USD on any public surface). **IVA decided 2026-08-24: prices are FINAL, IVA included** — stated beside the amounts and in the FAQ (a 13% appearing later would be exactly the hidden charge "sin cargos ocultos" denies). This makes the comparison *better* than recorded: GTI quotes "+ IVA", so its real cost is 13% over list. **No-USD decided 2026-08-24:** customer-facing surfaces quote **colones only**, both languages. Rationale is the customer, not simplicity — Tsuru is only usable by a Hacienda filer, so there is no foreign-currency segment; and card networks convert at charge time with the issuer's own rate + FX fee, so any USD figure we printed would be systematically low against the real statement. The live exchange-rate service stays in the **POS**, where Hacienda requires the BCCR rate on USD invoices. `fe/pos-landing`'s USD toggle is **parked** (site not deployed) — delete the display if revived, don't wire a live rate into it. **All research items now closed.** |
| TSR-148 | Soft doc cap + 80% warning + document packs | **Spec'd (in TSR-145 §5.6); packs ride TSR-146** | This session (2026-08-24) | Owner decision 2026-08-24: `docs.perMonth` is a **soft cap** — warn at 80%, never 402 on emission. Market research independently supports it: Hacienda's **TicoFactura is free and uncapped**, so a hard cap sends merchants to a government product rather than to a paid tier. Overflow sells as non-expiring **document packs** (₡2.500/25, ₡8.000/100 — priced as a bridge so upgrading wins on the merchant's own arithmetic past ~250 docs). New `document_packs` table, FIFO consumption after the monthly allowance, no `expiresAt` (an expiring pack is a hidden charge). |
| TSR-146 | Stripe activation (payment collection only) | Not started — gated on TSR-145 | This session (2026-08-24) | Deliberately separated: plan assignment + enforcement work without any payment integration, and Feria never touches Stripe. Stripe becomes the source of truth for *payment state only*; entitlement resolution never calls it on the request path. `past_due` must go to a grace period, not an instant downgrade. TSR-084's "publish before activating" guardrail is now satisfied. |

WhatsApp handoff stays the model (storefronts AND fairs). |
