| TSR-275 | **store-be is down: the TSR-272 error contract cannot import on its Python 3.9 Lambda** | **Fixed (code); deploy pending TSR-276 rollout** (2026-09-16) | Owner report 2026-09-16: orders API returns 500 with no ErrorResponse DTO | The orders API — and every other store-be route — returned 500 since `c369185`. CloudWatch `/aws/lambda/cd-backend-dev-lambda` shows the real failure is at init, not in the handler: `Runtime.ImportModuleError: Unable to import module 'app.main': cannot import name 'UTC' from 'datetime' (/var/lang/lib/python3.9/datetime.py)` — 88 occurrences in 6 hours, still firing. store-be's `Dockerfile` pins `public.ecr.aws/lambda/python:3.9`, but `app/error_contract.py` used `datetime.UTC` (3.11+) and `list[dict[str, Any]] | None` in a Pydantic model body (3.10+, evaluated at class creation). **The DTO was missing for the same reason the 500 happened**: the app never booted, so no handler ran and API Gateway surfaced a bare Lambda error. sales-be/data-be were unaffected because they build on 3.12/3.14. Fixed with `timezone.utc` + `Optional[List[Dict[str, Any]]]`; an AST sweep confirms these were the only two 3.10+ constructs in evaluated positions anywhere in `app/`. Separately, `ErrorResponseDTO` was never declared in OpenAPI, so the FE had no typed error schema even when handlers did run — now applied to all 115 operations via `FastAPI(responses=ERROR_RESPONSES)` and regenerated into `swagger/backend.json` (68 paths unchanged; `api-gateway/template.yml` byte-identical). Verified: handler boots and `GET /api/organizations/{id}/orders` returns 200 against the live dev DB; 440 tests pass. **Follow-up:** store-be is the last service on 3.9 — bump the base image so the shared contract stops being a deploy-time trap. |
| TSR-276 | **Backend-owned support, audit and error control plane** | **Done (code; manual rollout pending)** (2026-09-16) | Owner correction: observability must not depend on FE reporting | A private standalone `tsuru-support-be`, generated with the `be-builder` skill, now owns support tickets/messages, the backend service/error catalog, request audit records and backend error records. Every management/data/sales/store HTTP Lambda publishes `AUDIT_REQUEST_COMPLETED` for non-health requests with request type, user, organization, route, status and duration; a 5xx or unhandled exception additionally publishes `BACKEND_ERROR_OCCURRED` with the stable enum/catalog code and private diagnostic details. Deterministic per-producer IAM policies allow publishing before the manually deployed topics exist, avoiding a cross-stack rollout dependency. SNS subscriptions filter by event type into separate SQS queues; the support Lambda consumes idempotently by event id and publishes a minimal AppSync invalidation only after a backend error is durable. The shared `/support/platform` channel accepts subscriptions only from the isolated admin Cognito pool; customer ticket updates remain pinned to `/support/{sub}`. Existing dev ticket/catalog tables are adopted in place—no customer data is dropped—while the old management routes, migrations, entities and client incident ingest are retired. POS retains only explicit ticket actions against the dedicated support API at `support.tsuru.jcampos.dev`; ticket creation is an app-native drawer with a styled module selector and direct-to-private-S3 image evidence. Automatic browser error/rejection requests were deleted. The admin gateway composes support `/api/admin/**`, and the local dashboard adds live-refreshing ticket, incident and audit views plus signed evidence access. `fe/dashboard` and `be/support-be` are isolated private repos; dashboard configuration is loaded from `/tsuru/{env}/admin-dashboard/*` SSM parameters by the root reboot script. Deployment is manual from root with `deploys/deploy-support-control-plane.sh`; no dashboard deploy is created. |
| TSR-277 | **Support reaches the POS from the account menu, on organization-scoped URLs** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-17) | Owner request 2026-09-17 | Support moved out of the POS sidebar footer into the navbar account menu, beside profile/switch-org/sign-out — the sidebar hid it whenever it collapsed, and it is an errand the person runs rather than a section of the app. The support page also had **no horizontal padding at all** (`space-y-5 py-5 max-w-6xl`), so its cards ran into both screen edges; it now uses the standard `px-4 sm:px-6 pt-6 pb-10 max-w-[1100px] mx-auto` frame. The tenant-facing support contract moved from `/api/support/tickets?organization_id=…` to `/api/organizations/{organization_id}/support/tickets`: the tenant is the resource scope so it is a path segment, the caller is not so it stays in the verified JWT (plus the `x-user-id` header the POS client now attaches to the support base), and neither appears in a query string. `organization_id` was removed from the create body — with `extra="forbid"` a body copy that disagreed with the URL is now a 422 rather than a silent choice between two scopes — and `_load_ticket` takes the organization, reporting another tenant's ticket as "not found" so a tenant-scoped URL cannot probe for ticket ids. The POS ticket-detail query key gained the org segment. Commits: POS `decac2b`, support-be `ede00f1`. |
| TSR-278 | **The support API was never reachable: three latent deploy defects, all now fixed** | **Done (root-caused + deployed)** (2026-09-17) | Owner report 2026-09-17: "i got no response from it" | `support.tsuru.jcampos.dev` had **no DNS record** — the TSR-276 rollout created the Lambda (healthy on direct invoke) and both event stacks, then stopped, and nothing said so. Three independent bugs: **(1)** `sam` resolves a *relative* `--config-file` against the template's directory, not the working directory, so `deploys/deploy-api.sh` died on "Config file api-gateway/samconfig.toml does not exist" — the api phase failed while the outer pipeline still reported success. **(2)** `aws cloudformation list-exports` paginates and `--output text` prints the query result for *every* page, so the control plane's pool lookup returned `us-east-1_0cTNJ31eo\nNone\nNone`; CloudFormation accepted the three-line string as a parameter and API Gateway rejected it with "ProviderARNs need to be valid Cognito Userpools", which fails the whole RestApi import. A `first_value` filter plus a pool-id shape check now guard it, and the hosted-zone lookup too. **(3)** SAM defaults `AddDefaultAuthorizerToCorsPreflight` to true, so the generated OPTIONS methods carried the Cognito authorizer and returned **401 to every browser preflight** — the real request would never have been sent. Now false, matching sales-api and admin-api which both answer 200. Verified live: `GET /api/organizations/{org}/support/tickets` → 401 unauthenticated (route exists, authorizer active) and the retired query-param route → 403 (gone). Commits: support-be `ede00f1`+, root roadmap/scripts. |
| TSR-279 | **The admin API gateway could never have deployed: two OpenAPI import defects** | **Done (root-caused + deployed)** (2026-09-17) | Found while deploying TSR-274's edge for the first time | The first real deploy of `tsuru-dev-admin-api` failed, twice, for two unrelated reasons — and **one bad path fails the entire RestApi import**, not just itself. **(1)** Every generated OPTIONS method mapped `method.response.header.Access-Control-Allow-*` on its integration response without **declaring** those headers on the method response, so all ~130 of them were rejected as "Invalid mapping expression parameter specified". **(2)** API Gateway allows exactly **one variable name per path position**, and this gateway is the only place all 30 data services are merged into one API, so a genuine conflict surfaced: `exemptions` owns `/countries/{iso_code}/exemptions/{id}` (the CRUD the dashboard needs) while `consumer-exemptions` owns `.../{authorization}` (a POS validation read). `drop_sibling_variable_paths` now keeps the path carrying mutations — the admin edge exists to edit catalogs — and prints every drop; the per-service data gateways are unaffected because each gets its own API. Live at `https://admin-api.tsuru.jcampos.dev` (129 paths / 227 operations, OPTIONS 200), with `/tsuru/dev/admin-dashboard/*` finally published. |
| TSR-280 | **The admin dashboard is online, deployed by its own GitHub Actions, and owns its whole control plane** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-17) | Owner request 2026-09-17 | Replaces the "local-only, manual root rollout" posture. The dashboard now deploys itself keylessly (GitHub OIDC → S3 + CloudFront) to **`admin.tsuru.jcampos.dev`** (briefly `admin-app` during the session; the owner kept the original name); `cloudformation/deploy-role.yml` grew from read-only SSM into a scoped deploy role (its own hosting stack, its own bucket, CloudFront invalidation, ACM/Route53, SSM read + its one `site/url` parameter) with trust still pinned to `ref:refs/heads/main`, and it deliberately has **no IAM-write permission** — so an admin principal bootstraps it once and routine deploys are IAM no-ops. `deploys/deploy.sh` now takes `-` for ambient credentials and `SKIP_BUILD=1` so CI does not build twice. Per owner request the **admin stacks moved out of the root `admin-api/` into the dashboard repo** (`cloudformation/admin-cognito.yml`, `cloudformation/admin-dashboard-params.yml`, `api/generate_admin_api.py` + committed `api/template.yml`, one `deploys/deploy-*.sh` per stack, `deploys/deploy-all.sh` sequencing them, `deploys/lib.sh` holding the pagination/rollback/`sam` gotchas); the root directory is retired and `deploys/deploy-support-control-plane.sh` now sequences the dashboard's own scripts. The generator reads the backend OpenAPI from sibling checkouts (`TSURU_WORKSPACE_ROOT`), which do not exist in the dashboard's CI — hence the committed template and `--skip-generate`. |
| TSR-281 | **Incidents and Audit 500'd, and every backend audit event was being rejected: the support migration had never run** | **Done (fixed + data recovered)** (2026-09-17) | Owner report 2026-09-17: errors on the incidents and audit pages | `relation "audit_records" does not exist`. support-be's deployment map sets `databaseMigrations.automatic: false`, and the rollout deployed the Lambda and both event stacks without ever running `scripts/migrate-db.sh` — `alembic current` returned nothing at all. The visible symptom was three admin endpoints (`/api/admin/support/overview`, `/incidents`, `/audit`) returning `COMMON_500` while `/tickets` and `/error-catalog` returned 200, because the ticket/catalog tables already existed from management migrations 0021/0022 and only the **new** `audit_records` / `backend_errors` tables were missing. **The worse, invisible half:** the SQS consumer was failing every message (`BatchProcessingError: All records failed processing`), so audit events from all four backends were being rejected and dead-lettered — the observability plane was silently dropping the data it exists to keep. Applying `b8538a2a34fc` fixed it (the revision is written entirely with `IF NOT EXISTS`, and its DROPs are in `downgrade()` only, so it adopts the live tables without touching data). The 111 stranded messages in `tsuru-dev-audit-records-dlq` were then redriven — safe because the consumer is idempotent by event id — and the DLQ drained to 0. Now: all three endpoints 200, **129 audit records** across support-api/platform-api/organization-configurations, 6 incidents, 0 consumer failures. The 6 incidents are this outage's own 500s, which is the system correctly recording its own failure. |
| TSR-282 | **`<sub>@admins.invalid` in the platform user directory is by design, not a leak** | **Explained; no change** (2026-09-17) | Owner question 2026-09-17 | It is **not** in the customer Cognito pool — that pool holds exactly 4 users and none is this subject. It is a row in the platform `users` **table**, created by `createRequirePlatformAdmin` (management-be `src/middleware/permissions.ts`) the first time an admin token reaches `/api/admin/**`. Existing FK/audit columns (`support_messages.author_user_id`, `organization_modules.assigned_by`) reference `users.id`, so an administrator acting on them needs a row; the email is deliberately non-routable (`.invalid` is RFC 2606 reserved, so it can never receive mail) and the admin pool stays the only authentication source of truth. **The wart worth knowing:** these synthetic principals appear in the dashboard's Users directory looking like ordinary accounts, and `/api/admin/overview` counts them — the 9 users it reports includes this one. If that becomes confusing, filter `role = 'platform_admin'` out of the directory rather than stopping the mirror, which is load-bearing. |
| TSR-283 | **The admin console adopts the POS design system, end to end** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-18) | Owner request 2026-09-18 | The two consoles looked like different products. The POS's `@layer base`/`@layer components` were ported with their layer membership preserved (a flattened copy loses the cascade order and Tailwind utilities stop overriding), along with the Barlow faces, `.btn`/`.card`/`.t-*`/`.pp-th` primitives, the open/close sidebar with its toggle tucked **under** the rail, page transitions, and POS select/input primitives everywhere. Every module got pagination, a POS-style filters modal, drawers for add/edit, card-format skeletons while filters load, and tables that autoload rather than waiting for an *Actualizar* click. Catalogs gained typed editors instead of raw inputs: country select (CR/188 default), document-version select off the versions catalog, status select; economic activities and regimes are **read-only registries** (they are Hacienda's, not ours). Hardcoded strings were swept into the EN/ES dictionaries — the sidebar had never responded to the language switch at all. |
| TSR-284 | **The plan belongs to the user, not the organization** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-18) | Owner request 2026-09-18 | A paid owner can hold several organizations and a free owner exactly one, which the old `organizations.plan` column could not express — the quota question is about a *person*. `users.plan` is now the column; an organization derives its owner's. Migration `0021` backfills from owned orgs and keeps `organization_plan_backup_0021`. `src/lib/plans.ts` holds the one quota table (`semilla` 1 · `cosecha` 3 · `cooperativa` ∞ · `feria` 3) and `canOwnAnotherOrg`. Plan names are translated (*Gratis*, not `free`) and an org at `onboarding_step` 3 reads **Complete** rather than "step 3". |
| TSR-285 | **Walmart chain fields are order values on a manual order, not document `other_fields`** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-18) | Owner report 2026-09-18 | The three codes were verified against the chain's spec and pinned by a contract test (`chainOtherFields.test.ts`): exactly `WMNumeroVendedor`, `WMEnviarGLN`, `WMNumeroOrden`. The checkout wiring was the actual defect — on a **manual** order the purchase-order number, delivery point and department are properties of the *order*, so they are written to `document_number`, `delivery_location` and `department_id` rather than appended as document other-fields. The POS sends the customer identification and `useNationalTaxpayer` matches it against the taxpayer-companies catalog, so the chain fields appear (auto-selected, order number read-only) only for a customer that is one, and the definitions moved into the taxpayer catalogs instead of living in POS code. The order PDF is now generated on manual-order creation, not only on Excel import. |
| TSR-286 | **Customer tabs follow what the customer actually is** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-18) | Owner request 2026-09-18 | Departments and delivery points are chain concepts, so those tabs appear only once the client's identifier validates as a registered contribuyente; the Orders tab appears when the customer has emitted orders — for any customer, not just a chain. Supplier identifiers got the same cédula mask the rest of the app uses. |
| TSR-287 | **Support views show the state being asked about, not everything** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-18) | Owner requests 2026-09-18 | *Incidentes (24 h)* counted resolved incidents too, so the overview never went quiet after a fix — it now counts unresolved only, backed by a nullable `resolved_at` and a partial index. The audit table shows Método/Servicio/Ruta/Estado and moves the rest into the drawer, so every table in the console reads the same. A resolved ticket offers **Reabrir**; a closed one drops the reply field entirely; the priority select hides on both end states; priority colours the ticket row. The users directory stopped printing the role twice and gives the customer badge a colour of its own (beside the administrator's tint, plain grey read as a failure to render). |
| TSR-288 | **Users can be deactivated from the console, and organizations have a detail page** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-18) | Owner request 2026-09-18 | `PATCH /api/admin/users/:userId` flips `is_active` and **refuses self-deactivation** (409) — the console does not offer it either, since that is a support call answered from the database. The organization detail page mirrors the customer one: overview, a users tab carrying each member's role *in that organization*, and a Hacienda tab telling support whether the org is wired up at all. |
| TSR-289 | **One service per table: the fiscal reads the console shows come from sales-be** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-18) | Owner request 2026-09-18 | The organization detail page's Hacienda block was first built by having management-be read `registered_organizations` and `organization_hacienda` with raw cross-service SQL — two services authoritative for one schema, where a column renamed in sales-be breaks a page in the console silently, at runtime, with nothing in either repo's CI to catch it. Each owning service now exposes its own support read (`/api/admin/organizations/{id}/fiscal-identity`, `/fiscal-credentials`), the admin gateway composes them alongside management's and support's routes (with explicit Lambda names — the generator's `hacienda-*` fallback does not describe these two functions), and the console joins them with `fiscalStatus.ts`, whose cases mirror the POS's `useFiscalMode` so support is never told something the cashier's POS does not believe. **Credentials are presence-only** — the row holds the PKCS12, its PIN and the ATV password, and none of them reach a response. `/api/admin/**` is excluded from sales-be's own customer gateway (`SKIP_PREFIXES`): published there it would be authorized by the POS pool, letting any signed-in cashier read another organization's fiscal wiring by id. |
| TSR-290 | **Two deploy-time traps the fiscal reads walked into: a path-variable collision and a credential suffix** | **Done (fixed + deployed)** (2026-09-18) | Found while deploying TSR-289 | **(1)** sales-be named the new path position `{organizationId}` while management-be already owned `/api/admin/organizations/{orgId}`. API Gateway permits one variable name per position **across the resource tree**, so the two collided although nothing about them compares equal — and the import failed whole, taking all 233 operations with it and rolling the stack back. The generator's `drop_sibling_variable_paths` compares complete paths and could never have seen it; `check_variable_name_consistency` now raises at generation, naming the parent and both spellings, so the class costs a second rather than a fifteen-minute round trip. It **raises rather than resolves**: dropping a path would silently remove a working route, and renaming one in the generator would leave the service's own OpenAPI disagreeing with the gateway. **(2)** The credentials endpoint returned `configuration.username` straight from the column — but organization-configurations persists every credential as `base64(value + security_key)`, and that suffix is **shared with the Hacienda password and the certificate PIN**. A live probe showed 14 characters trailing the ATV domain: the endpoint built to disclose nothing would have published the key protecting the two things it deliberately withholds. It now decodes with `credential_utils.decode_stored_credential` and returns the identifier alone. |
| TSR-291 | **Orders 500'd with no error DTO and no incident — and none of it was the orders code** | **Done (fixed + deployed)** (2026-09-18) | Owner report 2026-09-18 | Three failures stacked on one endpoint, each hiding the one beneath. **(1)** `observability_events.py` called `traceback.format_exception(exc)` — the single-argument form, Python **3.10+**. store-be is the last service on 3.9 (the exact TSR-275 pattern: the shared file is byte-identical across four repos and was only ever tested where it works), so it raised `TypeError` *inside the error reporter*, in a `finally` block. **An exception raised in `finally` replaces the exception being handled**, so the real error never reached an exception handler — hence a bare 500 with no DTO. The three-argument form is correct on 3.9 through 3.14. **(2)** Everything around `_publish` ran unguarded in that `finally`; `_publish` swallowed its own failures but the payload-building did not. Reporting is best-effort by definition and can no longer change a request's outcome — applied to all four copies. **(3)** `cd-backend-dev-role` had no `SNS:Publish`, which is why no incident was recorded and what triggered (1) in the first place; it was missed when `tsuru-dev-sqs-policy` was attached to the 35 peer roles. With the mask removed, the actual error was visible on the first probe — see TSR-292. |
| TSR-292 | **Moving `plan` to `users` broke two other services, because they model `organizations` themselves** | **Done (fixed + deployed)** (2026-09-18) | Found by TSR-291's probe | `psycopg.errors.UndefinedColumn: column organizations.plan does not exist`. TSR-284's migration 0021 dropped the column, but **store-be and sales-be each keep their own SQLAlchemy model of `organizations`**, so the dropped column stayed in every SELECT they emitted and failed the query outright — `GET /orders` among them. Neither service ever *read* the attribute; it was only ever being selected, which is the whole reason nothing caught it. Removed from both models (plus sales-be's index, `__repr__`, `RegisteredOrganizationResponse` field and its mapping, and the dead copy on the shared `OrganizationResponse`), then **both models were diffed against the live table** to confirm `plan` was the only stale column in each. This is the same class of coupling TSR-289 addressed for reads, showing up in the opposite direction: there a console read another service's table, here two services model a table whose migrations they do not own. A schema change in management-be still has no CI anywhere that would fail on it. |
| TSR-293 | **Download your own signing certificate from the POS** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-18) | Owner request 2026-09-18 | "We lost the .p12" is real and otherwise unrecoverable — the certificate belongs to the organization that uploaded it. Served by its own endpoint (`GET /organizations/{id}/configurations/certificate`), deliberately NOT as a field on the configurations payload: that payload loads on every settings visit, and the signing key would end up in browser caches and proxy logs forever. Fetched only on the click, logged when it happens, and **the PIN is not included** — one intercepted response must not be enough to sign as the taxpayer, and whoever is entitled to the file knows the PIN they chose for it. |
| TSR-294 | **Login and register redirect when you are already signed in** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-18) | Owner request 2026-09-18 | Both apps rendered their auth pages regardless of session, so a signed-in user saw a form that made the app look signed out and could submit it against a live session. Both guards wait for the session to resolve before rendering — checking first would flash the login page at a signed-in user on every reload — and the POS honours the `redirectAfterLogin` that `RequireAuth` parks, so arriving at /login with a live session finishes the trip you started rather than dumping you on the dashboard. |
| TSR-295 | **The POS dashboard was answering the wrong questions, and one of its status sets did not exist** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-19) | Owner report 2026-09-18/19 | Three separate defects behind "0 orders and ₡0 ticket". **(1)** Every figure was derived by walking active session → active assignment → that assignment's orders, so an org with 45 orders and nobody on a till reported zeros *by design* — 44 of those 45 carry no `assignment_id` at all. **(2)** The aggregation queried `sales_orders`/`order_items`, tables that **do not exist** in this database, and the failure was caught and turned into an empty list with a comment calling it expected — so a broken query was indistinguishable from a quiet day. **(3)** The replacement's status tuples were written from assumption: five of the eight strings (`invoiced`, `completed`, `in_progress`, `dispatched`, `sent`) are not `OrderStatus` values, and `shipped` — which is — was in neither set, so a shipped order vanished from the dashboard entirely. Demonstrated against Postgres before/after with a synthetic row. The sets are derived from the enum now and `TestStatusBuckets` asserts the three groups partition it exactly. Also: **Reportes had been rendering ₡0 for everything** (a local `ReportData` with camelCase Spanish keys against a snake_case API — nothing matched, every total fell through to a zero default), and its "session" mode showed org-wide totals under a session's name because the deprecated endpoint passed `session_id` only to its stations query. Three panels displaying fabricated data were deleted rather than left: the payment breakdown and cash-difference KPI (cash/sinpe/card are hardcoded `0.0` server-side — those live on a session CLOSING, never fetched) and the ranking's emoji column (the API returns an image url, so the dashboard showed empty squares and the report a hardcoded 🍗 for every product in every business). |
| TSR-296 | **Dashboard granularity, orders-vs-documents, and the first authorization in store-be** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-19) | Owner request 2026-09-18 | `sales-trend` takes `hour`/`day`/`week`/`month` plus a date window (`date_trunc` over `created_on`); live it returns 18/17/15/8 buckets over the same orders. The granularity **cannot** be a bind parameter, so it is interpolated — whitelisted in the repository, which raises rather than formatting what it was handed, with the route rejecting a bad value as 400. Empty buckets are absent rather than zero, and the response echoes what it answered for so a chart cannot mislabel its axis. **Orders or documents**, chosen by the user: they are different populations (an order may never be billed; a document may have no order), so store-be aggregates orders and sales-be's new `/documents/*` panels aggregate documents — same response shapes, neither service reading the other's tables. Hacienda-rejected documents are excluded from revenue but counted in the status panel: 39 worth ₡444 226 on the live org, which is what an operator acts on. Live totals differ as they should — 33 orders/₡2 553 498 vs 202 documents/₡1 207 496. **Scope** resolves to org / session / own-in-session and is enforced **server-side**: this is the first authorization of any kind in store-be, which trusted `x-user-id` on every route. `caller_role_repository` reads `organization_members`/`roles` directly (no per-request hop on a five-panel dashboard, no token to forward; support-be already reads membership this way) and **fails closed** — an unresolvable role is not-admin, and a failed lookup is deliberately not cached so a blip cannot pin someone for five minutes. Verified on the deployed Lambda: a non-member asking for the owner's `user_id` gets 0 orders, not 33. `_scope_sql` is defined once and applied by all five queries, because a scope forgotten on one panel is a leak rather than a display bug. ⚠️ Session-scoped ORDER figures stay near-empty until orders are attributed to an assignment — only 1 of 45 is. |
| TSR-297 | **Order dates become real dates** | **Done (migrated dev + deployed)** (2026-09-19) | Phase plan | `crossdocking_orders.creation_date`/`.delivery_date` and `crossdocking_confirmations.delivery_date` were VARCHAR(20) holding **two formats**: `DD/MM/YYYY` from the Excel import, `YYYY-MM-DD` from the POS and storefront. So `search_utils` compared them as raw strings (day-first lexicographic — a range never matched an ISO-dated manual order), `confirmation_service._parse_date` HARD-FAILED on the ISO form, and the dashboard avoided the columns entirely because a cast reads the wrong month. **The migration matches each row's shape rather than casting**: this server is `ISO, MDY` and 12 of 45 rows have a day ≤ 12, so `'09/02/2026'::date` is 2 September — silently, on a quarter of the table. Verified against a pre-migration snapshot of all 45 rows: 0 mismatches, 0 NULLs, every ambiguous row kept its month. `app/utils/order_dates.py` owns the coercion so every reader works on both sides of the migration. Consequences: ranges and `orderBy>deliveryDate` are chronological (live: ISO and `DD/MM` ranges both return the same 9 orders); `/dashboard/session-sales` asks the real question and reports `delivery_date_today` instead of its `created_today` stand-in; responses serialise ISO always. On the FE, **five private date formatters would each have broken**: two split on "/" unconditionally (an ISO date rendered as the raw string) and three fell back to `new Date(iso)` — UTC midnight, the previous evening in Costa Rica, so they showed a day early. All now go through `formatOrderDate`. One flaw the new tests caught pre-ship: `_is_date_column` used a `startswith("DATE")` prefix and SQLAlchemy renders `DateTime` as "DATETIME", so `created_on` filters would have been silently coerced to whole days. |
| TSR-298 | **Reschedule a delivery without re-uploading the spreadsheet** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-19) | Owner request 2026-09-18 | `PATCH /orders/{doc}` took `{status}` only, so correcting a delivery date meant deleting the order and re-importing its Excel — and the unique document-number index refuses a re-upload, making it a two-step job. Body is now `{status?, delivery_date?}` with at least one required, so status-only clients are unaffected. **The trap that makes this more than a column write:** `reprocess_order` re-parses the stored Excel and `_update_order_from_parsed` assigns `delivery_date` from it, and Reprocess is an item in the order's own POS menu — so a database-only change is silently reverted. Proven on a real order: after the edit, `parse_order_detail_file` (the exact function reprocess uses) reads the NEW date back from the rewritten sheet. The rewrite already existed inside `confirmation_service`, reachable only as a side effect of linking a confirmation; it moved to `order_excel_dates.rewrite_delivery_date` so the edit and the confirmation flow share one implementation, and runs after the commit — an S3 failure must not roll back a correction. Guards: `pending` or `processing` only (owner call — an order being prepared can be rescheduled; shipped means the date has been acted on and the customer told), unbilled (the date is on the fiscal document, and the error names it), not in the past (the same rule confirmations apply). A refused edit or illegal transition is now **400**, not the 500 it used to be — and that handler referenced a `logger` the module never defined, so the 500 path would have raised `NameError` on top of whatever it was reporting. |
| TSR-299 | **16 TypeScript errors in fe/pos-landing that no command anyone runs would surface** | **Done (fixed)** (2026-09-19) | Owner question 2026-09-19 | Dating from the relocation commit and never compiled. The root `tsconfig.json` is `files: []` plus project references, so **`tsc --noEmit` type-checks nothing and exits 0** — the obvious command is the one that cannot fail; `pnpm typecheck` (`tsc -b`) surfaces all 16. The substantive one: `AppConfig['pricing']` declared `plans` and `features` as REQUIRED while `config.json` carries zero of each there — they live in `translations[lang].pricing`, which is where the components read them, so the type described a shape that never existed and the correct code was what failed to compile. Fixing that cleared 8 errors including all five implicit-`any` parameters (untyped only because the array they mapped had no type). Rest: two icons used but never registered; `TickerSection` passing an `onRemove` prop `ItemActions` has never had while omitting its required `index`/`total`; `TextField.label` typed `string` where three columns legitimately pass an icon plus text; and `useAutoTranslate` re-reading `window.Translator` inside an async closure where the guard's narrowing does not reach — captured in a local rather than asserted away. All four TS projects clean. |
| TSR-300 | **Checkout drawer: order facts on the order card, and three fields that were asked twice or not at all** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-19) | Owner reports 2026-09-19 | Proforma, order number and delivery date sat in the *Documento* card while describing the order, and for a chain the order number appeared **twice** — there and as the chain's purchase order, which is the same value (`useCartFlow` writes `purchase_order_number` into `document_number`; it reaches the billed document as `WMNumeroOrden`). Same duplication for the destination: the general delivery field beside the chain's registered delivery point, when `chainDeliveryLocation ?? manualFields.delivery_location` means the chain select already IS it. All moved to the Order card, with the chain variant rendering exactly one of each and saying so. The **branch/terminal card was a contradiction** — hidden for pedidos "because a pedido has no consecutive to segment", while the manual-order payload sends `branch_number`/`terminal_number` regardless, so the flow required a till and offered no way to see it. Now shown, and **read-only when an assignment is active** (owner call: the till is what the shift was opened on; overriding it for one document would make the closing reconcile against another station). The **economic-activity select is hidden for an org with no Hacienda profile** — activities come from the registered taxpayer record, so it rendered permanently empty on `PM`, the one type such an org can create. The **vendor number is a label under the department** that determines it, not an input: `supplier_code` was not on the manual-order DTO at all, so the value never left the browser, and `chainInfoFromOrder` read the department's CODE rather than its `supplier_code` — so `WMNumeroVendedor` appeared only after the departments list loaded and the back-fill ran. `POST /orders` now maps both chain identifiers the way the Excel import does (**backfill only**: absent gets filled, present is left alone). **Mobile currency overflow** was `grid-cols-2` unconditionally — ~120px per field in a 520px drawer, so "USD — Dólar estadounidense" overflowed onto the exchange-rate field and the rate looked missing; theme-dependent because each theme sets its own font, so a wider face overflowed sooner. One column below `sm` with `min-w-0` removes the dependence instead of tuning per theme. |
| TSR-301 | **Navbar cleanup, a Sync button that did nothing, and borrowed branding before sign-in** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-19) | Owner requests 2026-09-19 | The mobile `+` loses its label (the `+` says it; `aria-label` keeps it accessible). **Sincronizar had no `onClick` at all** — pressing it did nothing; it moved into the account menu, where it belongs to the person's session, and now calls `syncPendingSales` and invalidates the queries. **Tab overflow is measured, not guessed**: the count was a fixed 2 or 3 from the breakpoint, which assumes every tab is the same width — they are translated document-type names, which is how the last tab ended up under the navbar's right-hand controls. `overflow-hidden` stops the overlap and `DocumentsToolbar` measures what fits; clipped tabs stay in the DOM and are made inert, because unmounting them would remove their width from the next measurement, fit one more, and oscillate. **Auth and org-picker routes now render the default Tsuru theme** — they were showing the LAST organization's, since with no org in scope the resolver fell through to the id mirrored in localStorage. So signing out gave a login page in someone else's branding, and the org picker branded itself as one of the options it was asking you to choose between. Those routes also no longer write to localStorage, which would erase the org's theme. |
| TSR-302 | **The history summary card cost 11 requests against an account limit of 10** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-19) | Owner report 2026-09-19 | `Reportes → Documentos históricos de Hacienda` never loaded, for a reason that was invisible from the browser. The card derived its numbers from **eleven parallel `size=1` list requests** (total, four ATV verdicts, six document types), each read for its `total_elements`. The **account's Lambda concurrency limit is 10** — the AWS default, never raised — so the eleventh came back `ConcurrentInvocationLimitExceeded`; API Gateway renders that 429 as a **500**, and a gateway 500 carries **no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`**, so the browser reported a statusless network error with nothing in the console. The card's `isError` was `.some(...)` across all eleven, so one slice blanked the whole card — every single load. Diagnosed from the gateway execution log, not the browser: the Lambda logged only 10 invocations, all 200. New `GET /historical-documents/summary` answers it from one `GROUP BY (atv_status, document_type)` — every row is in exactly one pair, so the caller sums the same grid two ways. The request COUNT is now pinned in tests on both sides, because nothing in TypeScript or a rendering test can catch that regression. **See §7: the concurrency ceiling of 10 is an ecosystem-wide risk, not a POS bug.** |
| TSR-303 | **The IVA declaration report (D-150) had no backend at all** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-19) | Owner report 2026-09-19 | The POS has shipped the entire `Reportes → Declaración de IVA` surface — the page, seven components, `useIvaReport`, 197 lines of DTOs and a 353-line contract document — against an endpoint **nobody ever built**. `grep -rn tax-reports` across sales-be, store-be and data-be returned nothing, and no commit ever added or removed it from the gateway template. So it failed for every organization and every period, and it failed *badly*: an unmapped API Gateway path answers **403**, with no CORS headers, so the hook's "404 means an empty period" branch never fired and the page showed a hard error. `GET /tax-reports/iva` and `/tax-reports/iva/periods` now implement formulario D-150 as specified — Section I by rate bracket with the merchandise/service split (`product_type`, falling back to the CABYS first-digit rule), II by destination, III prorrata, IV determination, V/VI settlement, plus the warning codes. **Only ACCEPTED documents count** (1 and 2): TRIBU-CR prefills from what Hacienda accepted and this report exists to reconcile against that draft — the opposite choice from `document_metrics_repository`, where an in-flight document IS revenue. **Renglones the platform cannot source are zero, never guessed** — card-acquirer withholdings, prior-period balances, capital-goods credit and the purchase destination split are recorded nowhere here, and a plausible-looking number on a tax report is the one thing a reconciliation aid must not produce; each is called out. 30 tests written longhand from the form, not from the code's output: every rate bracket, the prorrata at 100/80/50 %, month-end exclusivity (`sale_date` is a timestamp, so `<= last day` silently drops a whole day of sales), leap February, the December year roll. |
| TSR-304 | **`SaleCondition` disagrees with Hacienda v4.4 Nota 5 in sales-be** | **Done (superseded by TSR-310)** (2026-09-19) | Found 2026-09-19 while building TSR-303 | `jbiller_common`'s `SaleCondition` reads 10 = *Regalía*, 11 = *Arrendamiento internacional*, 12-15 as consignment/lease variants. The POS's `SaleConditionCode`, built from Nota 5, reads 10 = *Venta a crédito con IVA diferido hasta 90 días (art. 27)*, 11 = its REP payment, 12 = mercancía no nacionalizada, 13 = bienes usados no contribuyente, 99 = otros. The POS list matches the published note; this enum looks like an older table — the **same class of divergence already documented for `ReferenceActionCode`, which disagreed with the data-api catalog on 8 of 14 values**. TSR-303's deferred-IVA figure is unaffected (both agree the code is `10`, only the name differs) and it pins the literal with that reasoning recorded. Fixing a fiscal enum needs its own change: check every consumer of `SaleCondition` against the data-api catalog, since a wrong `CondicionVenta` on a signed document is a rejected or misdeclared document. |
| TSR-305 | **The ledger only forward-filled: 226 POS documents, and every rejection, had no row** | **Done (backfilled dev)** (2026-09-19) | Owner report 2026-09-19 | The Reportes history card showed **0 rechazados** for an organization with 39 rejected documents. The card was right about the ledger; the ledger was wrong. The validator publishes a history event for every terminal verdict *including* rejections, but that call was only wired on **2026-09-13** — and the data splits exactly there: of the documents issued 13-14 Sep, **4 of 4** have a ledger row; of the 237 issued before, **0** do. **The sweep could never have repaired it**: it reads the taxpayer's ledger from Hacienda, and Hacienda does not list documents it REJECTED — so a rejection has exactly one record anywhere in the world, our own `billing_sales` row (which is also why the 100 `source='HISTORY'` rows are all `atv_status=1`). `scripts/backfill_pos_historical_documents.py` recovers them through `upsert_by_clave` — the table's single writer, which already refuses to let a skeleton overwrite a signed verdict — in one transaction, dry-run by default. Applied to dev with owner approval: **226 rows (187 accepted + 39 rejected)**, card now 330 / 291 aceptado / 39 rechazado. It deliberately does **not** synthesise `atv_errors`: the reasons live in `billing_sale_hacienda_errors`, and copying text onto a fiscal record without the signed response to check it against is not a backfill's job. **`stag`/`prod` need the same run if they hold documents issued before their validator carried this path.** |
| TSR-306 | **`billing_sale_summary` is not persisting the merchandise/service split** | **Done (superseded by TSR-309)** (2026-09-19) | Found 2026-09-19 while building TSR-303 | `taxed_merchandise`, `taxed_services`, `exempt_total` and `non_taxable_total` are **0 across all 190 summaries** in dev, while `iva_total` is populated. Those are `ResumenFactura` fields on the signed XML, so this is not cosmetic. It also cost the IVA report its best source: the summary IS the authoritative figure (it is what was signed and what Hacienda prefills from), and Section I would have come straight from it — sourcing it today would have reported zeros, so the report derives the split from the lines instead. Separately, `iva_total` (₡104 299.56 for Sep) excludes the code-07 IVACE amount (₡4 550) that the report's débito includes; worth confirming which is right before anything depends on `iva_total`. |
| TSR-307 | **A ₡617 residual where the persisted `base_amount` is short of the taxed base** | **Done (superseded by TSR-308)** (2026-09-19) | Found 2026-09-19 verifying TSR-303 | With the base corrected to `base_amount` (BaseImponible), every IVA bracket reconciles with its own tax to the céntimo **except** the 13 % row, which carries ₡2 826.96 more tax than 13 % of its base. It decomposes without remainder: **₡2 209.83 is the code-08 factor regime**, which is correct by Hacienda's definition (tax = base x `FactorCalculoIVA`, not base x rate), and **₡617.13 is not explained**. 128 of those 136 code-01 lines do carry `base_amount`; the residual is ~₡4 747 of missing base, consistent with the 8 lines that have none plus a persisted base that omits some base-building excise. Fix belongs upstream in whatever writes `base_amount`. **Deliberately left visible** — back-deriving the base as `tax / rate` would make every row self-consistent by construction and hide the gap, and on a tax report a figure that cannot be wrong is no longer evidence of anything. |
| TSR-308 | **The IVA report's base was the wrong column; every bracket now reconciles to ₡0.00** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-19) | Owner request 2026-09-19 | Closes TSR-307. `base_amount` is a client-supplied INPUT, legal only for the two editable cases (`TaxValidator` raises `HACIENDA_BASE_AMOUNT_NOT_EDITABLE` elsewhere); where an ordinary line has one it is a legacy pre-TSR-261 row holding the plain `subtotal`, so preferring it read a stale subtotal in place of the real one — and the two differ by exactly the base-building excises, which was the entire discrepancy. The base now mirrors the signing path (`sales_pipeline._compute_lines` + `tax_service.editable_base_for`): `subtotal` + excises, override only where Hacienda allows one. Codes imported from `tax_service.BASE_BUILDING_EXCISES` (02 ISC, 04 ISEBA, 05 ISEBEC, 12 ISEC — documented against Hacienda's `-454`), never retyped; deriving the set independently from 136 live lines produced exactly those four. **An override equal to the subtotal is not an override** — nine live lines carry `iva_collected_factory='01'` with `base_amount == subtotal`, and honouring it suppresses the excise addition `-454` demands; that guard is the difference between ₡231.51 unexplained and ₡0.00. Verified on live dev 2026-09: all brackets 0.00, code 08 at +2 209.83 (the factor regime, correct). The base is never back-derived as `tax / rate` — a figure that cannot be wrong is not evidence. |
| TSR-309 | **16 of 23 `billing_sale_summary` columns were NULL on every row ever written** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-19) | Owner request 2026-09-19 | Closes TSR-306. 241 of 241 rows in dev. `SummaryService.build_resumen` computes the taxed/exempt/exonerated/non-subject split for the signed XML and nothing carried it back — `SalesPipelineResult` lacked it and `_fill_summary` ended in an admitted no-op stub. The pipeline now returns a frozen `DocumentSummaryTotals` read off the resumen it actually signed. **`base` variant only**: the XML carries ONE `ResumenFactura`, in the document's own currency, so copying it onto the dollar/colon twins would state amounts the document never did. Absent stays absent (Hacienda omits a zero total). **The column names are inverted** — the PDF proves it: "Total exento" renders `taxFreeTotal` and "Total exonerado" renders `exemptTotal`, so `exempt_*` holds exonerado. Mapped explicitly with tests asserting each pair in the direction that would be got wrong; renaming the columns needs a migration and is left recorded. Also fixed `iva_total`, the **fourth** copy of the literal `{"01","08"}` — it understated the stored total by exactly the IVACE amount (₡4 550 over one month). `IVA_FAMILY` now lives in `enums/tax_type`, beside the codes, in a module importing only `enum`; `summary_service`, `sale_calculation_service` and `iva_report_repository` all delegate. |
| TSR-310 | **Four Hacienda enums diverged from the catalog; the fidelity probe now covers all six** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-19) | Owner request 2026-09-19 | Closes TSR-304 and widens it. `referenceCodes` was the only catalog anybody had ever checked; extending the probe found `saleConditions` wrong on **12 of 16** rows (and missing 99), `paymentMethods` on 3 of 8 **with `07 = Plataforma Digital` absent entirely**, `discountTypes` on 9 of 11 (plus a code 10 the catalog lacks), `otherCharges` on all 11 (plus invented 11/12). **`referenceCodes` itself is clean** — 0 mismatches, fixed in TSR-126 — which a report I was working from claimed was wrong on 12/12; checking rather than trusting it is why this is scoped correctly. No wrong Spanish ever reached a document (`description` is unused outside each enum; the XML and PDF render the caller's text), but the values drive accept-sets: **a Plataforma Digital payment was impossible to file**, `06` meaning "Permuta" accepted SINPE MÓVIL under a barter code, and `SaleCondition.OTHER = "15"` made `_OTHER_CODES` demand `sale_condition_otros` for a legitimate *Arrendamiento Financiero* while `document_xml_mapper` emits `CondicionVentaOtros` for 99 alone — validator and mapper now agree. Narrowing three accept-sets verified against live data first (no row uses discount 10, `billing_sale_other_charges` is empty, dev holds only payment 01/04 and condition 01). `probe_matrix.catalog_fidelity` went 80 → **92 probes** and fails if 07 is removed again. PDF: sale conditions 13/14/15 had no label and printed a BLANK cell, and 99 printed a literal `%s` because that cell never piped its printf placeholder through `format`; both fixed, with a test that every enum code renders something. The PDF's payment and other-charge tables were ALREADY correct — more evidence the enums were the stale copies. |
| TSR-311 | **Typed rows and response contracts across all five backends** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-19) | Owner request 2026-09-19 | "No `get('')` calls, only DTO-defined calls", both readings. **Typed rows:** `iva_report_repository` returned `Dict[str, Any]` read with 25 string-key lookups where a typo was silent end to end (`.get` → None → `_money(None)` → 0.0 → a renglón reporting nothing); now three frozen dataclasses following the existing `_OrgConfigurationRow`/`OrganizationAggregate` shape, with a `_hydrate` that REJECTS an unmodelled column. store-be's `calculate_expected_amounts` returns a typed `ExpectedAmounts` with an `available` flag — see TSR-312. support-be's 16 dict-returning service methods return DTOs; zero `dict[str, Any]` left in that file. **Response contracts:** 192 data-be routes bound (2 → 194), derived by AST from the DTO each already named in `responses=create_responses(...)`; **37 distinct DTOs round-tripped** through `create_response` → `model_validate` → `model_dump` with zero mismatches, each sub-app in its own interpreter because they shadow each other on `dtos.responses.*`. Regenerating swagger changed exactly ONE file (the CABYS import, which had no `responses=` before): the rest were already published, so binding added runtime validation without moving the contract, and `api-gateway/template.yml` is byte-identical at 79 paths. Also bound: sales-be's bell (4) + two admin fiscal reads, store-be's verticals (6) + stores upload, support-be's 15. **Three reasoned exclusions, not oversights:** `document-notification`'s four routes return `JSONResponse` to control status per branch and FastAPI does not validate a returned Response; store-be's `create_order` answers with one of two genuinely different shapes and a `Union` would let Pydantic drop the storefront tracking number; 34 `/health` + 33 cache PATCHes are contract-free. |
| TSR-312 | **The closing's expected amounts have never been measured, and every difference read as a surplus** | Open (made visible, not fixed) | Found 2026-09-19 during TSR-311 | `ClosingRepository.calculate_expected_amounts` queries a `sales_orders` table that **does not exist** — `to_regclass('sales_orders')` is NULL in dev — so its except branch is the only one that has ever run, returning zeros with a log line calling that "expected". `closings.cash_difference` is a GENERATED column (`declared_cash - expected_cash`), so a zero expectation reports the cashier's entire declared cash as a **surplus**, on every closing. dev holds 0 closings, so nothing has been mis-reconciled yet — the first one would have been. It now returns `ExpectedAmounts` with `available: false`, so "no source" and "this till expected nothing" are different values, and `closing_service` logs loudly when it writes such a closing. **The real fix needs the payment split, which lives on documents in sales-be** — no table store-be owns has it, and the cross-service read is exactly what TSR-289 forbids. Decide between a sales-be aggregate endpoint and an event-fed projection before anyone runs a real closing. |
| TSR-313 | **Catalog imports had no console, and the admin tables had three different paginations** | **Done (code + deployed)** (2026-09-19) | Owner requests 2026-09-19 | data-be's three Excel importers (CABYS ~20k rows, locations, pharmaceutical forms) were reachable only by hand-crafting a request, though the admin generator had always swept them in — the normal data gateway is GET-only, so that edge is the only one that can run a mutation. New `/admin/imports` module with one card per importer, each rendering its own result shape because each counts different things and "0 new, 19 842 updated" is the answer that says the file was already loaded. CABYS also gained the `ImportResultDTO` the other two already had. **Pagination standardised:** `Organizations` paged on the server, `DataCatalogs` on the client with inline arithmetic, and `Tickets`/`Users`/`Incidents`/`Audit` not at all — `Incidents` narrowed its list from a `limit` filter inside the row detail modal, discoverable only after opening a row. One `useTablePagination` + `TableFooter` now serves all of them, `DataCatalogs` folded onto it, and `Organizations` deliberately keeps server paging (it is the only endpoint reporting `total`). **`atLimit` is the honest part**: the support endpoints are limit-capped, not paginated, so the footer says when a view is cut short rather than letting "1-24 of 200" read as a total. `api/template.yml` regenerates byte-identical (135 paths / 233 operations). |
| TSR-308 | **The Cognito `CustomMessage` trigger killed a real registration — again (TSR-137 follow-up finally closed)** | **Done — deployed + verified** (2026-09-19) | Owner report 2026-09-19 | A sign-up at 13:39 failed and left `johnny.campos@gmail.com` UNCONFIRMED in `tsuru-user-pool`, unable to finish and unable to reset. The Lambda **never ran**: no log stream for that minute, no `Invocations` datapoint, only an `Errors` datapoint — and its last successful invoke was **29 days earlier** (2026-08-21). That is TSR-243's reclaimed-image-cache failure, confirmed by a probe 34 min later succeeding on a cold init (`Init` ≈3 s). On a CustomMessage trigger that failure is not a retryable API call: Cognito cannot build the verification mail, so sign-up dies with the user half-created. **This function was the only `x86_64` Lambda in an otherwise `arm64` fleet**, and that same mismatch had already broken sign-up once (TSR-137, `Runtime.InvalidEntrypoint`); the open follow-up in §7 step 11 is now closed by removing the odd one out. Fixed in `chepelcr/tsuru-cognito-templates` (cloned to `be/cognito-templates`, gitignored): `Architectures: [arm64]` + `--platform linux/arm64` via buildx + native `ubuntu-24.04-arm` runner; a `KeepWarmRule` (`rate(5 days)`, `{"warmup": true}`) with the handler short-circuit, the same pattern as the 43 image Lambdas in data-be/sales-be/store-be — this trigger needs it **more** than they do, since it only runs when somebody signs up; and log retention 7 → 30 days (the fleet standard since 2026-09-11 — 7 days is shorter than the gap in which this failure happens, which is why the August logs were already gone). SAM-validated, handler smoke-tested. Commit `942a06e`. **Deployed 2026-09-19** and verified live: `Architectures: ["arm64"]`, `tsuru-dev-cognito-templates-keepwarm` ENABLED on `rate(5 days)` with `Input {"warmup": true}`, retention 30, warm ping → `{"status": "warm"}`, and a synthetic `CustomMessage_SignUp` rendering "Tsuru — Confirma tu cuenta" in ES with `{####}` intact. **The deploy had its own trap, worth knowing:** the first run's `KeepWarmRule` failed `events:DescribeRule` — the repo's OIDC role had never needed EventBridge because the stack had never owned a rule — and CloudFormation **rolled the whole stack back, reverting the architecture change with it**, so a half-failed deploy left the function x86_64 again. Note it is `DescribeRule`, not `PutRule`: CFN reads the rule back after creating it, so a write-only grant is not enough. Scoped `events-keepwarm` policy added to `cloudformation/deploy-role.yml` (commit `fa02dfa`); role stack deployed with an admin profile, then the Lambda stack by hand, then CI re-run **fully green** incl. the SES-template upload that had never reached that step. Pairs with the SES sandbox blocker in §7 step 2, which is the *other* reason that address gets no mail. |

## 5. Phase plan (Now / Next / Later) and dependencies

### Dependency spine (load-bearing orderings)

```
RBAC code (TSR-020..025, DONE) ─▶ migrate+seed (TSR-026) ─▶ enforce flip (TSR-027) ─▶ Python-side enforcement (TSR-030/031)
                              └─▶ platform_admin gate + BE-connected admin pattern (TSR-025)
                                       └────────────────▶ fairs admin "Ferias (online)" (TSR-063)
fairs-be skeleton (TSR-062) ─▶ admin vertical (TSR-063) ─▶ merchant vertical (TSR-064) ─▶ public SPA (TSR-065) ─▶ trueque (TSR-066) ─▶ launch (TSR-067)
rebrand R1 content (TSR-041) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────▶ fairs public launch messaging (TSR-067)
API GW authorizer check (TSR-028) ─▶ any hosted landing-admin build (TSR-025 hosted, TSR-063)
userId↔sub gateway mapping (TSR-029) ─▶ production trust in the whole O1–O15 surface
repo split (TSR-090) ─▶ clean homes for POS/landing/fairs work; mirror step (TSR-100) interim
```

Key dependency statements:
- **RBAC `platform_admin` gates the Tsuru admin.** The landing admin's BE-connected sections (RBAC today, fairs tomorrow) are only safe behind `requirePlatformAdmin` + the gateway authorizer (TSR-026/028 before any hosted admin build).
- **The admin BE-connection pattern (TSR-025) gates the fairs admin (TSR-063).** TSR-025 established the layering (admin-api → repository → service hooks → gated pages, `online:true` manifest rows, tree-shake intact) that fairs admin pages replicate against `VITE_FAIRS_API_URL`.
- **Rebrand content (TSR-041) gates fairs public launch messaging (TSR-067).** The `/ferias` page must already be honest-roadmap-framed so the launch flip is "en construcción → live", never "false present tense → live".
- **Fairs reuses POS theme + catalog plumbing** (theme registry from `themes.ts`, product picker over the shared `products` table) — coordinate with the storefront collapse (TSR-083) so the 28-token registry stays single-sourced.
- **Trueque (TSR-066) is inside fairs**, after the merchant + public verticals exist (offers need stands and a floor).

### NOW (0–3 months) — finish what this session started; stop contradicting

| Order | Items | Notes |
|---|---|---|

| 3 | TSR-041/042/043 (rebrand execution R1→R3), TSR-046 | Content-first; the DXP was built for this. |

| 5 | TSR-002, TSR-003 (fiscal secrets + realm) | Top-10 #2 — currently UNOWNED; assign before anything else ships publicly (§6). |
| 6 | TSR-062, TSR-063 (fairs M1–M2) | Can start in parallel once TSR-026 lands (admin pattern proven). |
| 7 | TSR-027 (enforce flip; TSR-035 already superseded by TSR-037) | After the catalog reseed + POS roles UI in real use. |

## 6. Open decisions & risks no workstream covers yet (anti-silent-drop list)

Carried from the audit; **none of the four 2026-06-11 workstreams owns these.** Review this section on every roadmap update.

| # | Risk / decision | Severity | Tracked as | Why it can't wait silently |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | **Plaintext fiscal credentials** (P12 certs, PINs, ATV passwords in Postgres, returned by GET) + staging-hardcoded Hacienda realm | Critical (legal) | TSR-002, TSR-003 | Combined with the remaining authz gaps, any authenticated user could read another org's tax-authority credentials. Top-10 #2 and still unowned. |
| R2 | **Duplicated fiscal truth** — two consecutive allocators, two sale entities, three tax engines synchronized by convention | Critical (legal) | TSR-004 | Drift produces legally wrong invoices. Top-10 #3, unowned. |
| R3 | **markets-api gateway lacks the documented userId↔sub check** — the new RBAC `attachUserId` trusts a gateway validation that doesn't exist yet | Critical (security) | TSR-029 | The entire O1–O15 surface assumes it in production. Cheap (gateway template change) but unassigned. |
| R4 | **Authorization on the Python services** (sales-api/orders-api/data-api have no membership checks; lambda-authorizer TODO stub) | Critical (security) | TSR-030, TSR-031 | Explicitly out of scope of the Express RBAC contract; fiscal documents + ATV credentials are the exposed data. |
| R5 | **Consumer-order build-vs-relabel decision** (promise #6) | High (product) | TSR-081 | R1 fixes the copy; the product decision is still open. |
| R6 | **Monetization narrative before Stripe** | High (brand) | TSR-084 | Activating the dormant Stripe scaffold without the published solidarity pricing model detonates the 4× "gratis, sin cargos ocultos" promise. |
| R7 | **Cross-docking org fabrication** (`_sync_organization`, `owner_id='system'`) keeps `organizations` dual-mastered until TSR-093 | High (data integrity) | TSR-093 | Every new fairs/RBAC feature that reads `organizations` inherits the polluted rows. |
| R8 | **Hosted landing-admin exposure**: the admin shell has no auth of its own; safe only as dev-only/tree-shaken or a PRIVATE build (`VITE_ENABLE_ADMIN=true` never in the public Pages workflow) | High (security) | TSR-025 follow-ups, TSR-028 | The RBAC pages enforce server-side, but a leaked hosted admin build would still expose the shell + other dev-only sections. |
| R9 | **Fairs spec open questions** (spec §13): public hostname timing; one-side vs two-side barter "completed" confirmation; per-fair vs global category taxonomy; stand slug collision policy | Medium | TSR-062 inputs | Must be answered in the fairs M1 implementation workflow; defaults are written in the spec. |
| R10 | **Repo-split limbo divergence** — every monorepo edit (including this session's POS + landing changes) risks silent drift until TSR-090/TSR-100 | Medium | TSR-090, TSR-100 | Guardrail is a CLAUDE.md convention only. |
| R11 | **Drizzle journal anomaly** (`0000_swift_prodigy` without a .sql file) can make `db:migrate` error | Medium (ops) | TSR-102 | Workaround documented (§7 step 1); reconcile properly before the next generated migration. |
| R12 | ~~System `admin` role template can't reach the roles UI under enforce mode~~ **Resolved by TSR-037** — reseed executed 2026-06-11; verified the admin template holds 26 grants incl. the `admin` module | Closed | TSR-035/TSR-037 | TSR-027 unblocked. |
| R13 | **Tier 2 Bribri cultural assets** must stay blocked without RIBCA partnership — a future contributor could "helpfully" ship the cacao logo | Medium (ethics/brand) | TSR-045 | The gate is documented in the rebrand plan §1.3; this row keeps it visible. |
| R14 | **Email-verification + invitation UX holes** (decline no-op, dropped redirects) erode the funnel quietly | Low-Med | TSR-012 | |

## 7. Pending manual steps (updated 2026-09-19)

The earlier list (migrate, seed, **catalog reseed**, commit, mirror, swagger) is **done**: migration + seeds + org-modules backfill applied; the TSR-037 catalog reseed executed and verified against the live DB on 2026-06-11 (new 6-module catalog live); everything committed and pushed (monorepo `develop`→`main` AND the three standalone repos); the GH Actions Lambda deploy ran green. What actually remains, in order:

1. ~~**Disable/remove the obsolete monorepo CodePipeline stages + buildspecs**~~ — DONE 2026-06-20 (TSR-114/TSR-090): the CodePipeline template, pipeline-roles, deploy-pipeline scripts and all 8 root buildspecs were `git rm`'d (commit `001d965`). Every repo deploys via its own GitHub Actions now.
2. **Request SES production access** (account `947999370977`, us-east-1) — the account is in the SES **sandbox** (`ProductionAccessEnabled: false`, 200 mails/day, 1/s, verified 2026-09-19), so Cognito verification/branded emails (TSR-115/116) only reach **verified** recipients. Until then, test with a verified address, or add the tester's address first:
   ```
   aws sesv2 create-email-identity --email-identity <address> --profile PACIFIC-PROD --region us-east-1
   # the recipient must then click the AWS verification link before any mail reaches them
   aws sesv2 list-email-identities --profile PACIFIC-PROD --region us-east-1   # who is verified today
   ```
   **This is a live QA blocker, not a theoretical one.** On 2026-09-19 a tester could not register with `johnny.campos@gmail.com`, which was not a verified identity — it was **added the same day and is `PENDING` until he clicks the AWS verification link**; nothing reaches him before he does — so even after the TSR-308 trigger failure is fixed, that address receives nothing. Currently verified: the `jcampos.dev` domain plus `chepelcr@outlook.com`, `chepelcr70@gmail.com`, `Ignaciodmurilloz@gmail.com`, `enrikesoto38@gmail.com`, `jc_charpa@hotmail.com` (+ `johnny.campos@gmail.com` PENDING since 2026-09-19). **Every new QA tester needs their address added here first**, which is the strongest argument for getting out of the sandbox.
2. **API Gateway authorizer verify (TSR-028)**: after the manual TSR-274 deploy, confirm `/api/admin/*` exists only on `admin-api.tsuru.jcampos.dev` with the dedicated admin-pool authorizer, while org-scoped `/rbac/*` remains on the normal gateway with the customer/POS authorizer. The generated normal endpoint manifest already contains 0 admin routes; retain a live negative probe before rollout.
3. **userId↔JWT-sub mapping at the gateway (TSR-029)** — load-bearing for trusting O1–O15 in production.
4. **Enforce flip (TSR-027)**: after real use of the POS roles UI, set `RBAC_ENFORCEMENT=enforce`; consider making POS `usePermissions` fail-closed at the same time. TSR-035 is already satisfied by the realigned catalog.
5. ~~**Deploy the dedicated admin control plane (TSR-039/274)**~~ — **DONE 2026-09-17 (TSR-278..280)**, against `dev` / PACIFIC-PROD. Live now: `tsuru-dev-admin-cognito`, `tsuru-dev-admin-api` at `admin-api.tsuru.jcampos.dev` (129 paths / 227 operations), `tsuru-dev-admin-dashboard-params` (all 5 required SSM values sourced from the two stacks by `Fn::ImportValue`, not copied), `tsuru-dev-support-api-lambda` + `tsuru-dev-support-api-api-gateway` at `support.tsuru.jcampos.dev`, and `tsuru-dev-admin-dashboard-hosting`. The dashboard is **no longer local-only**: it deploys itself from GitHub Actions to `admin.tsuru.jcampos.dev`. The three deploy-layer traps that had blocked this (a relative `sam --config-file`, a paginated `list-exports` yielding a multi-line pool id, and SAM authorizing CORS preflight) are fixed in the scripts and generators. Still never re-add `/api/admin/**` to the normal management gateway.
   - **First administrator created** 2026-09-17 in the admin pool (`chepelcr@outlook.com`, CONFIRMED with a permanent password).
   - ⚠️ **Admin MFA is OFF, by owner decision 2026-09-17 "for now"** — this weakens TSR-039's isolated-administrator posture, which specified mandatory TOTP. It is a `MfaEnforcement` parameter on `fe/dashboard/cloudformation/admin-cognito.yml` rather than an edited literal, so **restoring it is one word**: `bash fe/dashboard/deploys/deploy-admin-cognito.sh <env> <profile>` with `MfaEnforcement=ON`. Each administrator then enrols on their next sign-in (`Login.tsx` already handles `CONTINUE_SIGN_IN_WITH_TOTP_SETUP`). `AllowAdminCreateUserOnly` and the 14-character mixed password policy are unchanged. **Re-enable before this pool guards anything in stag/prod.**
   - **Remaining:** re-run the AppSync Events stack with `AdminUserPoolId` if the admin pool id ever changes (the current stack already trusts it), and repeat the whole plane for `stag`/`prod` with `pnpm run deploy:admin-control-plane -- <env> <profile>`.
5b. **Redeploy the admin API gateway whenever a backend's `/api/admin/**` routes change (TSR-289).** `api/template.yml` is COMMITTED because the generator reads the backends from sibling checkouts that do not exist in the dashboard's CI — so a new admin route reaches the gateway only when someone regenerates and runs the stack by hand:
   ```
   cd fe/dashboard && pnpm run generate:admin-api   # needs ../../be/* checked out
   pnpm run deploy:admin-api -- dev PACIFIC-PROD
   ```
   The dashboard's own GitHub Actions deploy publishes the SITE, never this stack. A console page calling a route the gateway does not carry gets a 403 from API Gateway, which reads exactly like an authorization problem and is not one.

5d. **Raise the account's Lambda concurrency quota — it is 10 (TSR-302).** `aws lambda
    get-account-settings` reports `ConcurrentExecutions: 10` and
    `UnreservedConcurrentExecutions: 10` in `947999370977` / us-east-1 — the AWS default for a
    new account, never raised. **Ten concurrent invocations for the whole ecosystem**: every
    Lambda in sales-be, store-be, data-be, management-be and support-be draws from the same
    pool. This is not a theoretical limit; it is already causing failures:

    - It is the entire cause of TSR-302. A card firing 11 parallel requests could never
      succeed, and the way it failed is the dangerous part — API Gateway renders Lambda's 429
      as a **500**, and a gateway 500 carries **no CORS headers**, so the browser reports a
      statusless network error. Nothing appears in the console, nothing appears in the Lambda
      log (the invocation never happened), and the only place the truth exists is the API
      Gateway **execution log**, which is where it was finally found.
    - Any page that fans out — the dashboard's five panels, the POS's catalog warm-up, two
      cashiers checking out at once during a cold start — is one burst away from the same
      symptom, and it will look like a random intermittent frontend bug every time.

    Raise it through **Service Quotas** (`lambda`, *Concurrent executions*, default 1 000) or a
    support case; it cannot be raised with an API call alone on a fresh account. Until it is,
    treat "no more than a handful of parallel requests per screen" as a real constraint and
    prefer one aggregate endpoint — which is the right shape anyway (TSR-299, TSR-302).

5c. **Convert the fiscal credentials in `stag`/`prod` when those environments get rows (TSR-002).** **`dev` is DONE** (2026-09-18): both organizations are KMS-encrypted, nothing plaintext is recoverable from the columns, both certificates still open with their PINs, and the live Hacienda preflight authenticates. For a new environment:
   ```
   cd be/sales-be
   bash deploys/deploy-fiscal-credentials-key.sh <env>       # the CMK
   STAGE=<env> PYTHONPATH=shared python3 -m alembic upgrade head   # widens the columns
   python3 scripts/reencrypt_fiscal_credentials.py <env>     # dry run
   python3 scripts/reencrypt_fiscal_credentials.py <env> --apply
   ```
   Order matters: the key first, then the migration (KMS ciphertext does not fit `varchar(255)`), then the Lambdas carrying the new readers, and only then the backfill — converted rows are unreadable to older code. ⚠️ **The trap, in case it recurs:** the legacy suffix is only strippable when the security-key secret resolves, which it usually does not; the signing pipeline recovered it by brute-forcing the PIN boundary against the P12. Converting without that stores credentials **Hacienda answers 401 for while the database looks perfectly correct**. The script now handles it, and re-converts a row that carries a suffix inside its ciphertext rather than skipping it as "already encrypted".

6. ⚠️ **Run `scripts/migrate-db.sh upgrade head` as part of EVERY support-be rollout to a new environment.** `deployment-map.json` sets `databaseMigrations.automatic: false`, so no deploy script runs it, and nothing fails loudly when it is skipped — dev ran for a day with `audit_records` missing (TSR-281): three admin endpoints 500'd and the SQS consumer dead-lettered every audit event from all four backends. `dev` is now at `b8538a2a34fc`; **`stag` and `prod` have never been migrated.** Run it from `be/support-be` with `AWS_PROFILE=<profile> SSM_BASE_PATH=/tsuru/<env>/support-api ENVIRONMENT=<env>`. Worth adding a preflight check to `deploys/deploy-support-control-plane.sh` so a missing schema fails the rollout instead of producing a Lambda that 500s.
7. ~~**Two duplicate `tsuru-dev-admin` Cognito pools exist**~~ — **DONE 2026-09-17.** `us-east-1_5CnUibRNa` was an orphan from the earlier failed admin-cognito attempt: the template sets `DeletionPolicy: Retain` on the pool, so a rolled-back stack leaves it behind owned by nothing. Verified unreferenced before deleting — 0 users, **0 app clients** (it never finished provisioning), no Cognito domain, no owning stack, no CFN export, no SSM value, and `tsuru-dev-appsync-events` trusts `krsBO6Gjd`. Deletion protection was `ACTIVE` and had to be cleared first. The live pool, its administrator and all four stacks verified healthy afterwards. **Watch for this again:** every failed `admin-cognito` deploy leaves another retained pool.
8. ~~**Landing admin env (private builds only)**~~ — SUPERSEDED by TSR-274/280. Do not reconnect the legacy landing-admin surface; use `fe/dashboard` (now online at `admin.tsuru.jcampos.dev`) with its dedicated admin Cognito/API variables instead.
9. ~~**Repo-split mirroring (TSR-100)**~~ — ENDED 2026-06-12: TSR-090 untracked the folders; the standalone repos are the only home for POS/landing/server changes. No more mirror commits.
8. ~~**Re-run the `tsuru-sales-be` API Gateway regeneration**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-06-13. Root cause: the `/organizations/{id}/configurations` + `/media` routes (in the `organization-configurations` lambda) 404'd because the GH Actions workflow had drifted into a manual, single-service, **skippable-API** deploy, and the 2026-06-12 run was dispatched with `deploy_api=false` (lambda updated, gateway never regenerated). Deeper cause: the workflow no longer matched the old CodePipeline. **Fix (commit `fc92eab`):** `deploy.yml` rewritten to replicate the CodePipeline 1:1 — auto-trigger on push to `develop`, three sequential stages (`pipeline-build.sh` → `pipeline-update.sh` → `pipeline-api.sh`, all services), API-Gateway regen **always** runs (skip toggle removed). The first push-run (`27470473107`) then failed at build because the all-services build hit a **pre-existing broken `document-pdf-generator`** (wkhtmltopdf install → exit 127), which skipped update+api. Disabled that service in the build/update lists (commit `8bec33f`, same convention as `infrastructure-service-provider`; it's SQS-only, not in the gateway). Re-run `27471310518` went **fully green** — gateway regenerated with `organization-configurations` wired in; live probe `GET /organizations/{id}/media` now returns **401 (auth required), not 404**. **Bugs fixed + verified.** Follow-up: fix `document-pdf-generator`'s Dockerfile wkhtmltopdf step to re-enable it. See memory `sales-be-api-gateway-deploy-gotcha`.

11. ~~**Deploy the Cognito-trigger fixes (TSR-308)**~~ — **DONE 2026-09-19.** Live and verified: arm64, keep-warm rule ENABLED, 30-day retention, warm ping and a real `CustomMessage_SignUp` render both answered by the arm64 image; CI green end to end. The deploy role needed an `events-keepwarm` policy first (`events:DescribeRule` denied → rule failed → **stack rolled back and un-did the arm64 switch**), deployed with an admin profile. *Original step:* push `chepelcr/tsuru-cognito-templates` to `main` (TSR-308). The code is committed locally at `be/cognito-templates` (`942a06e`) and **not pushed**: arm64 everywhere (`Architectures`, `--platform linux/arm64` via buildx, native `ubuntu-24.04-arm` runner), the `KeepWarmRule` + handler short-circuit, and 30-day log retention. The push runs the whole workflow (build image → deploy stack → upload SES templates); afterwards confirm with `aws lambda get-function-configuration --function-name tsuru-dev-cognito-templates --query Architectures` (expect `["arm64"]`), `aws events list-rules --name-prefix tsuru-dev-cognito-templates` (expect the `-keepwarm` rule ENABLED), and one synthetic `CustomMessage_SignUp` invoke returning rendered HTML.

14. **Stand up the notification centre (TSR-238/239)** — the code is written; none of it is live. There is a script for it:
    ```
    bash deploys/deploy-notifications.sh dev PACIFIC-PROD --plan   # shows the phases, touches nothing
    bash deploys/deploy-notifications.sh dev PACIFIC-PROD
    ```
    It runs, in the order the dependencies demand: **migrate** (alembic → `l2m3n4o5p6q7`, creates `app_notifications` + `user_notifications`) → **appsync** (`cloudformation/appsync-events.yml`, stack `tsuru-dev-appsync-events`, writes `/tsuru/dev/notifications/appsync/events/*` and exports `tsuru-dev-events-api-arn`) → **lambda** (`user-notifications`) → **validator** (redeploy: its role gained `appsync:EventPublish`) → **api** (gateway regen — the new routes exist only in a regenerated template). Every phase has a `--skip-<phase>` so a partial re-run is cheap.

    **The container image is NOT built by the script.** `user-notifications` is in `scripts/pipeline-build.sh`'s `SERVICES`, which is the single source of the CI matrix, so **pushing the branch builds and pushes it**. The script checks ECR for `tsuru-user-notifications-ecr:latest` and skips the Lambda phase with an explicit message rather than pointing a stack at a tag that is not there. So: push first, then run the script.

    **Status (2026-09-11):** migrate ✅, appsync ✅ (incl. the `events.tsuru.jcampos.dev` custom domain), validator ✅ — all verified live. `develop` pushed at `7da8379`, so CI is building the `user-notifications` image.

    **The remaining two steps are ordered, and the order is not obvious.** CI's `deploy-api` job regenerates the gateway from `deployed_lambdas()`, which skips functions that do not exist yet — so the push's own API regen will NOT contain the notification routes. After the image lands:
    ```
    bash deploys/deploy-notifications.sh dev PACIFIC-PROD \
      --skip-migrate --skip-appsync --skip-validator --skip-api   # creates the function
    gh workflow run deploy.yml --repo chepelcr/tsuru-sales-be --ref develop   # regen, now with its routes
    ```
    (`pipeline-update.sh` also skips a function that does not exist, so the Lambda stack has to be created by SAM once; CI maintains it from then on.) The API regen belongs in CI rather than locally: the swagger refresh needs every service's Python deps, and `gen_api_template.py` now aborts rather than emit a gateway missing those services' routes (TSR-244).

    ~~Afterwards, publish the events endpoint for the POS build.~~ **DONE 2026-09-11** — `/tsuru/dev/platform/appsync/events-url` is live, and the POS workflow reads it over OIDC (no repo variable). For a new environment: `bash deploys/deploy-params.sh <env> --events-api-url <url>` (the custom domain is the default, so the flag is usually unnecessary). Until the parameter exists the workflow warns and builds without live notifications: the bell still lists what its one hydrate fetch loaded, it just does not update until a reload.
