Existing orgs carry vertical modules that the old business-type sync **disabled**; the backfill now re-enables them as well as inserting what is missing. Without it the ungating is only true for orgs created after the deploy. Then deploy `management-be`.

16. ~~**Redeploy the data-be Hacienda services to activate keep-warm + logging (TSR-243, TSR-168)**~~ — DONE 2026-09-11: push run `34559744590` shipped the images, then `bash deploys/deploy-sam-stacks.sh dev PACIFIC-PROD` deployed 34/34 stacks and created the 33 `*-keepwarm` rules (verified ENABLED on `rate(5 days)`, warm ping returns `{"status": "warm"}`, images not rolled back). Original note: The `KeepWarmRule` resources exist in 33 `app/*/cloudformation/lambda.yml` templates but no EventBridge rule exists in the account yet, so the 500-on-first-request-after-idle is still reproducible on every idle service. Each service deploys with `sam deploy --config-env dev` from its `app/<service>/` directory (profile `PACIFIC-PROD`), or by pushing `tsuru-data-be` so the GH Actions 3-job flow rebuilds the images and updates all stacks — the handler short-circuit ships **inside the image**, so a CFN-only update would create rules whose pings fall through to Mangum and 500. Verify afterwards with `aws events list-rules --name-prefix tsuru-dev-hacienda` (expect 33) and by confirming an INFO line appears in a service's log group.

16. **Apply the `reports/historical` RBAC move outside dev (TSR-257)** — per environment other
   than dev, which is already done by hand. `npm run db:seed` in `management-be` adds the
   `reports/historical` + `reports/iva` submodules and their `submodule_actions`, but
   `seedRolePermissions` **skips any role that already has rows**, so the two grant changes do
   not ship with it and must be applied directly: delete the stale `documents/historical`
   submodule, replace `staff`'s module-wide `reports` grant with `reports/general` +
   `reports/iva` reads, and insert `admin` → `reports/historical` → `update`. Then dump the
   effective matrix and confirm `historical` resolves read+export for owner/admin/manager,
   `update` for admin, and **nothing** for staff. Skipping the staff half is the dangerous one:
   a module-wide `reports` row re-expands onto `historical` and hands every cashier the whole
   taxpayer history.

17. ~~**Repair the poisoned products and orders (TSR-262)**~~ — **DONE 2026-09-13** against dev
    with `PACIFIC-PROD`: 5 products and 44 orders repaired, both scripts re-run clean. Keep the
    recipe for other environments. **Products first**, because the order backfill reads them.
    Dry-run is the default; inspect, then `--apply`.
    ```
    # be/store-be
    STAGE=dev AWS_PROFILE=<profile> AWS_REGION=us-east-1 \
      python scripts/backfill_product_fiscal_defaults.py --dry-run    # then --apply
    STAGE=dev AWS_PROFILE=<profile> AWS_REGION=us-east-1 \
      python scripts/backfill_order_line_calculations.py --dry-run     # then --apply
    ```
    The order backfill now prints a `fiscal:` line per field it would fill, and counts a repair
    as a change even when the money lands on the same figure — filling in a missing CABYS is
    what makes the order billable and moves no total. Narrow with
    `--organization` / `--document` / `--limit` while checking the result.

    That gap is closed: `repair_tax_rows` fills a MISSING rate code on an existing IVA row (not
    only products with no taxes), and the order backfill applies the same repair to each line's
    own stored tax rows — without which it failed on 16 orders, because the new guardrail
    rejected the very shape it was trying to fix. A 0% row is reported for manual attention
    rather than guessed, since exento (10), no sujeto (11) and crédito pleno (01) are all 0%.

18. **Deploy the three repos for TSR-258..262** — nothing is live. Push `fe/pos-system` (main),
    `be/store-be` (develop) and `be/sales-be` (develop). No migration is involved: the
    exoneration rides inside the existing `order_line.taxes` JSONB deliberately, so there is

    routes changed.

19. ~~**Run the layer-4 acceptance suite for the fiscal changes**~~ — **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-13** by
   a browser test, at the owner's direction: two real pedidos (2900679388, 7026682727) billed
   through the POS UI, both **ACCEPTED** by Hacienda (consecutives …0238 / …0239), signed XML
   verified for `CodigoTarifaIVA`, `CodigoCABYS`, `UnidadMedida`, commercial `Codigo`,
   `CodigoDescuento`, the goods-vs-services split and `TotalComprobante == TotalMedioPago`.
   Driving the real UI is what surfaced TSR-263, which a JSON suite could not have.
   **Still unproven by any test**: the exoneration path (TSR-124), the reference path (TSR-126),
   `/xml/regenerate` on an ordinary invoice (TSR-261) and the code-08 factor — none of the billed
   orders carried those. The suite recipe below stays for when they need covering.

   Original note: **Run the layer-4 acceptance suite for the fiscal changes (TSR-258..262, TSR-124, TSR-126)** —
    approved by the owner, and **each case emits a real document and burns a consecutive**.
    Preflight first, which emits nothing:
    ```
    # be/sales-be
    ./.venv/bin/python -m tests.local.hacienda_live_runner tests/local/suites/<new>.json --preflight-only
    ./.venv/bin/python -m tests.local.hacienda_live_runner tests/local/suites/<new>.json
    ```
    Seven cases, one variable each: a billed order carrying `rateCode`; code 08 with a `factor`;
    an ordinary line regenerated via `/xml/regenerate`; a line with `codes` +
    `commercialUnitMeasure` + `customsPart`; a Factura referencing a provisional contingency
    comprobante (reference code **05** — not 06, which is *Devolución de mercancía*); a Factura
    with a local exoneration **and** its now-mandatory reference; and a reference with code 17,
    which the old enum rejected. Assert on the **signed XML**, not the API response:
    `CodigoTarifaIVA`, `FactorCalculoIVA`, `BaseImponible`, `IVACobradoFabrica`, `Exoneracion`,
    `Codigo`, `InformacionReferencia`, `TotalComprobante`. Expect **`PROCESSING`, not
    `ACCEPTED`** — Hacienda answers **-37** for this issuer (TSR-216).

    Note the runner's `sourceOrder` mode cannot drive the order→invoice case: it hard-stops when
    the order carries any tax (`hacienda_live_runner.py:586-590`). Author explicit cases, or
    relax that guard now that the mapping exists.

20. **Settle the `referenceTypes` doc-vs-catalog discrepancy (TSR-126 follow-up)** — the catalog
    carries 01-18 + 99 and no 20; the doc's Note 10 table omits 15/18 and its REP block requires
    TipoDocIR 20. Both extras are accepted and named in `reference_code.py`, which is safe
    because the POS does not emit a REP. Resolving it needs
    `ANEXOS_Y_ESTRUCTURAS_V4.4.pdf` and **blocks REP support**.

21. **Roll out TSR-270 and TSR-274..276 (owner deploy layer).** **store-be first, and it is urgent:** its
    Lambda has been failing at import since `c369185`, so every store-be route (orders included)
    returns 500 until the image is rebuilt with the TSR-275 fix. That deploy also publishes the
    error-DTO OpenAPI declaration, so regenerate the gateway spec with it.

    Then run the root manual control-plane entry point:
    `bash deploys/deploy-support-control-plane.sh dev PACIFIC-PROD`. It deploys the support Lambda,
    separate SNS→SQS audit/error planes, customer support gateway, isolated admin Cognito/API and
    dashboard SSM parameters. Before deploying producers, run the support repo's explicit Alembic
    migration and catalog seed; they adopt the existing ticket/catalog tables and add audit/error
    tables. Deploy management/data/sales/store afterward so their request middleware begins
    publishing, then rebuild POS so it resolves `/tsuru/dev/platform/api/support-url`. Do not
    configure the superseded `SUPPORT_INGEST_TOKEN`/`SUPPORT_INCIDENTS_URL`, do not deploy the
    dashboard, and do not reintroduce browser automatic error reporting. Verify: an explicit POS
    ticket/reply; an authenticated admin TOTP login; a data-catalog write; one successful backend
    request in Audit; one forced backend 500 in Incidents with the correct service/code/user/org;
    and no audit/error event for `/health`.


## 8. Changelog

| Date | Change |
| 2026-09-19 (a tester could not create an account — TSR-308/309/310) | **Three independent defects sat between a tester and a Tsuru account, and one of them had already been written down as a follow-up nobody closed.** Reported: the *Crea Tu Tienda* link on the Gourmet Foods example went to a dead domain; registration with `johnny.campos@gmail.com` could not be completed; the password reset for that address sent no mail; and `UsernameExistsException` "no la muestra al usuario". *The registration failure was infrastructure, not the form* — **the `CustomMessage` trigger Lambda never ran** (no log stream for that minute, no `Invocations` datapoint, only an `Errors` one; last successful invoke 29 days earlier, and a probe 34 minutes later succeeded on a cold init). That is TSR-243's reclaimed-image-cache failure landing on a person instead of an API call: Cognito could not build the mail, sign-up errored, and the user row survived UNCONFIRMED — which is why every retry then answered "already exists" and why the reset had no verified email to send to. **TSR-308:** the function was also the only `x86_64` Lambda in an arm64 fleet, the exact mismatch that broke sign-up in TSR-137 and whose follow-up had been open in §7 step 11 ever since; it is now arm64 end-to-end with a keep-warm rule and 30-day retention (`942a06e`, not yet pushed — §7 step 11). **TSR-309:** the POS auth pages sent every error to the notification **bell**, which only `DashboardHeader` renders — `AuthLayout` has none — and passed the raw Cognito string as an i18n *key*; so on all five auth pages a rejected request looked like nothing happening. Mapped errors now render in the form with a recovery button (`608b399`, 449/449 tests, verified live). **TSR-310:** the CTA default `VITE_LANDING_PAGE_URL || 'https://j-markets.jcampos.dev'` shipped because CI never set the variable; fixed in template-gourmet-foods and template-beauty-essentials, with the variable now pinned in both workflows. *Also:* the tester's UNCONFIRMED Cognito user was deleted so QA can restart clean (the database had no row — sync only happens after verification, and a scan of all 11 email columns found nothing). *Deployed the same day:* everything above is live — 12 repos pushed, all their workflows green, and the trigger verified on arm64 with its keep-warm rule. The Cognito deploy earned a lesson of its own: `KeepWarmRule` was denied `events:DescribeRule` (CFN reads a rule back after creating it, so a write-only grant is not enough), and the failure **rolled the stack back and reverted the architecture change with it** — a half-failed deploy that leaves the exact bug you came to fix. *Also:* **TSR-311**, the ecosystem-wide sweep the owner asked for next — no `j-markets`/`jmarkets` URL remains in any shipping file in any repo, and two of the hits were live defects rather than text (the landing featured a template name that no longer exists; the POS installed itself as "JMarkets POS"). *Still open:* **SES is in the sandbox**, so that address receives no mail until it is added as a verified identity — §7 step 2, now written up as the live QA blocker it is. |
| 2026-09-19 (closing the fiscal incidences, and contracts everywhere — TSR-308..313) | **The IVA report reconciles to ₡0.00 now, and the reason it did not was the wrong column.** `base_amount` is an input the validator only permits on two kinds of line; where an ordinary line had one it was a legacy row holding the plain subtotal, and subtotal-versus-BaseImponible differs by exactly the base-building excises. Deriving the excise set from 136 live lines independently produced the same four codes the repo already had documented against Hacienda's `-454`, which is the kind of agreement worth having before touching a tax figure. The summary split that had been NULL on 241 of 241 rows is persisted from the signed ResumenFactura — and its column names turn out to be inverted, with `exempt_*` holding *exonerado*, which the PDF template proves. Four Hacienda enums were corrected against the catalog and the fidelity probe now covers all six (80 → 92); `paymentMethods` was the urgent one, since a Plataforma Digital payment was simply unfileable. `referenceCodes`, which a report claimed was wrong on 12 of 12, was already clean. Typed rows and response models landed across all five backends — 192 data-be routes bound, 37 DTOs round-tripped to prove the wire did not move, and three exclusions kept deliberately because a model bound to a two-shaped or Response-returning route is worse than none. Two findings left open on purpose: the closing's expected amounts have never been measured (TSR-312), and the legacy tax-row translation in store-be is defeated one line later by the DTO's own validators. Plus a console for the three catalog importers nobody could reach, and one pagination where there had been three. |
| 2026-09-19 (the dashboard, the dates, and three things that were asked twice — TSR-295..301) | **Every headline number on the POS dashboard was zero, and none of the three reasons was a missing feature.** It counted through open cashier sessions, so an organization with 45 orders and nobody on a till reported zeros by design; it queried two tables that do not exist in this database and swallowed the failure with a comment calling it expected; and the status tuples I wrote to replace that contained five strings that are not `OrderStatus` values while omitting `shipped`, which is — so a shipped order was in no bucket at all. The sets are derived from the enum now, and a test asserts the three groups partition it exactly. The same class of thing was true of Reportes, which had been rendering ₡0 across the board because it declared camelCase Spanish keys against a snake_case API, and of three panels whose data the server hardcodes to zero — deleted rather than left, along with a ranking column that showed a hardcoded chicken for every product in every business. Then the work the owner asked for: hour/day/week/month granularity with a chosen period; an orders-versus-documents toggle, aggregated by whichever service owns the table, with Hacienda-rejected documents excluded from revenue but counted where an operator can act on them; and scope resolution — the whole business, one session, or only your own rows in it — **enforced server-side, which is the first authorization of any kind in store-be**, reading membership directly and failing closed, verified on the deployed Lambda by asking for somebody else's rows and getting 0 instead of 33. **The order dates became real dates**, and the migration is the part worth remembering: the columns held `DD/MM/YYYY` and `YYYY-MM-DD` in the same varchar, this server's DateStyle is MDY, and 12 of 45 rows have a day ≤ 12 — so a bare cast would have silently moved a quarter of the table to a different month. Matching each row's shape gave 0 mismatches against a pre-migration snapshot. Five private date formatters on the frontend would each have broken on the ISO switch, two by rendering the raw string and three by showing the previous day. That unlocked rescheduling a delivery from the order page instead of deleting the order and re-importing its spreadsheet — which only works because the edit rewrites the spreadsheet too, proven by having the reprocess parser read the new date back out of it. Also: 16 TypeScript errors in `fe/pos-landing` that survived because the root tsconfig makes `tsc --noEmit` a no-op that exits 0, one of them a type declaring a shape the data has never had; and a pass over the checkout drawer, where the order number, the delivery destination and the vendor number were each asked twice or not at all, the branch/terminal card was hidden while the payload required it, and the currency select overflowed onto the exchange rate on mobile in some themes but not others because each theme sets its own font. |
| 2026-09-19 (the ledger that only ever looked forward — TSR-305..307) | The owner said the history card showed no rejected documents when they knew there were some. The card was right and the ledger was wrong: the validator's history publish was wired on 13 September, and of the 237 documents issued before it, **none** had a ledger row — including all 39 rejections. The sweep could never have fixed that, because it reads Hacienda's ledger and Hacienda does not list what it rejected, so those 39 existed in exactly one place on earth: our own `billing_sales`. Backfilled 226 rows through the table's single writer. Two findings stayed open rather than being papered over: `billing_sale_summary` is not persisting the merchandise/service split at all (zeros across 190 signed summaries, which also cost the IVA report its authoritative source), and a ₡617 residual on the 13 % bracket where the stored `base_amount` falls short of the base its own tax was computed on. The second one could have been made to disappear by deriving the base as `tax / rate` — which would have made every row reconcile by construction and told the reader nothing. |
| 2026-09-19 (two Reportes surfaces that could never have worked — TSR-302..304) | **Both failures were invisible from the browser, and neither was the code the error pointed at.** The Hacienda history card fired eleven parallel requests to fill eleven numbers; the account's Lambda concurrency limit is **10**, so the eleventh was throttled on every load, API Gateway turned the 429 into a 500, and a gateway 500 has no CORS headers — so the browser could only say "network error", with an empty console. The Lambda logs showed ten invocations, all 200, which is what named the cause. It now answers from one `GROUP BY`, and the request count is pinned in tests on both sides. The IVA report was worse: the POS has shipped the whole D-150 page, seven components and a 353-line contract for an endpoint **that was never built**, and an unmapped gateway path answers 403 — so even the hook's "empty period" path could not fire. It is implemented now, counting only documents Hacienda accepted (that is what TRIBU-CR prefills, and reconciling against that draft is the whole purpose) and leaving every renglón this platform cannot source at zero rather than at a plausible guess, which on a tax report is the one unacceptable answer. Its 30 tests are written longhand from the form. One finding left open: sales-be's `SaleCondition` disagrees with Nota 5 on codes 10-15 — the same shape as the `ReferenceActionCode` divergence, and not something to slip into a report fix. |
| 2026-09-18 (the credentials were never encrypted — TSR-002, TSR-293/294) | **The function was called `encode_string` and the key was called an AES key; neither was true.** What it stored was `base64(value + suffix)`, with a single suffix shared across every row and every organization — so reading the table read the Hacienda password and the certificate PIN, and one known plaintext exposed the suffix for everyone. It was found by accident: a support endpoint returned a stored username, and 14 characters trailed the ATV domain. Its decode was a substring `.replace()` besides, which silently corrupts any secret that contains the suffix. These three columns are the ability to emit legal invoices as the taxpayer. Now KMS — direct for the small secrets, envelope encryption for the PKCS12, which exceeds the 4 KB limit — with each ciphertext bound to its organization as encryption context, verified by moving one between organizations and watching it be refused. The migration was designed not to be a flag day: both formats are version-marked, every reader takes either, and the backfill is a separate dry-run-by-default script that re-opens each certificate with its PIN before committing the row, because the only question that matters is whether the organization can still SIGN. The other half of the ticket — "stop returning them in GET" — was closed the same day: the tenant payload had been handing back the password, the PIN and the certificate, decrypted, on every settings-screen load. Alongside it, the two things the owner asked for: you can download your own .p12 (its own endpoint, logged, PIN deliberately withheld so one intercepted response cannot sign), and the login and register pages now send you to the dashboard instead of showing a signed-in user a form that makes the app look signed out. |
| 2026-09-18 (three failures stacked on one endpoint — TSR-291/292) | **"Orders gives 500, no error DTO, no incident" was three defects deep, and each one hid the next.** The outermost was the error reporter breaking itself: `traceback.format_exception(exc)` is Python 3.10+, store-be is the last service on 3.9, and the call sat in a `finally` — where a raise **replaces** the exception being handled. So the genuine application error was destroyed on its way to the exception handler, and the caller got a bare 500 describing nothing. Under it, the reason the reporter was running at all: `cd-backend-dev-role` had never been granted `SNS:Publish` when the peer roles were, so every audit and incident publish failed. And under *that*, once the mask came off and the real error was finally legible on the first probe: `column organizations.plan does not exist` — the same day's TSR-284 migration, breaking **two services that keep their own model of a table whose migrations they do not own**, neither of which ever read the attribute they were selecting. The fixes are a version-safe formatter, a `finally` that can no longer change a request's outcome (applied to all four copies of the shared file), the missing policy, and both models diffed against the live table to prove `plan` was the only stale column left. The lesson is the middle one: an observability layer that can fail a request is worse than no observability layer, because it deletes the evidence of the thing it exists to record. |
| 2026-09-18 (the console becomes a product, and stops reading other services' tables — TSR-283..289) | **The admin console spent the day becoming the same product as the POS, and ended it giving a table back to the service that owns it.** The POS design system was ported with its Tailwind layer membership intact, and every module got pagination, a filters modal, drawers, card skeletons and autoloading tables; the sidebar had never once responded to the language switch, so the hardcoded strings were swept into the dictionaries. Three model corrections landed underneath the styling. **The plan moved from the organization to the user** — a paid owner holds several organizations and a free owner exactly one, which a column on `organizations` cannot express — with one quota table and a backfill that keeps its own backup. **The Walmart chain fields were mapped correctly all along; the wiring was not**: on a manual order the purchase-order number, delivery point and department are properties of the *order*, so they now become order values instead of document other-fields, they appear only for a customer whose identifier validates against the taxpayer-companies catalog, and the order PDF is generated on manual creation rather than only on import. And *Incidentes (24 h)* had been counting resolved incidents, so the overview never went quiet after a fix. The last change is the one worth remembering: the new organization detail page's Hacienda block was first built by having **management-be read sales-be's `registered_organizations` and `organization_hacienda` with raw SQL** — two services authoritative for one schema, where a rename in sales-be breaks a console page silently, at runtime, with nothing in either repo's CI between the two. Each owning service now answers for its own table, the admin gateway composes those routes, and the console joins them with the POS's own `useFiscalMode` rule so a support agent is never told something the cashier's POS does not believe. Credentials are reported by presence only — the PKCS12, its PIN and the ATV password never enter a response — and `/api/admin/**` is excluded from sales-be's customer gateway, where the POS pool would have authorized it and any signed-in cashier could have read another organization's fiscal wiring by id. |
| 2026-09-17 (the support API was never reachable, and the admin console went online — TSR-277..280) | **Three services were reported as "no response"; none of them was an application bug.** The support Lambda had been healthy all along — `support.tsuru.jcampos.dev` simply had no DNS record, because the rollout's api phase died on a `sam` quirk (a *relative* `--config-file` resolves against the template's directory) while the outer pipeline still reported success. Behind it sat two more deploy-layer traps that each fail an entire API Gateway import: a paginated `list-exports` whose `--output text` returns `value\nNone\nNone`, which CloudFormation happily accepted as a Cognito pool id, and SAM's `AddDefaultAuthorizerToCorsPreflight` default of true, which put the authorizer on generated OPTIONS methods so **every browser preflight was a 401** and the real request was never sent. The admin gateway, deployed for the first time, failed twice more: ~130 OPTIONS methods mapped CORS response headers they never declared, and merging all 30 data services surfaced a genuine `exemptions/{id}` vs `exemptions/{authorization}` collision — API Gateway permits one variable name per path position, and the per-service gateways had never had to care. All five are fixed in the generators and scripts rather than by hand, so the next run cannot repeat them. On top of that the tenant support contract moved to `/api/organizations/{org}/support/tickets` with the caller in the JWT and `organization_id` rejected in the body, support moved from the POS sidebar footer (where a collapsed sidebar hid it) into the account menu, the support page got the horizontal padding every other page already had, and the admin dashboard went **online at `admin.tsuru.jcampos.dev`** with its own OIDC GitHub Actions deploy — taking ownership of its whole control plane, moved out of the root `admin-api/` into per-stack scripts in the dashboard repo. |
| 2026-09-16 (backend-owned support/audit/error plane — TSR-276) | **Replaced the client incident-ingest design before rollout.** A standalone private support backend now owns tickets, catalog data, audit records and backend errors, with two filtered SNS→SQS streams consumed idempotently by one Lambda. Every backend emits its own request audit and 5xx/unhandled error event, including request type, user and organization; the POS no longer reports automatic browser failures and only calls support when the user creates or replies to a ticket. Ticket creation now runs in the shared drawer, uses the styled app select for the affected module, uploads optional image evidence to a private encrypted bucket, and shows a support-specific unavailable state instead of the generic network error. Management's old support entities/routes/migrations were retired while the support migration adopts the live ticket/catalog tables without dropping data. The local-only private dashboard gained audit and signed-evidence views and reads admin configuration from SSM through the root reboot script. Producer IAM references deterministic topic ARNs, so existing backend pipelines can deploy safely before the manually deployed support stack. Root owns the only deployment command; the dashboard has validation CI but no deploy. |
| 2026-09-16 (the admin plane is isolated; legacy dashboard retired — TSR-039/274) | **The platform console no longer borrows the customer identity or normal API edge.** A root-owned, manual-only deployment now creates an admin-create-only Cognito pool with mandatory TOTP and a generated REST gateway that allowlists management `/api/admin/**` plus the data-catalog read/write operations. Management also checks the pool issuer/client from gateway stage variables, so a token accepted by another Tsuru pool cannot be replayed there. The normal management gateway regenerated with **0 admin routes**, while the normal data gateway remains GET-only. The local dashboard now handles first-login password/TOTP challenges and can browse/create/edit/change-status/delete across 30 generated data catalogs. Its migrated organization/POS/CMS/orders/products/customer implementation, legacy documents/templates, npm lock and unused dependency surface were removed. Deployment remains explicitly manual from root; the PACIFIC-PROD check found no existing stacks/domain and confirmed the real `jcampos.dev` hosted zone, catching and correcting the initial zone lookup before rollout. |
| 2026-09-16 (template/org audit + support operations + common backend errors; TSR-270..273) | **Closed the missing-storefront diagnosis and established the common error contract.** Template CMS copy is sector-specific instead of beauty text copied into every storefront, while preserving the section/component contract cloned during onboarding. A PACIFIC-PROD read-only audit proved Pacific Code Labs currently has its complete 6-page/13-section/38-content graph, active CloudFront/Route53 infrastructure, and a 200 org site. The local dashboard became the platform admin surface for orgs/users/tickets/incidents. All four backends share one safe response DTO whose `message` is a stable enum/catalog code. At this checkpoint the source generator documented 47 services / 237 active entries; TSR-276 subsequently moved ownership into support-be, removed client-side automatic reporting, and expanded the current catalog to 48 / 242. All deployment/database/AppSync rollout remains owner-managed and the dashboard remains local. |
| 2026-09-13 (billed through the browser; two documents ACCEPTED — TSR-258..263) | **The fix was proved the only way that counts: two real pedidos billed through the POS UI, and Hacienda ACCEPTED both.** Orders 2900679388 and 7026682727 produced consecutives …0238 and …0239, and the signed XML carries what the whole change was about: `CodigoTarifaIVA` **08** on every line and in the summary rollup — the field whose absence produced the reported error — plus `CodigoCABYS`, `UnidadMedida`, the line's own commercial `Codigo` entries, and `CodigoDescuento` **07** rather than a defaulted regalía. The CABYS `2…` prefix routed both to `TotalMercanciasGravadas` with `TotalServGravados` empty, which is the goods-vs-services classification the summary depends on. `TotalComprobante` equals `TotalMedioPago` to the céntimo, and equals the order's stored grand total — so the cart engine, the order and the document all agree. Before the run, `tax_rate.id` was corrected to carry the Hacienda rate CODE instead of a data-services row id (a reseed renumbers that, and the product form bound its rate selector to it, so a tax with a code and no id rendered unselected); 41 products and 44 orders were repaired again for it. **The browser test then found what no harness would have**: the POS never called `POST /orders/{doc}/invoice`, so a billed pedido stayed billable and a second click would have burned another consecutive for the same delivery (TSR-263). |
| 2026-09-13 (manual order billed end to end; a third document ACCEPTED — TSR-264) | **The manual-order path was driven from the POS for the first time, and it did not work — for a reason no test suite could have reached.** Registering a pedido reported *"Pedido en cola"* and filed it offline while `navigator.onLine` was true and the backend answered curl in 0.45s. The POST carries `Idempotency-Key`, which makes it preflighted, and store-be's gateway did not allow that header — so `fetch` rejected with a bare `TypeError`, which the POS cannot distinguish from an unplugged cable. **A permanent failure was retried as a temporary one, forever, with no error surfaced at any layer.** sales-be's gateway had always allowed it, which is why billing worked in the very session manual orders were silently failing. With the header allowed, the queued pedido replayed on its own and became **PM-000001** — proof the outbox and its idempotency key work as designed. It was then walked pendiente → procesando → enviado → entregado through the UI, *Facturar pedido* appeared at delivered, and it billed to **consecutive …0241, ACCEPTED** by Hacienda: `codigo` 08 and CABYS on both lines, `invoice_sale_id` written back (TSR-263 holding on the manual path too), for a NON-chain customer. Order …0240 was billed the same way in the same run. Three further defects fell out of the same 40 minutes: the delivery card showed the receiver's address over a payload that carried none (only `selectMode` copied it, and neither way into receiver mode goes through it); `canonical_line_dtos` left `tax_rate.id` null where the repair tool writes the rate code; and every pedido displayed both its dates **one day early**, because `YYYY-MM-DD` reaches `new Date()` as UTC midnight — 18:00 the previous day in Costa Rica — in a date formatter that existed twice, so it had to be fixed in two places and had been fixed in neither. |
| 2026-09-14 (the wire converges; three documents ACCEPTED — TSR-265..267) | **Every service now speaks snake_case, and the fiscal matrix was emitted to prove the fix it was built to test.** The run began by rejecting a document: a health-services line sold by `Unid` was filed as merchandise because the summary classified goods vs services by the unit of measure while the spec — and data-be's own CABYS typing — use the **first digit of the CABYS**. The function's docstring cited the right section and implemented a different rule. Re-emitting the identical document after the fix gives the cleanest proof available: **…0001 Rechazado, …0002 Aceptado**, same ₡47 230, with `TotalServGravados` 20 000 and `TotalMercanciasGravadas` 18 000 summing to the unchanged `TotalGravado` 38 000. Two more documents followed, both accepted: the discount cascade (second discount **900**, i.e. 5% of what the first left, not of the original) and the Note 20 routings (regalía → subtotal 0 with **`ImpuestoAsumidoEmisorFabrica` 1950**; `IVACobradoFabrica` priced normally beside it). Alongside, 330 of our own camelCase aliases were deleted across sales-be, and store-be and data-be were finished off — data-be verified by regenerating all 34 swagger specs and confirming **zero camelCase domain properties** remain anywhere. Hacienda's own field names, HTTP headers and the `_type` discriminator keep their aliases by design. Two things the run caught that no harness had: the POS media upload was broken by the backend change (it still sent `fileName` and read `uploadUrl`), and a 20% regalía was refused at validation **before a consecutive was allocated**, because Nota 20 makes codes 01/03 free goods at 100%. |
| 2026-09-14 (validation moves to where it can be acted on — TSR-268/269) | **Two kinds of lateness fixed in one pass.** A product was saved with a 20% regalía, looked entirely reasonable in the catalogue, and was refused at emission because Nota 20 makes codes 01/03 free goods — so store-be now applies the rules sales-be will judge the document by, at the moment the person who can fix them is present. Three more followed it: an unchecked tax code, a code-08 tax with no factor (which prices the line at **zero**, silently), and the transitional rates a product can never legitimately default to. Every one of them is conditional on fiscal data being present — a shop that is not registered with Hacienda still saves products with nothing fiscal on them at all, and that is now pinned by its own test class rather than left to follow. The same day, six payloads that were being parsed as raw dicts got types, worst first: Hacienda's history response had been `**`-splatted into a database upsert, so the dict's keys *were* the repository's keyword arguments; the sales list's `search` filter **silently returned an unfiltered page** when the JSON was malformed; the sweep's continuation cursor lived in three incompatible hand-written schemas; the one untyped body in the repo was an SSRF-shaped callback URL; and the authorization path's dicts turned a missing key into a 500 where a deny belonged. Two of these were caught by existing tests pushing back on changes that were wrong — ISEBA's derived `proportion`, and a date-vs-datetime bound that silently drops a day of results. **Legacy camelCase tolerance was removed entirely**: one language, both directions. |
| 2026-09-12 (the fiscal structure survives the whole trip — TSR-258..262, TSR-124, TSR-126) | **A pedido could not be billed, and the cause was three repos away from the error.** `tax.rate_code is required when tax.code='01'` came from the ORDER path, but the rate code was being destroyed on the PRODUCT: `ProductDetailPage` and `ProductsPage` each carried their own copy of the save mapping, and the detail page's dropped `tax_rate.code`, emitted no `tax_rate` at all unless the catalog id happened to be set, and also lost the unit of measure, the customs part, the editable base and the alcohol proportion. Editing a product from its detail page was a data-loss operation whose damage surfaced weeks later on a different screen, because an imported order line copies the product's taxes verbatim. Both directions are now one function, and the loader had the mirror defect. The same duplication explained the rest: the order→invoice and scan-and-charge paths were two more copies of the stored-tax→document translation and had drifted — no `rate_code` fallback on the order path, the code-08 `factor` dropped by both (so IVARBU priced at zero), and an `as never` cast hiding that `special_fields` nests the per-unit amount on the stored side and flattens it on the document side, so **every specific excise reached the engine with no unit amount**. The order path also defaulted a missing tax type to IVA and a missing discount nature to commercial; both now drop the row, because a totals mismatch is a safe failure and a misdeclaration is not. Per owner decision the guardrail went into store-be, where the data is entered: an IVA-family tax without its rate code is now rejected outright. **The fiscal document was carrying less than the non-fiscal pedido** — `base_amount`, `IVACobradoFabrica`, the line's Nota-6 codes, `customs_part` and two more were declared on the type, captured by the drawer, used for the on-screen total, sent on the order payload, accepted by sales-be, and omitted from the document. **Reprocess could not repair the orders that most needed it**, because phase 2 re-derived money only and money over a line with no tax structure is a no-op; the repair now refills missing fields from the product and says what it changed, the backfill script's divergent 5-dp copy of the allocation rule is gone, and the phase's warning-only `except` no longer returns 200 on an order it failed to repair. Two upstream causes went with it: storefront orders were created with no fiscal data *and* were unrepairable for the same reason, and import-created products had no unit and no taxes — which is how 26 orders came to report zero tax. **`/xml/regenerate` rejected every ordinary invoice**: `_recompute_line` backfilled `base_amount` from the subtotal, and that column means the issuer EDITED the base, which is legal in exactly two cases. Finally, references. Asked to ungate them, reading the spec showed something worse: `ReferenceCode` disagreed with the doc **and** the catalog on eight of fourteen values — 06 is *Devolución de mercancía*, not a contingency substitution — invented two codes, and omitted 17, so a REP was unfilable. Second instance of the TSR-221 pattern, now pinned by a catalog-fidelity probe that was verified by making it fail. References are available on every document and required per the five cases the doc lists, including the FE-with-local-exoneration case that makes exonerations (TSR-124) and references one change rather than two; the per-document restrictions are enforced; and `type`/`number`/`date`/`Razon` moved from XML-build time to step 1, because the old order burned a consecutive to discover a missing field. `Razon` had been papered over with a single space. |
| 2026-09-11 (retention deployed; TSR-252 found) | **TSR-168 fully closed; new TSR-252.** Deployed the retention change: 44 stacks (34 data-be, 9 sales-be, store-be) — **44/44 OK** — taking Lambda and API-Gateway log groups from 7/14 to **30 days**; the account went from `48×7, 7×14, 0×30` to `4×7, 7×14, 44×30`. Applying the last one surfaced **TSR-252**: `management-be`'s lambda template carries a placeholder `Code` stub, so the stack deploy that would have set its retention would instead have silently replaced the live platform API handler with a stub returning 200 and a placeholder body. Caught before running it; retention was set out of band with `put-retention-policy` and the template now carries a warning. The two log groups still at 7 days belong to other projects in the account (fire-code, prestamos), not Tsuru. |
| 2026-09-11 (open items closed) | **Closes the last piece of TSR-168; resolves the TSR-243 collision; TSR-250 root-caused.** *Retention:* Lambda log groups were at 7 days and API-Gateway access logs at 14 — shorter than the window in which these incidents actually surface. The CABYS 500 that started TSR-243 was diagnosed from API-Gateway **execution** logs, and a 7-day window would have lost them. All 46 raised to 30 days (matching `lambda-authorizer`, which already used it); CodePipeline build logs stay at 14, since they are not an audit trail. The groups are tiny (largest under 300 KB) so the cost is negligible. *CI:* `paths-ignore` added to all three workflows — a commit touching only `deploys/**` or docs was rebuilding every service image, 34 of them in data-be. **`scripts/**` is deliberately not ignored**: CI executes `scripts/pipeline-*.sh`, so changes there must still trigger a run (an earlier suggestion to ignore it was wrong). *ID collision:* the POS CABYS-dropdown item was **renumbered TSR-243 → TSR-251**. First assignment wins: the cold-image item took the ID on 2026-09-10 and is referenced in 11 commits across three backend repos, while the POS one was added a day later in `6988bf5`. POS-repo commits still say `[TSR-243]`, so the new row carries a forwarding note. *TSR-250:* the obvious hypothesis was wrong and is now recorded as such — raising sales-api to a full vCPU (1769 MB) left init at `9999.13 ms / timeout`, so it is **not** CPU-bound; the memory was reverted. The surviving explanation is lazy container-image layer loading, which also explains why the re-init inside the invoke takes 3.7s. `consumer-cabys` sits at 8.6s on the same base image, so the fleet runs near the 10s ceiling generally. |
| 2026-09-12 (the browser was throwing the answer away; historical moves into Reportes) | **Completes TSR-255 and TSR-257.** The sweep was working and the POS card was still empty: DevTools showed a **red 200**, which is what a successful response blocked by CORS looks like. `hacienda-history` never added `CORSMiddleware`, and API Gateway's `aws_proxy` integration answers the preflight itself but does not decorate the real response — so the preflight passed, the GET returned 200 with real rows, and the browser discarded the body. Invisible to curl, to the Lambda logs and to the status code; only the browser can see it. The per-app `_configure_cors` was one hardcoded origin list copied per service and already drifting, so the policy now lives once in `jbiller_common/configuration/cors_config.py` (allowlist + `*.jcampos.dev` regex) and both new services call it. **Then the module moved:** the ledger reads as a report, not a sibling of Documentos, so `historicalDocuments` became an item of Reportes on `reports/historical` and `ReportePage` gained a summary card. Worth recording because the move broke RBAC in both directions at once, silently: `staff` held a module-wide `reports: ['read']`, and module-wide grants re-expand over every available submodule on each resolve — so the moment `historical` appeared under `reports`, every cashier could read the whole taxpayer history. Simultaneously `admin` **lost** `update`, the sweep trigger, because module-wide `reports` is read/export only where the old module-wide `documents` grant happened to include update. Neither shows up as an error anywhere; both were found by dumping the effective matrix after the move, which is now the rule for adding or moving any submodule under this catalog shape. Staff is two explicit rows (`reports/general`, `reports/iva`), admin holds `reports/historical: ['update']`. Also added `reports/iva`, in the sidebar for a while and never in the catalog. The card's counts are exact whole-history totals (one `size=1` request per bucket, read off `total_elements`) rather than an aggregate of the current page, which would have reported 20 rows as the ledger; the four ATV verdicts are labelled icon tiles because the status ramp does not separate warning from success under protanopia (dE 3.9 measured), so colour cannot be what identifies a verdict. |
| 2026-09-12 (branch discovery: the constraint was hiding three real branches) | **Extends TSR-254.** With the sweep working, branch discovery still produced nothing, and the per-record logging added earlier is what finally said why: `ValueError: Terminal code 1 is already assigned to another branch`. `terminals` carried a unique constraint on `(organization_id, code)`, but Hacienda numbers terminals **within** a branch — its consecutive is branch(3) + terminal(5) + type(2) + number(10), so the pair identifies the point of sale and terminal 1 can legally exist under branch 1 and branch 14 at once. VILMA CORELLA ARTAVIA has exactly that. The sync behaved correctly (refuse to reassign an existing terminal, roll the message back rather than corrupt the mapping); the schema was wrong, and store-be's own URLs had assumed the looser rule all along. Migration `c2d3e4f5a6b7` moves it to `(organization_id, branch_id, code)`. Two details worth keeping: the model had declared this as a differently-named unique `Index` while the database held a `UniqueConstraint`, so the migration drops whichever it finds rather than assuming; and the downgrade deliberately **fails** if two branches already share a terminal code, because the only ways to satisfy the old constraint are renumbering or deleting a terminal, and both break the fiscal consecutive that terminal owns. After the fix the sweep discovered **three** branches (12, 14, 115) that had been invisible, left the operator's two untouched, and produced six per-branch per-document-type counters without moving branch 1's existing 237. The old cross-branch test asserted the opposite behaviour and was replaced by two that pin the new rule, including that idempotency within a branch survives the loosening. |
| 2026-09-12 (the history sweep runs, end to end; TSR-167/253/254/255/256/257 closed) | **Closes TSR-167, TSR-253, TSR-254, TSR-255, TSR-256, TSR-257.** The first-run Hacienda history sweep completed for the first time: 100 documents for `d09b299b` (VILMA CORELLA ARTAVIA), every one carrying an ATV verdict and a real total, issuer and receiver — so the whole round trip works (sweep → skeleton → document-validator → verdict → enriched row), and `consecutives.current_number` never moved off 237. Getting there cost four fixes that only a live run could surface, and each was invisible in a different way. **The params stack never existed:** `deploy-params.sh` still named it `jcampos-{env}-hacienda-history-ssm-params` after the `/jcampos`→`/tsuru` migration, so `/tsuru/dev/hacienda-history` was empty and the sweep could not resolve database credentials — the failure mode was a Lambda that started, logged its SSM base path, and stopped. **Hacienda answers HTTP 500, not an empty list**, for an offset past the end of a history; with 100 documents at page size 50 the short-page stop never fires, so a sweep that had already committed 100 rows lost the entire record to the DLQ. This is not a sandbox quirk — it hits any taxpayer whose document count is an exact multiple of the page size. **The consumer had no logger**, so that failure produced START/END and a DLQ message with no traceback, indistinguishable from never running; it took reproducing the sweep in-process against real AWS to see it. **And store-be went down in dev:** `Optional[str | int]` in the new branch DTO is unevaluable on its `python:3.9` image, and because `app/main.py` imports the SQS handler at module scope the failed import returned `Runtime.Unknown` for every invocation — the HTTP API included, not just branch discovery. Two further defects were found by review rather than by running: the POS-side `SAVE_HISTORICAL_DOCUMENT` publish sat **inside** `_persistence_scope`, so an SNS outage would have rolled back the Sale verdict, the audit row, the attachment and the bell and skipped GENERATE_PDF — and the test that claimed to cover it stubbed the scope with `nullcontext`, passing while the code had the opposite property; and an RBAC grant backfill shipped as raw SQL, redundant for owner/admin and quietly granting `manager` an `export` it holds nowhere else under `documents`. Both corrected, the 9 stray rows deleted from dev, and the module-wide-grant expansion that makes any backfill unnecessary is now pinned by tests. Also closed: the IVOIS purge, which mattered most in `invoice_footer.html` — every emitted fiscal document carried another company's cédula jurídica. New cross-repo contract test: one byte-identical `SAVE_BRANCHES` fixture asserted from both sides, verified to fail on a field rename, because that event crosses a repository boundary where nothing type-checks it. **Operational note for the next event-source mapping:** sales-be CI runs build → `update-function-code` → API Gateway and never sam-deploys the per-service stacks, so a mapping added to a `lambda.yml` needs `sam deploy` by hand. |
| 2026-09-11 (sales-be logging; TSR-168 closed) | **Closes TSR-168; new TSR-250.** Applied the data-be logging fix to `jbiller_common` (`233fa0c`), closing the half of TSR-168 that lived in another repo. Same two independent causes: nothing called `logging.basicConfig` outside `lambda-authorizer`, so the root logger stayed at WARNING and every `logger.info` across the **87 modules** using plain `logging.getLogger` — `sales_pipeline.py` and `event_publisher.py` among them — was discarded; and `ResponseUtils.handle_exception` re-raised as 500 without logging, so an unhandled bug left its traceback nowhere. The evidence was direct: warm pings from the TSR-243 rollout log `"Keep-warm ping received"` at INFO and **nothing reached CloudWatch**. Wiring had to differ from data-be — `FastApiConfig.__init__` covers six services, but `document-validator`, `document-pdf-generator` and `hacienda-history` build their apps directly and call `configure_logging()` explicitly, placed after their imports so module-level `logger = ...` statements already see the configured root. **Powertools is deliberately untouched**: its `Logger` keeps its own handler and does not propagate, so the 14 modules using it cannot double-log — only the root logger is modified. Verified live on three functions. Verifying it also surfaced **TSR-250**: `sales-api`'s init phase reports `Status: timeout` at 9999 ms, so Lambda abandons initialization and re-runs it inside the invoke — invisible today because the retry succeeds, but every cold start pays init twice. **Still open on TSR-168:** `retentionInDays: 7` on the pipeline log groups. |
| 2026-09-11 (keep-warm across the backends; four dead Lambdas found) | **New: TSR-247/248/249; extends TSR-243 (infra).** Applied the cold-image keep-warm pattern to the remaining backends, and the act of testing it uncovered more than it fixed. **`management-be` was deliberately skipped**: `tsuru-dev-api-handler` is `PackageType: Zip`, which cannot produce the 409 — a rule there would be pure cost against a problem it cannot have (same for `sales-be/lambda-authorizer`). sales-be's nine image functions and store-be's `cd-backend` got the rule plus a handler short-circuit; unlike data-be their handlers were not uniform (mixed signatures, multi-line docstrings, `handler` vs `lambda_handler`, and `document-pdf-generator`'s `CMD` pointing at an alias), so placement was done with `ast` and the entry point resolved through the Dockerfile `CMD`. **A template bug was caught by validation rather than by review**: `infrastructure-service-provider` has a `Conditions:` section between `Resources:` and `Outputs:`, so anchoring the insert "before Outputs" put the rule *inside Conditions*, where a grep for `KeepWarmRule` still passes — only parsing and asserting which section it landed in catches that. **Then the warm pings exposed four Lambdas that had never run at all** (TSR-247): their images copy `jbiller_common`'s code without installing its dependencies, so they died at import on fastapi/redis/pydantic_xml. `hacienda-history` needed three further rounds (TSR-248) — deleted exception modules restored verbatim from `ebfe30a~1`, a `models.base` import that never existed, and four call sites still on the pre-migration `configuration` package; it has not started since that migration, which also means the `document-history.fifo` sweep in TSR-167 was never running. The detection that found the last layer had to be rebuilt: a local package deleted *entirely* is indistinguishable from a third-party import unless every name is resolved against stdlib + requirements + the shared lib's declared deps + the local tree. Also fixed the Windows `py -3` launcher in six deploy scripts (TSR-249) and installed SAM globally. **Deployed to dev**; 10 new `*-keepwarm` rules beyond data-be's 33, all ten functions verified answering `{"status": "warm"}`. **Note: TSR-243 is now assigned to two different items** — this infra one and a POS CABYS-dropdown fix added later in `6988bf5`; both are referenced in pushed commits, so neither was renumbered and an owner should reassign one. |
| 2026-09-11 (events custom domain LIVE; the CAA trap) | `events.tsuru.jcampos.dev` is live on dev — cert ISSUED, DNS on CloudFront, TLS verified, unauthenticated publish correctly 401s — and `/tsuru/dev/platform/appsync/events-url` is published for the POS build (other 13 platform params verified unchanged). **The first attempt rolled back** on a bare "Certificate validation failed with status: FAILED" after two minutes, too fast for a DNS timeout. Cause: `tsuru.jcampos.dev` is a CNAME to `chepelcr.github.io`, and RFC 8659 has CAA lookup walk up the tree *following CNAMEs*, so a new `*.tsuru.jcampos.dev` name inherits GitHub's CAA (sectigo/digicert/letsencrypt) and ACM reads that as "Amazon may not issue here". The tell was already in the zone — every AWS-certified name there carries its own explicit CAA record — so the template now creates one, with the certificate `DependsOn` it. Rollback was clean: the Events API, namespace, authorizer and original SSM params were untouched throughout. Gotcha documented in sales-be CLAUDE.md §6 since it will bite the next cert added to this zone. |
| 2026-09-11 (events custom domain + POS config from SSM) | **New: TSR-245, TSR-246.** The Events API got a name of its own — `events.tsuru.jcampos.dev` — because the generated endpoint is a 26-character hash that gets compiled into the POS bundle, so recreating the API would strand every client until a rebuild. Cert (DNS-validated in-stack), `AppSync::DomainName` + association and a Route53 A-alias all live in `cloudformation/appsync-events.yml` alongside the API, conditional on `CustomDomainName`/`HostedZoneId` so the stack still deploys without them. Checked Amplify rather than assuming: `getRealtimeEndpointUrl` appends `/realtime` for a non-generated host instead of substituting `appsync-realtime-api`, so one HTTP URL drives both endpoints. The **backend publisher stays on the generated endpoint** (SigV4 with no CloudFront hop), so publish and browse now have separate SSM parameters. The POS build stopped carrying a repo variable: the workflow assumes the deploy role and reads `/tsuru/{env}/platform/appsync/events-url`, soft-failing to a no-live-notifications build rather than blocking a release. **TSR-246** fell out of that: `deploy-params.sh` named `tsuru-{env}-jmarkets-ssm-params` while the live stack is `tsuru-{env}-platform-ssm-params`, so it could only ever have created a conflicting second stack — fixed, and given an `--events-api-url` flag. |
| 2026-09-11 (notification centre deployed to dev; **TSR-244**) | **Ran `deploys/deploy-notifications.sh dev PACIFIC-PROD`.** Live on dev: migration `l2m3n4o5p6q7` (both tables, all indexes, the unique recipient constraint), stack `tsuru-dev-appsync-events` (API `eabriwxvynhmhebiw5x752sldq`, Cognito subscribe / IAM publish, `onSubscribe` DIRECT authorizer, endpoints in SSM), and `document-validator` redeployed with `appsync:EventPublish` verified on its role. The authorizer was smoke-tested by direct invoke — own channel allows, another user's channel is refused. Two phases deferred to CI: the **lambda** (waits on the CI-built ECR image — the script checks ECR and skips rather than pointing a stack at a missing tag) and the **gateway regen**, which belongs in CI anyway. **TSR-244** came out of the local run: `gen_api_template.py` regenerates the entire gateway, and a service whose spec fails to build was silently dropped from it (exit 0) rather than stopping the run. This does **not** bite on a push — CI installs every service's requirements and nothing is missing — but it would bite a commit that breaks one service's import, or a swallowed `pip install` failure (the loop ends in `|| true`). Guards added on all three paths plus a zero-path backstop; the locally clobbered `template.yml`/`endpoints.json` were restored from git and the guards verified to leave them untouched. Script fixes from the same run: `shared/` on `PYTHONPATH` for alembic, and the SAM venv on `PATH` for `pipeline-api.sh`. |
| 2026-09-10 (deploy script + CABYS) | **New: TSR-243.** `deploys/deploy-notifications.sh` — one ordered, skippable, `--plan`-able script for the notification centre (migrate → appsync → lambda → validator redeploy → gateway regen), with both Lambda preconditions checked up front (the events-ARN export, and the ECR image) instead of being discovered inside a CloudFormation error. **The image stays a CI artifact**: `user-notifications` is in `pipeline-build.sh`'s `SERVICES`, which is the source of the GH Actions matrix, so a push builds it — the script deploys and never builds, and says so when the tag is missing. Also **TSR-243**: the CABYS dropdown was rendering behind the product drawer (`z-popover` 80 under `z-drawer` 200, both portaled to body) — the same class of bug `SelectField` documents; both pickers moved to `OverlayPortal` at `z-drawer-modal` and to fixed/viewport positioning that survives scrolling the drawer. Verified: POS tsc clean, 256/256, build + style policy green; script `bash -n` clean and `--plan` exercised against the live account. **The deploy itself has not been run** — see §7 step 14. |
| 2026-09-10 (CABYS 500 → cold-image + logging fixes) | **New: TSR-243; advances TSR-168.** A reported 500 on `GET /countries/188/cabys?search=almohada&size=20&type=4` turned out not to be a bug in the CABYS service at all — **the Lambda never ran**. API Gateway's execution log shows the Lambda service returning **409 `CodeArtifactUserPendingException`**, which the gateway converts to a 500 with no retry: `consumer-cabys` is a container-image function that had been idle since its 2026-08-07 deploy (log group **0 bytes, no streams — it had never once logged**), so AWS had reclaimed its optimized image cache. A direct invoke returned 200 with 4 correct rows and `Init Duration: 8607.87 ms`, longer than the 7 s between the user's two attempts, which is why both failed. All 34 data-be Hacienda functions are image-based and 13 more have empty log groups, so this was one cold spell away from happening to any of them. **Fixed both halves.** *Keep-warm:* a `KeepWarmRule` (`rate(5 days)`, constant `Input: {"warmup": true}`) plus its EventBridge permission added to 33 `lambda.yml` templates — `consumer-exchange-rate` skipped, its daily business cron already keeps it warm — and `is_warmup_event`/`warmup_response` (`jmarkets_common/utils/lambda_utils.py`) wired into all 34 handlers **before** any existing routing, so the ping costs one init and no application work. Because a constant `Input` replaces the whole event, a warm ping cannot be confused with the `source: aws.events` cron or an SQS batch — asserted in tests. *Logging (TSR-168):* the reason nothing ever reached CloudWatch was that no code called `logging.basicConfig`, so the root logger stayed at WARNING and every `logger.info` in the shared libs was discarded; separately `ResponseUtils.handle_exception` re-raised as 500 **without logging**, so unhandled bugs left no traceback. New `configure_logging()` (`LOG_LEVEL` override, reuses the Lambda runtime's root handler rather than stacking a duplicate, pins botocore/boto3/urllib3/sqlalchemy to WARNING) is called from `FastApiConfig.__init__`, so all 34 services inherit it; `handle_exception` now logs domain errors at WARNING and unhandled ones at ERROR with `exc_info=True`. 71 files. Verified: 34/34 `app.py` compile, 34/34 templates parse with the keep-warm resources asserted, and the helpers unit-tested across 9 event shapes, both handler modes (local/Lambda), idempotency, and all five exception paths. **Nothing is deployed** — see §7 step 16; `sales-api` and the `document-*` Lambdas use `jbiller_common` in a different repo and still need the same logging change. |
| 2026-09-10 (products get a unit and an IVA; the receiver panel stops erasing itself — TSR-234, TSR-235, TSR-236) | **A product created by an order import has no unit of measure and no taxes**, because the customer's spreadsheet has no fiscal columns — so every invoice line built from one fell back to "Unid" and carried no IVA at all, which is why 26 orders reported `taxes = 0`. Backfilled 41 units and 39 IVA rows (rate from the product's CABYS where it has one, 13% otherwise, never overwriting a configured tax) and re-costed all 42 orders. On the form itself, **the unit-of-measure control was rendered but never connected** — no `value`, and an `onChange` that only flipped local state — so the field could not be set even deliberately. **The product form also computed the same line two different ways on one screen**: one path passed the tax service the PRE-discount price and a discount list the service ignores for routing, so a discounted product previewed the wrong IVA and a royalty never routed to the issuer; `useProductLineAmounts` is now the single path, sharing the cascade-then-tax order with the line-detail drawer and the backend, and the ISEBA alcohol bracket and ISEBEC soap/beverage selection came over with it. **The receiver panel was discarding the selected client** for the same reason the chain card was invisible: a receiver holding nothing but the id-type select's default `{ code: "01", number: "" }` counted as user input, so the identification, the address and the GLN all came back empty — and separately, the id-type reset fired when the catalog loaded rather than when the user changed anything, wiping the code and number of every client whose customer type was not on record. Also corrected order 2900679405 back to `processing` with a new script, since the status machine is deliberately forward-only and a mis-click was otherwise unfixable. |
| 2026-09-10 (order money, DTO alignment, and why the chain card was invisible — TSR-231, TSR-232, TSR-233) | **An order's lines did not add up to its total, roughly half the time.** `_round_money` quantized at 5 dp while its docstring said 2, and order totals were summed from raw line values and rounded at the end — a different number from the sum of the rounded lines. Measured over 3 000 generated multi-line orders with a header discount: 47% diverged. Both sides now own the rule in one place — round each part, then add the ROUNDED parts — and the same 3 000 orders reconcile on every check. The FE mirror shifts through the decimal string rather than multiplying, because `Math.round(1.005 * 100)` rounds down where Python's `ROUND_HALF_UP` rounds up, and a one-céntimo disagreement is a total the two sides cannot reconcile. **The order line now mirrors the document line field for field** (migration `bd0e1f2a3b4c`, applied to dev): `codes`, `unit_measure`, `commercial_unit_measure`, `customs_part`, `base_amount`, `iva_collected_factory`. The codes are the LINE's own — they used to be read off the linked product, whose array holds only the latest import's, so two chains ordering the same product overwrote each other and an order could show a different customer's article code. `unit_measure` is required by Hacienda on every line and was falling back to "Unid". And `iva_collected_factory` was never checked by the FE tax service at all, so a factory-collected line showed the customer paying IVA the document declares the issuer absorbs. **The Walmart card was invisible for a reason no amount of fixing the lookup would have solved**: a client created by an order import has no cédula (the spreadsheet has no such column) and is written with status 0, which also hides it from the POS client picker. Chain matching now accepts any identity the caller holds, and falls back to the only signal that is always present — the client's own registered departments and delivery points. |
| 2026-09-10 (orders and the biller now compute the same money — TSR-229, TSR-230) | **The orders service had its own copy of the discount/tax engine, and it had drifted from the one that actually files documents.** Every divergence was a place where a pedido and the invoice it becomes disagree, so all of them were brought in line with what Hacienda accepted in TSR-224/TSR-226: nature 02 cascades ordinarily, the excises 03/04/05/12 are absorbed by the issuer **per tax row** rather than per line, `IVACobradoFabrica` 01 is honoured, ISEBEC branches on the real CABYS prefix with the beverage and soap formulas the right way round, volumes quantize to the 2 fraction digits the XSD allows **before** the amount is derived from them, and code 99 prices off the subtotal like every other rate-driven code. A separate bug had `_hydrate_tax_jsonb` zipping input DTOs against results returned in Hacienda's evaluation order, writing the ISC amount onto the IVA row of any product that listed its IVA first. **Imported orders are now fully costed.** The customer's spreadsheet has no tax structure and, in practice, no line discounts either — a real Walmart file leaves every line at zero while the header totals one — so the product supplies the taxes, codes, CABYS and net price, the order's discount is recorded as 07 Descuento Comercial, and a header-only discount is allocated across the lines in proportion to gross with the rounding remainder on the largest line so the parts add back exactly. Reprocess recomputes whether or not the spreadsheet is still on file, which turns the button into a repair tool, and `scripts/backfill_order_line_calculations.py` does the same across existing rows. On the POS side, **facturar pedido stopped opening a document tab and a whole point-of-sale screen**: the checkout drawer is now a component both callers share, over the order's own lines rather than a round-trip through the cart store — which had been dropping any line whose product was not in the offline catalog, so the checkout could open showing a total of zero. The Walmart card never appeared for a client picked in the POS because `??` cannot fall through an empty-string identification; it uses the shared `resolveReceiverId` now. Two fiscal fixes surfaced on the way: an ad-hoc cashier discount was being filed as nature 01 Regalía, which declares that the issuer absorbed tax the customer actually paid, and a manual order sent no structured taxes at all, so a POS-captured excise was unrecoverable when the pedido was billed. |
| 2026-09-08 (special tax cases wired end to end — TSR-221, TSR-222) | **The FE could edit a taxable base the backend then ignored, and the two sides disagreed about what three tax codes even are.** sales-be named tax code 07 `OTROS`, gave 08 the description belonging to 07, and named 12 for the regime that is 08 — while the data-api catalog and the v4.4 analysis doc agree with each other and with the FE: 07 is IVA cálculo especial (editable base), 08 the used-goods factor regime, 12 cement. Because 07 was not in `_IVA_CODES`, the one code whose whole purpose is an editable base was rejected as "requires either amount or rate" (**TSR-221**). Underneath that, `base_amount` had been accepted and persisted by the API since the beginning and **never read** — every line was priced off the subtotal, so an edited base silently diverged from what the POS displayed. The doc allows the base to be edited in exactly two situations and neither is a rate: tax code 07, and IVACobradoFabrica "01"; `editable_base_for` now owns that rule and a base supplied anywhere else is rejected up front rather than quietly applied, because a base the issuer picks freely is how VAT gets under-declared. Code 08 was separately computing `subtotal x factor x tarifa` — over-taxing by the whole rate, where the doc says the factor IS the calculation. `iva_collected_factory` was threaded through DTO, ORM (migration `k1l2m3n4o5p6`), mapper and `_LineAdapter`, and `IVACobradoFabrica` — hardcoded to None in the mapper — now reaches the XML (**TSR-222**). Verified live, 2/2 on signed XML: BaseImponible 5000.00000 against a 4333 subtotal, ImpuestoNeto 650.00000, TotalComprobante 4983.00000, with the factory flag present on the second. FE tests pin the same numbers (`specialBase.test.ts`), so the two sides now agree on the special cases as well as the ordinary ones. |
