All eight cleared the full chain — sales-api 201, signed XML in S3, document-validator, PDF generated and verified in S3, Hacienda PROCESSING. The check was made against the **signed XML**, not the API response, because the XML is what Hacienda actually receives: `CodigoTarifaIVA` matched the requested code in all eight and `TotalComprobante` matched the expected total in all eight (4333.00000, 4376.33000, 4419.66000, 4506.32000, 4896.29000, 4354.66500, 4333.00000, 4333.00000). Those are the same per-rate numbers `ivaRates.test.ts` pins on the FE, scaled to net 4333 — so the two sides now demonstrably agree at every rate, not just at 13%. Codes 05/06/07 were deliberately not filed as invoices (TSR-219). |
| 2026-09-08 (every suite ACEPTADO — TSR-224, TSR-225) | **Hacienda now accepts every document the suites emit: discount matrix 12/12, rate matrix 8/8, regression 7/7, final 9/9.** Mapping the rejection reasons (TSR-170) is what made this possible — eleven opaque failures became seven named defects, each then fixed and confirmed by a document the ATV actually accepted rather than by re-reading the spec. Two of them overturned conclusions this session had reached from the prose: natures **01/03 require a 100% discount** (-518), and nature **02 cannot charge the customer on the un-eroded base** (-45 with -454 make it unrepresentable, and -45 named the exact figure it expected), so 02 was dropped from `NATURES_BASE_NOT_ERODED` on both sides of the wire. The recurring shape of the rest was duplication: the IVA family as a literal in four places, the assumed-tax rule known to the line but not the summary, and `BaseImponible` conflated with the base the tax was computed on — each one a place where two pieces of code could disagree about the same fact, and each one a rejection. Also fixed the harness itself (**TSR-225**): it reported the first poll as the outcome, which is what produced the false "everything is stuck at PROCESSING" reading. |
| 2026-09-08 (transitional rates restricted to NC/ND — TSR-219) | **The spec says the transitional IVA rates belong to credit and debit notes only, and nothing was enforcing it.** Nota 8.1 marks rate codes 05 and 06 "Exclusive use NC/ND" and 07 "Disabled / NC/ND only". `TaxValidator` checked only that a rate code existed in the enum, and `TaxService._rate_for` happily prices whatever code it is handed — it reads the code and asks nothing about the document carrying it. So an invoice could go out at transitional 8%, be priced correctly, and be rejected by Hacienda after a consecutive had already been burned. `TaxValidator.validate` now takes `document_type` (keyword-optional, one call site, existing callers unaffected) and rejects 05/06/07 on anything that is not a debit note (02) or credit note (03). Verified as a full matrix across Factura, Tiquete, Nota Débito, Nota Crédito and Exportación: the three transitional codes reject everywhere except the two note types, the other eight accept everywhere. This also settles the FE derivation cleanly — on the documents the POS emits the transitional codes are unreachable, so 8% has no valid code and 4% has exactly one, and `ivaRateCodeFor` is not making a tie-break in either case. |
| 2026-09-08 (IVA rate matrix + catalog fidelity check — TSR-218) | **All 11 `TarifaIVA` codes under test, and the enums checked against the catalog that actually serves them.** `services/ivaRates.test.ts` (46 tests) covers every rate code: the arithmetic (`base × rate`, and nothing for the four legally distinct 0% codes — crédito pleno, transitorio, exento Ley 9635, no sujeto), the derivation `ivaRateCodeFor` that turns a stored percentage into the code filed with Hacienda, and Note 20 re-run at **every** rate rather than only 13%. Verified against `be/data-be/scripts/catalogs_seed_data.json` (countryCode 188) instead of trusting the FE enum: `taxRates` has all 11 codes with percentages matching exactly, `discountTypes` the same 10 natures, `taxes` the same 10 types. Two derivation gaps are now pinned as decisions rather than accidents: **8%** (code 07) is unambiguous but unmapped because it is NC/ND-only and disabled, and **4%** is the one live percentage two codes share (04 reducida vs 06 transitoria) — the map picks the ordinary reduced rate, correct for a product line. A guard asserts every non-zero catalog rate either resolves or is one of those two documented refusals, so a rate the catalog serves can never silently produce a line with no `rate_code` (which sales-api rejects). Also confirmed on the backend: all nine non-99 natures are accepted with no reason at all and emit `CodigoDescuentoOtros=None`; only 99 requires it. 165 FE tests green. |
| 2026-09-08 (every discount nature under test; nature 99 settled — TSR-213, TSR-217) | **The nature matrix is now exhaustive rather than illustrative.** `services/note20.test.ts` covers all ten codes Hacienda defines — 01/03 issuer-assumes, 02 customer-pays-on-gross, and 04-09 plus 99 as ordinary erosion — with a guard asserting the case table still equals `Object.values(DiscountTypeCode)`, so a code added to the enum cannot quietly go untested. Writing it settled **nature 99**: the schema calls the free text `NaturalezaDescuento` / `CodigoDescuentoOtros`, the DTO called it `other_discount_type`, and the POS asked for a "Razón" and sent `reason` — one fact with two spellings that could disagree, and did, rejecting every code-99 line. Per owner decision the FE sends `reason` and the XML spelling is derived backend-side in `DiscountDTO`, mirrored both ways so inbound Hacienda documents keep a populated reason. Verified rather than assumed: both backend validators accept reason-only and still reject an empty one, and `build_xml_descuentos` was run to confirm the reason lands in `CodigoDescuentoOtros`. `DiscountsTab` no longer writes the XML field at all. 119 FE tests green. |
| 2026-09-08 (Note 20 implemented, FE mapped to it — TSR-213) | **The discount nature now drives the tax base and who pays the tax, on both sides of the wire.** The rule has one definition — `line_discount_natures()` in `discount_service`, with `NATURES_ISSUER_ASSUMES={01,03}` and `NATURES_BASE_NOT_ERODED={01,02,03}` — read by both `_compute_lines` and `summary_service`, so a line and the summary it rolls into cannot drift. For 01/03 the base no longer erodes and the VAT moves out of `impuesto_neto` into `ImpuestoAsumidoEmisorFabrica`, which was hardcoded to zero and left issuer-assumed IVA undeclared; for 02 the same gross base is charged to the customer instead. **The FE was mapped to it in the same session, and needed to be:** the POS cart was totalling `Σ price×qty` off the stored gross price and backing tax out as the difference — no tax engine involved — which matched the backend only for as long as the catalog stayed on nature 07. The cart now runs every line through the same discount and tax services the document is built from, so the figure on the screen and the figure on the document come from one place. `services/note20.test.ts` pins all four natures to the backend's longhand numbers rather than to the implementation, and earned its keep immediately: it caught the cart passing gross as `base_amount` — the IVACE *manual base override*, not "the gross" — which had every ordinary-discount line taxed on its pre-discount amount. The gross base Note 20 actually needs travels as `monto_total_original`. 107 FE tests green. Still open: the discount matrix wants a re-run against the deployed implementation, and Hacienda **-37** (TSR-216) keeps every document at PROCESSING regardless. |
| 2026-09-08 (discount matrix run — Note 20 is not implemented; TSR-213…217) | **The matrix found what it was built to find: the discount nature does not affect the tax calculation at all.** At 3% every nature 01-09 returns the identical total `4749.40130` — VAT on the *discounted* base. At 100%, natures 01, 02, 03 and 07 return identical summaries: discount 4333, net 0, tax 0, voucher 0, nothing assumed by the issuer. Note 20 requires 01/03 to leave the base intact (tax = 13% of the original 4333 = 563.29, reported as 0 in the summary but consolidated in `TotalImpuestoAsumidoEmisorFabrica`, which is never populated — so issuer-assumed IVA goes undeclared) and 02 to charge that same tax to the CUSTOMER (voucher ≈563.29, not 0 — the customer is under-charged by the whole VAT). Only nature 07, the ordinary commercial discount, behaves correctly today, which is why it works as the control (**TSR-213**). Getting there surfaced three more. Payments must **equal** the total (`TotalComprobante == Sumatoria(MontoTotalMedioPago)`), and the POS was sending cash *tendered* — so the ₡5 000/₡10 000 quick-amount buttons emitted documents sales-api rejects and only *Exacto* ever worked; a payment now carries `amount` (fiscal), `tendered_amount` and `change_amount` so the drawer reconciles without the tendered figure ever reaching a fiscal document, and the error message no longer claims "10000 does not cover 4749" (**TSR-214**). Nature 99 needs `other_discount_type`, not `reason` (**TSR-217**). And every document comes back PROCESSING on Hacienda message **-37**: the issuer's provincia/cantón/distrito do not match Tributación's registry — an org-registration mismatch that blocks ACCEPTED for everything equally and explains why nothing reaches a terminal status (**TSR-216**). Also shipped: the manual revalidation endpoint, `POST …/sales/{id}/validation/refresh` → 202, verified live (**TSR-215**; FE wiring still to do), and the PDF now generates at emission — confirmed by the runner, which produced and verified PDFs in S3 for both 100% cases under the corrected `hacienda/dev/` prefix. |
| 2026-09-08 (pipeline reordered, deploy-api fixed, discount matrix authored; TSR-206…212) | **The PDF no longer waits for validation.** GENERATE_PDF was published only by document-validator after a terminal ATV status, so the printable copy queued behind a polling loop — which is exactly why 006-008 sat with signed XML and no PDF. sales-api publishes it at emission now, the validator still republishes on a terminal status to refresh the artifact, and `notification_pipeline` returns early while `pdf_url` is empty so the customer's copy is never sent without one. Either order works; the existing `_already_sent` guard means only one email (**TSR-207/208**). **`deploy-api` is green.** It had been failing on `AWS::Lambda::Permission` — the GitHub Actions role has `apigateway:*` but no `lambda:AddPermission`, so the stack could neither create the permission nor roll it back. It only began failing once the document-* lambdas existed and the regenerated template started wiring them up (**TSR-209**). The S3 prefix is fixed too: the mapping is a public `stage_token()` in jbiller_common now, used by both pipelines, instead of one correct copy in the validator and a raw `STAGE` read in sales-api (**TSR-203**). **Correction on 006.** The earlier note said a consecutive was burned; it was not — the rollback correctly released it and 006 was reused. What actually happened is narrower and still real: two *different claves* were built for consecutive 006, and the first was submitted to Hacienda before the rollback, so a filing exists that no local row points at (**TSR-202** stands). Also: the duplicate PDF surface is gone (**TSR-206**), a just-submitted document now reads *En proceso* instead of printing a literal "0" into the action row (**TSR-210**), and the discount-nature matrix is authored as 11 runnable fixtures (**TSR-211**). **Not done:** on-demand revalidation or a 30-minute sweep — sales-api has no revalidate endpoint and the validator no scheduled entry point (**TSR-212**), and the matrix has not been run. |
| 2026-09-08 (first documents emitted end to end; TSR-201, 204, 205) | **The POS now emits. Documents 00100001010000000006, 007 and 008 exist, and the FE and BE totals agree to the céntimo** — `discount 129.99 / net 4 203.01 / IVA 546.3913 / total 4 749.4013` on both sides, which answers the owner's original question once the POS sends the product's taxes *and* discounts. Two more grants were missing from `tsuru-dev-sales-api-role`, and the second was invisible: with no `s3:PutObject` the pipeline died at step 14 **after** submitting to Hacienda; once S3 was granted the sale persisted but nothing downstream ran, because step 16's `sns:Publish` was also absent and `sales_pipeline` deliberately swallows a publish failure so it cannot roll back a filed document. Both are in the SAM template now (**TSR-201**). That in turn exposed that **`document-validator` had never worked at all**: the image copies `src/`'s contents to the task root, but six modules still imported `from src.…`, so every invocation died with `No module named 'src'` before reaching any code — the sibling lambdas use bare imports, which is why only this one was broken (**TSR-204**). With all four fixed the chain runs: signed XMLs are in S3 for 006–008 and the validator is polling the ATV on its ~5-minute retry cadence. The documents sit at `atv 0` (submitted, awaiting Hacienda), so the PDF and notification stages have not fired yet — they trigger on ACCEPTED/PARTIAL. Also noted: the workflow's `deploy-api` job fails on a CloudFormation handler error, which does not block the pipeline but does mean HTTP endpoint changes are not reaching the gateway (**TSR-205**), and the S3 prefix is still `hacienda/DEVELOPMENT/…` rather than `dev` (**TSR-203**). |
| 2026-09-08 (totals reconciled — the gap was a product discount; TSR-198 retracted, 201…203) | **The owner was right and my earlier conclusion was wrong.** I had recorded the ₡146.89 gap as "the catalog is internally inconsistent"; it is not. The product carries a **3% discount**, and once you include it every figure ties out exactly: `4333 − 129.99 = 4203.01`, `× 13% = 546.3913` (the stored `taxes[0].amount`), `4203.01 + 546.3913 = 4749.4013` (the stored `sale_price`). The defect was the same one as the missing taxes — line `discounts` are only populated by the line-detail drawer, so a scan-and-charge sent none. Lines now fall back to the product's configured discounts, resolving the stored **catalog row id** through the discount-types catalog, and dropping rather than guessing anything unresolvable (natures 01/03 divert IVA to the issuer). TSR-198 is retracted and rewritten. **With the totals reconciled the pipeline finally ran end to end — and died one step from the finish.** It built clave `50608092600010244007700100001010000000006193232389`, allocated consecutive **…006**, signed the XML and submitted to the ATV, then failed on `s3:PutObject`: `tsuru-dev-sales-api-role` has **no S3 policy at all** (**TSR-201**). Two things fall out of that. The upload writes to `hacienda/DEVELOPMENT/...` because `_upload_signed_xml` reads the raw `STAGE` instead of `_stage_token()`, while every existing artifact lives under `dev` (**TSR-203**). And far more seriously: submission to Hacienda happens at step 13, *before* the upload and the log row, all inside one transaction — so the rollback left the sale list showing **5 documents with no record of …006**, after the document had already been handed to the tax authority (**TSR-202**). A legal filing must not be able to outlive the local record. |
| 2026-09-08 (FE↔BE totals disagree by the whole tax; TSR-195, 197…200) | **The sumatorias question is answered, and the answer is no.** Driving one real invoice through the POS took four more layers. The branch/terminal **UUIDs** were never sent — only the Hacienda codes — so the Sale row took a generated default and sales-api rejected every attempt with *"Branch <uuid> not found for organization"*, a different uuid each time (**TSR-199**); `useAssignment` compounded it by throwing its own "no assignment" error inside the try block, so a successful empty answer fell through to the offline cache and resurrected an assignment pointing at a branch the org no longer has (**TSR-200**). Confirmar's silent disable turned out not to be float precision — that theory was tested and **disproved** — but a display lie: the product's `sale_price` is `4749.4013` shown as `₡4 749`, so a cashier collecting what the screen says is 40 céntimos short (**TSR-195**). **Then the real finding.** Line `taxes` are only populated by the line-detail drawer, which a scan-and-charge never opens, so documents went out with `taxes: []` and sales-api computed a `TotalComprobante` of **₡4 333 — the net, with no IVA at all**. Had a payment matched, the invoice would have been filed **under-declaring IVA entirely**. Lines now fall back to the product's configured taxes, deriving `rate_code` from the percentage (13% → `08`) and deliberately not guessing 0%, which splits across exento / no-sujeto / crédito-pleno (**TSR-197**). With taxes flowing, the two engines finally showed their disagreement in full: **sales-api computes ₡4 896.29 (net 4 333 × 1.13) while the POS charges ₡4 749.40 — a ₡146.89 gap on a single line.** The product row carries three irreconcilable figures — `price` 4 333, `sale_price` 4 749.4013 (an implied **9.61%**), `taxes[0].amount` 546.3913. The FE trusts the stored gross; the BE recomputes it from net × rate. **The customer is charged one amount at the till and the legal document states another**, and no amount of frontend work reconciles a catalog whose gross ≠ net × rate — one side has to become authoritative and the data has to be fixed (**TSR-198**). **No document was emitted**, and deliberately so: emitting now would file an invoice whose total does not match what the POS charged. The per-discount-type matrix is blocked behind the same decision. |
| 2026-09-08 (account menu, profile fixes, checkout walls; TSR-190…196) | **Account actions left the sidebar.** *Salir* and *Cambiar organización* were unreachable whenever the sidebar was collapsed, so they are a navbar avatar dropdown now alongside *Perfil*; the sidebar keeps the identity row as a profile shortcut that marks active like any nav item, showing `username` rather than a four-part name that only ever rendered ellipsed (**TSR-190**). The owner spotted that Profile said *Cliente* where the sidebar said *Administrador* — `ProfilePage` was reading `user.role`, the platform-level Cognito role that is `customer` for everyone after first sync, instead of the org-scoped role both surfaces should share (**TSR-191**). Profile fields are paired two-up with role on its own row, the security card is two side-by-side actions with justified copy, and both cards share an even split (**TSR-192**). The collapse handle now tucks by the same 20px in both states instead of showing all 28px when collapsed (**TSR-193**). **Billing the one CABYS-valid product went two walls further and then stopped.** `unit_measure` is only populated by the line-detail drawer, which a scan-and-charge never opens, so Hacienda rejected the most ordinary sale there is with *"unit_measure is required"* — it defaults to `Unid` now (**TSR-196**). "Exacto" turned out to be a no-op whenever a cash payment already existed, which is why a ₡4 749 cart kept offering ₡9 261.4013 from the cart before it (**TSR-194**). **The document still did not emit.** With one valid line, a receiver and the drawer itself reporting *Pagado ₡4 749 · Faltan ₡0*, Confirmar renders disabled with no message and no request leaves the browser. A float-precision theory for `isPaid` was tested and **disproved** — the button stayed disabled with céntimo comparison in place, and that change was kept on its own merits rather than as the fix it turned out not to be (**TSR-195**). So the FE↔BE sumatorias comparison the owner asked for is still open: the FE side is captured (subtotal ₡4 333 + IVA ₡416 = ₡4 749) with no accepted document to diff against, and the per-discount-type matrix has not been started. |
| 2026-09-08 (full Hacienda chain deployed; org isolation; TSR-176/177, 183…189) | **Every stage of the pipeline has a consumer for the first time.** `deploys/deploy-sam-stacks.sh` put all 7 stacks green: `document-pdf-generator` now exists and consumes `hacienda-pdf-queue.fifo`, and `document-notification` consumes `hacienda-notification-queue.fifo` (**TSR-176/177**). The notification stack failed its first deploy on `AWS::EarlyValidation::ResourceExistenceCheck` — it declares its own log group and `/aws/lambda/tsuru-dev-document-notification-lambda` was still orphaned from a deleted stack; it held 0 bytes across 0 streams, so it was removed and the deploy re-run. **Organizations no longer bleed into each other.** The mechanism was that `selectedOrgId` lived in `sessionStorage` — tab-scoped — so a second tab silently fell back to `orgs[0]` and the user really did have two tabs on two tenants (**TSR-183**). On top of that, `pos-document-store` persisted one flat tab list with no organization on it, so switching carried open drafts — cart, client and receiver included — straight across; drafts are now parked per organization and swapped in a layout effect (**TSR-184**). Switching is a sidebar entry now instead of a logout/login round trip (**TSR-185**), and the puesto list loads on mount like every other select (**TSR-186**). **Emitting an invoice went two walls further.** With a receiver finally attached, sales-api rejected *"Identification.number must be all digits"* — clients are stored as `1-1664-0506` and that display string was going onto the document; `buildReceiver` now normalises it (**TSR-187**). The next rejection, *"cabys must be exactly 13 digits"*, is **data, not code**: 9 of the first 10 products for this org have `cabys: null`, so they cannot legally be invoiced, and the POS only discovers it at submit rather than at add-to-cart (**TSR-188**). Also caught: the *Exacto* cash button fills `9261.401300000001` on a ₡9 261 total, so `payments_total` can drift from `voucher_total` (**TSR-189**). **No document has been emitted yet**, so the FE↔BE totals comparison the owner asked for is still open — the FE side is captured (subtotal ₡8 845 + IVA ₡416 = ₡9 261 on an IVA-included catalog) but there is no accepted document to compare against until a product with a valid CABYS is billed. |
| 2026-09-08 (POS emission path unblocked, four layers deep; TSR-174…182) | **Trying to emit one invoice from the POS uncovered four separate blockers stacked on top of each other, each hidden behind the one in front of it.** (1) `cd-backend` had been dead since the previous day's commit — a PEP 604 `str | None` annotation evaluated at import on the **python3.9** runtime — so branches, terminals, sessions and orders all 502'd (**TSR-174**, fixed and deployed). (2) With the API back, the puesto dropdown was *still* empty: `components/ui/Select` accepts `onFocus` in its prop type but never forwards it, and `SessionSetupScreen` loads its branches on focus — so the handler had never once run (**TSR-178**). (3) With a shift finally started and a cart built, `POST /sales` answered `422 int_parsing` on `details[].product_id`: the DTO typed it `int` while product ids are UUIDs and `SaleLine.product_id` is already `String(255)`; the read path had the mirror bug and returned `None` for any UUID (**TSR-179**, fixed and deployed). (4) That cleared, the API then said *"Receiver is required for document type '01'"* while the UI plainly showed a selected receptor — `buildReceiver` treats a truthy `inbound` as authoritative, and an untouched checkout carries `{}`, so the selected client was never consulted (**TSR-180**). That last one is the strongest available explanation for why documents #001–#004 were rejected by Hacienda. **The PDF generator now builds in CI.** It had been commented out of the build matrix since Wave 4 as "wkhtmltopdf install fails", blamed on a 404 — but the asset still answers 200. The real faults were that AL2023 has no `wkhtmltopdf` package (so the `||` chain's first branch always failed), that the fallback called `wget` before installing it (the reported 127, which also swallowed the true error), and that this image's dnf5 refuses a local RPM by path *or* URL. It installs with `rpm -Uvh --nodeps` now, gated on a real `wkhtmltopdf --version`, with DejaVu/Liberation fonts added so pages do not render as empty boxes. `tsuru-document-pdf-generator-ecr:latest` exists (316 MB) and the whole sales-be workflow is green (**TSR-176**). Creating the two missing SAM stacks (pdf-generator, notification) is the one step still outstanding — `pipeline-update.sh` only updates functions that already exist, and `deploys/deploy-sam-stacks.sh` was blocked by the local permission classifier. **No document has been emitted yet**; the receiver fix is FE-only and needs one more checkout run. Also noted: the same session resolved to two different default organizations across a reload, carrying open cart tabs between them (**TSR-182**), and the documents grid now caps at two cards per row on desktop (**TSR-181**). |
| 2026-09-08 (POS end-to-end attempt blocked; TSR-174…177) | **Trying to emit a document from the POS surfaced a bigger outage than the one being audited: `cd-backend` has been dead since yesterday's commit.** The new-document editor opens on the shift-setup screen, the puesto dropdown was empty, and CloudWatch showed why — `cd-backend-dev-lambda` never finishes `INIT`. `app/enums/order_status.py:59` annotates `current: str | None`; PEP 604 unions are runtime-invalid before Python 3.10 and this service runs the **python3.9** Lambda runtime, so the annotation is evaluated at `def` time and raises `TypeError: unsupported operand type(s) for |` **at import**. Every orders/products/branches/terminals/sessions/assignments request 502s. Introduced by `cded617` (the TSR-150…164 verticals work, 2026-09-07), which added the `QUOTE` status and that signature. One line fixes it — `from __future__ import annotations` — and an AST sweep of `store-be/app` for runtime-evaluated PEP 604 annotations without the future import found **exactly one** module, so this is the whole of it (**TSR-174**). Until it deploys, no document can be created from the POS at all: the cashier cannot start a shift, so the cart is unreachable and the Hacienda pipeline is never entered (**TSR-175**). Answering the question the audit raised: **PDF generation is not internal to sales-api.** It is a separate container Lambda that renders HTML→PDF with wkhtmltopdf and then publishes `SEND_NOTIFICATION` — which is also why the notification Lambda alone cannot close the loop. Its ECR repository does not exist and no image was ever pushed; with no CodePipeline in the account the image has to be built locally as `linux/amd64`, and Docker is not running (**TSR-176**). The notification Lambda, by contrast, already has a `latest` image in ECR from 2026-08-21 and its template needs no packaging, so it is a one-command deploy (**TSR-177**). FE: the documents grid now caps at **two cards per row** on desktop (`.grid-docs`) instead of letting `auto-fill` pack three — a 20-digit consecutivo plus a five-button action row does not fit a third of a 1280px viewport. |
| 2026-09-08 (POS invoicing QA audit + document detail page; TSR-165…173) | **The Hacienda pipeline works up to validation and stops there — and the documents list was blank for a reason nobody had looked at.** Audited manual and from-order invoice creation against PACIFIC-PROD (947999370977, `tsuru/dev`) with the POS running locally on :9000. The list rendered five cards with `?` doc type, "Invalid Date" and ₡0: **sales-api serializes camelCase** (`response_utils.py` dumps with `by_alias=True`) while every POS type for that gateway is snake_case, so each field read back `undefined` — which also produced the duplicate-React-key warning, since `sale_id` was undefined on every row. Fixed once at the client boundary (`createClient(SALES_API_BASE, { snakeCaseResponses: true })`) rather than in ~20 hooks; requests were already fine because the BE models set `populate_by_name=True` (**TSR-165**). With the list readable, the artifact trail is unambiguous: for `00100001010000000005` the signed XML (9.4 KiB), the `Respuesta Hacienda` XML (5.8 KiB) and a 16.4 KiB PDF all exist in `tsuru-uploads-dev`, and ATV returned `validation_status=1`. **The reason none of it is reachable from the POS is a dead hostname**: `/tsuru/dev/hacienda/s3/cloudfront_domain` was `https://tsuru-uploads.jcampos.dev`, which is NXDOMAIN — the live CDN is `https://uploads.tsuru.jcampos.dev`, where the identical key fetches `200 application/pdf, 16 813 bytes`. TSR-106/134 moved that domain and updated cd-backend's SSM but missed the Hacienda namespace. Fixed in both places — the live parameter (v2) and the hardcoded default in `hacienda-params.yml` that would have reverted it on the next deploy (**TSR-169**). **That only heals future emissions**: `get_xml_files` returns the stored `sale.pdf_url` column verbatim rather than synthesizing it, so the dead host is baked into all 5 existing rows and their attachments and needs a backfill (**TSR-169b**). But **`document-pdf-generator` has no Lambda and no log group in this account at all**, and `document-notification`'s function is gone (its log group survives) — so the PDF that exists was produced out of band, not by deployed infra. Their SNS→SQS wiring is intact but neither queue has a consumer, and every one of the five documents is `notified=false`, `send_attempts=0` (**TSR-166**). `historical-documents.fifo` has zero subscriptions, so the periodic history sweep `sales_pipeline.py` names as its publish-failure fallback cannot run either (**TSR-167**). **The pipeline is also unauditable**: `sales-api`, `hacienda-taxes` and the document lambdas emit only `START`/`END`/`REPORT` despite logging at INFO on every stage, and the document log groups are on 7-day retention with 0 stored bytes (**TSR-168**). also carries `errors: null` with no stored ATV response — the operator cannot learn why Hacienda rejected a legal invoice (**TSR-170**). On the from-order path, `isOrderInvoiced()` gates "Facturar pedido" on `order.invoice.sale_id`, but **nothing ever writes it** — the flow rebuilds the cart client-side and the checkout POSTs `/sales` with the order number only as free text in `notes`, so the guard never fires and a delivered order can be billed twice (**TSR-171**). Shipped alongside the audit: a **document detail page** at `/dashboard/documents/:saleId` modeled on `OrderDetailPage`, whose 5-stage timeline (signed → submitted → validated → PDF → notified) is derived from the field the backend writes at each stage — it renders the stall as "PDF generado / En curso" and a rejection as a red "Con error" step, which is how the pipeline gap was made visible rather than argued (**TSR-172**). The route is mounted *after* `/documents/new/:tabId` because wouter's `Switch` takes the first match. A full-height PDF viewer (`DocumentPdfDialog`, same modal shell as `CrossdockingDetailsDialog`) shows the voucher for accepted **and** rejected documents with the ATV status as a header badge — a rejection is precisely when someone needs to read what was sent. The list card's "Ver PDF"/"Descargar" buttons were permanently disabled because they gated on `doc.pdf_url`, which the sale row never carries (the urls come from `/xml/files`); that gate is gone. A sidebar-handle change was made and then **reverted at the owner's request** — the component, its z-index and its locale keys are back to their original state. `tsc --noEmit`, `check:styles` and 103/103 tests pass. **Not deployed** — POS working copy only; no backend change was made. |
| 2026-09-07 (POS verticals by business type; TSR-127, TSR-150…164) | **The POS became polymorphic: an organization declares what kind of business it is and gets the matching feature set.** `organizations.business_type` (9 values) plus two *independent* flags — `is_retail_supplier` and `is_pyme` — which are deliberately **not** type values, because a minisuper can be a registered PYME *and* supply a chain and a single-value column cannot express that. The backend is the writer: `syncBusinessTypeModules` resolves the union of type + flags into `organization_modules`, disabling revoked modules rather than deleting them. Applied to `tsuru/dev`: 18 modules seeded, `quotes` reached all 8 orgs, **0 orgs gained a vertical module** — nothing switches on until someone chooses, so no existing org's UI moved. Two capabilities ship **ungated** on purpose: proformas (any business quotes before it sells) and product-code lookup (scanning is how a POS is used) — the latter rides `commercial/read/products` rather than inventing a gate. `useBusinessType` **fails closed**, unlike `usePermissions().hasModule()` which fails open and auto-grants to owners: right for a permission, wrong for a vertical, since a minisuper owner must not see Mesas. **Three bugs were caught by writing tests and checks rather than by reading code**: `ProductTaxDTO` takes `tax_type_id` with a nested `TaxRateDTO`, not the `tax_code` the first pass guessed; `product.cabys` is a relationship, so a `cabys_code` attribute would have silently written `None` on every imported line; and `categories.id` is a String PK, so a UUID `category_id` FK would have failed on apply. **The pedido manual's Documento card is now absent rather than collapsed** — its fields moved onto the Pedido card and its *Notas* was deleted because `useCartFlow` already merged it into *Comentario*; delivery point became registered-point / receiver-address / structured-cascade with no free-text-only path. The payment "Más" menu turned out to be a **CSS-layer bug**: `.dropdown-menu` sat outside `@layer`, beating every utility at equal specificity, so the portaled menu rendered `absolute` at `z-index:30` behind the drawer. On the owner's instruction the Excel importer now emits structured data too — the discount as **07 Descuento Comercial** (the sheet carries an amount but no type, and unlike 99 it needs no reason; critically, only 01/03 divert IVA to `ImpuestoAsumidoEmisorFabrica`) and taxes copied from the product — after querying the live `discount_types` catalog revealed **10 codes, not the 4** both enums carried. Imported *amounts* are untouched: on a cross-docking order the customer's figures are what you reconcile against. Ticket printing (TSR-127) was built **server-side** after a browser-print version was written and rejected, because two maquetadores would drift and a reprint would stop matching the original. 149 tests (59 store-be, 90 POS). Migrations `aa7b8c9d0e1f`, `ab8c9d0e1f2a`, `ac9d0e1f2a3b` and management-be `0019` applied to dev; `db:reseed-rbac` run with 0 custom roles lost. **Not deployed** — code and dev DB only. |
| 2026-08-31 (POS branch-path contract fix; TSR-149) | **The terminals 422 was a frontend contract bug, not an API bug.** store-be addresses branches by their Hacienda integer `code` (`/branches/{branch_code}/...`), but the POS session-setup screen posted the branch **UUID** into that slot, so `branch_code` failed `int_parsing`. Two sibling calls on the stations page had the same defect (branch update, branch status) — and the status toggle was additionally PATCHing `{status}` at the general branch route, whose `BranchUpdateRequestDTO` has no `status` field; it now uses `/branches/{code}/status`. The trap was `CreateTerminalRequest.branch_id: number` (a branch *code*) shadowing `Branch.branch_id: string` (a UUID); since the branch is already in the path and store-be ignores the extra key, the field and the `branchId` form props were deleted rather than corrected. Four files in `tsuru-pos-system`, `tsc --noEmit` clean. **Not deployed** — committed in the POS repo only. |
| 2026-08-21 (register/terminals/branch-types bug sweep; TSR-137…141) | **Two independent causes behind "500 on register", both found in CloudWatch.** (1) The Cognito `CustomMessage` trigger Lambda was dead — configured x86_64 but pinned to an arm64 image, failing `Runtime.InvalidEntrypoint` on every invoke since 2026-06-20; repointed to the amd64 `:latest` image already sitting in ECR and smoke-invoked green (**TSR-137**, live). (2) The Cognito→DB sync re-linked only by email, so a colliding `preferred_username` raised `users_username_unique` and surfaced as a 500 on the profile fetch that runs right after verification; the sync now picks the next free username variant and unique violations map to 409 (**TSR-138**, code only). **`branch-types` was never implemented anywhere** — the gateway's 403 "Missing Authentication Token" was an unknown-route answer, not a broken one — so the per-org catalog was built end to end in store-be: table, migration (which also drops the legacy `check_branch_type` constraint and seeds every org), immutable slug `code`, in-use-guarded delete, 15 tests, regenerated API-Gateway template (**TSR-139**). Fixed `create_terminal` passing 3 args to a 4-arg service (**TSR-140**). On the POS register page, the shared `Stepper` gained `stackedRail` so the 3-step rail keeps its two-row grid beside the brand panel instead of wrapping with a dangling connector; story copy justified; the gender "back to select" control made single-click reliable; native `select` option colors pinned to the app palette (**TSR-141**). Regenerating store-be's swagger also revealed `POST /api/organizations/{id}/orders` had never been wired into API Gateway — it is included now. The store-be migration was applied and verified the same day (head `z6b7c8d9e0f1`; constraint dropped, 6 rows seeded across 3 orgs, no orphaned branches). Backend/FE **deploys** remain pending — all four repos are committed but unpushed. |

| 2026-09-10 (POS session + notifications) | **New: TSR-237..242; closes TSR-128; amends TSR-150.** Five things the POS was getting wrong, plus the notification centre it never had. (1) **The shift-setup gate is gone** — branch and terminal were a full-screen precondition for opening the POS; they are two fields of the document being issued, so they now live on it, in a checkout card, auto-answered (assignment → current → first usable) and overridable per document. `SessionSetupScreen` deleted. (2) **A persisted, per-user notification centre** with real-time push over AppSync Events and **zero polling** — two tables (event vs. per-recipient read state), a new `user-notifications` Lambda, publish-where-you-persist in the shared service, a subscribe authorizer that pins each channel to its owner's Cognito `sub`, and a frontend that hydrates once and then only listens. (3) **Hacienda's verdict now reaches the person who issued the document**, with the rejection reasons in the body and a click straight to the document detail page where they are rendered — fired from `document-validator` when the status lands, not from the notification lambda that waits for a PDF. (4) **Business type stops gating anything**: every org gets every vertical module (so roles stop being limited by it either), the type keeps only the emphasis job, and hiding a surface became a reversible per-org preference. (5) **Two report-driven fixes** — the chain card's departamento/punto de entrega stayed empty when billing a pedido (order carries codes, selects are keyed by UUID: the ids are now resolved from the loaded lists and written back), and typing in an open select did nothing on lists shorter than eight options (it filters everywhere now, revealing the search box as you type). Verified: POS `tsc` clean, 256/256 tests, build green, style policy green; `management-be` `tsc` clean; new Python modules import-checked and the new Lambda's OpenAPI exports 5 paths. **Nothing is deployed** — see §7 steps 14 and 15. |
| 2026-08-24 (no-USD decision) | **Colones only, both languages — decided; no code change needed.** Confirmed the EN landing never showed USD (`formatCRC` takes no language input, `config.currency: CRC`, EN labels are suffixes like "/ month"), so there was no burned rate on the landing at all. Evaluated wiring `be/data-be/app/consumer-exchange-rate` (Hacienda-sourced, DB-cached) into the page and **decided against it**: Tsuru is only usable by a Hacienda filer so there is no foreign-currency buyer segment, and card networks convert at charge time at the issuer's rate + FX fee — any USD figure printed would be systematically low versus the real statement, i.e. a number we can't stand behind. Verified the endpoint is Cognito-protected (`data-api` → 401, `public-api` → 403), so no public-API exposure is now needed. The service stays where it is load-bearing: the **POS** (`ExchangeRateContext`), where Hacienda requires the BCCR rate on USD invoices. `fe/pos-landing`'s `usdRateCRC: 600` reframed — the CRC\|USD toggle itself should be deleted rather than fed a live rate; **parked**, that site isn't deployed. All `tsuru_pricing_market_research.md` items are now closed. |
| 2026-08-24 (IVA decided) | **Prices are FINAL — IVA included** (`tsuru-landing` `27f4ec1`). Stated beside the amounts under the billing toggle, not buried: for a brand promising "sin cargos ocultos", a 13% surfacing at checkout would be the hidden charge itself. Also answered as a FAQ entry ("¿Los precios incluyen IVA?", placed third). Copy in `planLabels.taxNote`, editable from `/admin/plans`. Recorded in both planning docs; the `plans` table comment now states amounts are **IVA-inclusive gross** so nobody adds tax on top at checkout. Improves the competitive read: **GTI quotes "+ IVA"**, so its real cost is 13% above list (persona física ₡7.650 → ₡8.645; empresa ₡12.750 → ₡14.408). **TSR-084 / TSR-147 now have nothing blocking `draftPricing` being turned off.** |

Annual stays exactly 9× monthly so "pagás 9 meses y te regalamos 3" is arithmetically true; toggle badge, savings note and Cosecha subline all updated. **Colones-only, CR formatting:** `Intl` `es-CR` groups thousands with a NO-BREAK SPACE (₡200 000, reads as a typo in CR) — new `src/lib/currency.ts` groups explicitly with a period (**₡200.000**), whole colones, no decimals, used by the plans page and home teaser. | 2026-08-24 (market research + origin fix) | **New: TSR-147/148.** Wrote `docs/roadmap/tsuru_pricing_market_research.md` — CR market check at ₡452/USD. Tsuru competes in **POS + e-invoicing ($20–80/mo)**, not pure invoicing ($5–20): **Cosecha ($44.25/mo, $36.87 annual) is defensible**; **Cooperativa ($99.56/mo) is above the $80 market ceiling** → recommend ₡35.000/₡350.000. Biggest strategic finding: **Hacienda's TicoFactura is free AND uncapped**, so a hard document cap pushes merchants to a government product — which independently confirms the owner's **soft-cap** decision (warn at 80%, never block emission) plus non-expiring **document packs** (₡2.500/25, ₡8.000/100), now spec'd in TSR-145 §5.6 with a `document_packs` table and FIFO consumption. Also flagged: the page never states **IVA** treatment (every competitor does) and `fe/pos-landing`'s `usdRateCRC: 600` is ~33% stale. **Origin corrected** (`tsuru-landing` `6fe138a`): Tsuru was born in **Puntarenas** — Guápiles is where the inspiring org, **Feria del Trueque Verde Manantial**, is; fair references kept, birthplace fixed across branding/about/legacy translation keys. | 2026-09-16 (store-be was returning 500 on every route; TSR-275) | **The orders API had no error DTO because it had no application — the Lambda was failing at import, and had been since the error-contract commit landed.** The report was "orders gives 500 and no error response DTO", which reads like a handler bug; the handler was never reached. CloudWatch showed `Runtime.ImportModuleError: cannot import name 'UTC' from 'datetime' (/var/lang/lib/python3.9/datetime.py)` on every invocation — 88 in six hours — so API Gateway was returning a bare Lambda failure with no body for FastAPI to shape. The cause is a version mismatch the other services hide: TSR-272 gave all four backends the same `error_contract.py`, but store-be's `Dockerfile` alone still pins `public.ecr.aws/lambda/python:3.9`, where `datetime.UTC` (3.11+) and a `X | None` annotation in a Pydantic model body (3.10+, evaluated at class creation, unlike the signature unions that stay lazy strings) are both fatal. sales-be and data-be build on 3.12/3.14 and took the identical code without complaint — **the shared contract was only ever tested where it worked**. An AST sweep of `app/` confirmed those two lines were the only 3.10+ constructs in evaluated positions, including a check that no route handler signature carries a pipe union, since FastAPI resolves those at decoration time. With `timezone.utc` and `Optional[List[Dict[str, Any]]]`, the handler boots and `GET /orders` returns 200 with real data from the dev DB. The second half of the report was a genuine and separate gap: `ErrorResponseDTO` existed and was installed, but no route declared it, so swagger documented only `200`/`422` and the FE had no typed error schema even on a healthy deploy — now declared once on the app and present on all 115 operations, with `swagger/backend.json` regenerated (68 paths unchanged, `api-gateway/template.yml` byte-identical, so no gateway change is implied). 440 tests pass. **Not deployed** — store-be's image rebuild belongs to the TSR-273 owner deploy layer, and the 500s continue until it ships. Left open deliberately: store-be is the last backend on Python 3.9, and until its base image is bumped, any future shared-library idiom will break here and only here, at deploy time rather than in CI. |
